Grammar & commentary

Uttama Carita · उत्तमचरितम् · Chapter 11 of 13

देव्याः स्तुतिःThe Hymn to Nārāyaṇī

devyāḥ stutiḥ

55 mantras · 11.1–11.55

❁

Overview

Śumbha is dead, and the gods, Agni at their head and Indra among them, their faces shining like opened lotuses, praise the Goddess as Kātyāyanī (11.2). They ask her grace as the refuge of the afflicted, the mother of the world and its sovereign (11.3); they name her the earth that supports the world and the waters that nourish it (11.4), Viṣṇu’s power and the supreme Māyā, by whom the world is bewildered and who, when gracious, is the cause of liberation (11.5); and they confess that no praise can reach one whose forms are all knowledge and all women (11.6–7). Then the refrain begins, नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते: she is understanding in every heart, time and its changes, the blessing of all that is blessed, the power of creation, continuance and destruction, the rescuer of those who come for refuge (11.8–12); the nine powers who fought in chapters 8–9, Brahmāṇī to Cāmuṇḍā, are each saluted as Nārāyaṇī (11.13–21); and so are Lakṣmī, Modesty, Faith and the rest, Great Night and, in the Gita Press reading, Great Nescience (11.22–23). Under the name Durgā the refrain becomes a prayer for protection from fear (11.24), and her gentle face, her trident, her bell and her sword are asked to guard the gods (11.25–28). In the longer metres again they praise her power over illness and desire and the refuge she gives (11.29), ask who but she could have become many to destroy the asuras (11.30), and who but she dwells in all knowledge and whirls the world in the dark pit of ‘mine’ (11.31); she stands guard wherever danger is (11.32–33), and they ask her to keep them from fear and calamity and to be the giver of boons (11.34–35). The Goddess offers a boon that will benefit the worlds (11.36–37); the gods ask that every affliction of the three worlds be quelled and their enemies destroyed in just this way (11.38–39). Her answer is a prophecy (11.40–55): in the twenty-eighth age of Vaivasvata Manu a second Śumbha and Niśumbha will be born, and she, born of Yaśodā in the house of Nanda and dwelling on the Vindhya, will destroy them (11.41–42); descending in a terrible form she will devour the Dānavas of Vipracitti’s line and be called Raktadantikā, the Red-toothed (11.43–45); in a hundred-year drought, born of no womb, she will look on the sages with a hundred eyes as Śatākṣī, feed the world with plants from her own body as Śākambharī, and slay the asura Durgama, so that her name will be Durgā (11.46–50); on the Himālaya she will devour the rākṣasas as Bhīmā (11.50–52); against Aruṇa she will take a form of countless bees as Bhrāmarī (11.52–54); and whenever the Dānavas oppress the world, she will descend and destroy the enemies (11.54–55).

  • Nārāyaṇī: the powers of all the gods as one Goddess
  • Refuge given, and refuge passed on
  • The power that binds is the power that frees
  • Gentle face and fierce weapons, both for protection
  • The boon that becomes a promise of descent
  • Names given by those she saves
About this chapter
Forms of the Devī
Kātyāyanī, the name under which the gods begin and return to their praise (11.2, 11.25); Viśveśvarī, the sovereign of the universe (11.3, 11.33); Vaiṣṇavī śakti and the supreme Māyā (11.5); Ambā and Ambikā, the Mother (11.6, 11.30); Nārāyaṇī, the name of the refrain (11.8–23), joined in one verse with Śiva’s consort as Śivā, Tryambakā and Gaurī (11.10); the nine forms who fought in chapters 8–9 — Brahmāṇī, Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Nārasiṃhī, Aindrī, Śivadūtī and Cāmuṇḍā (11.13–21); the powers Lakṣmī, Lajjā, Mahāvidyā, Śraddhā, Puṣṭi, Svadhā, Dhruvā, Mahārātri and Mahā-avidyā (11.22), Medhā, Sarasvatī, Bhūti, Bābhravī, Tāmasī and Niyati (11.23); Durgā, Bhadrakālī and Caṇḍikā in the prayers for protection (11.24, 11.26, 11.28); Varadā, the giver of boons, the gods’ word and her own (11.35, 11.37). In the prophecy: the daughter of Yaśodā who dwells on the Vindhya (11.42), Raktadantikā (11.45), Śatākṣī (11.47), Śākambharī (11.49), Durgā (11.50), Bhīmā (11.52) and Bhrāmarī (11.54).
Significance of the events
This is the fourth of the Devī Māhātmya’s great hymns, after Brahmā’s (1.73–87) and the gods’ hymns of chapters 4 and 5, and from its refrain it is known as the Nārāyaṇī Stuti; it is often recited by itself, and its verse सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये (11.10) is perhaps the most widely recited line of the whole text. The hymn gathers what the battles showed: the Mothers who fought in chapters 8–9 are saluted one by one as forms of the one Nārāyaṇī (11.13–21), and the gods’ question ‘who else?’ (11.30) echoes the Goddess’s own ‘who else is there besides me?’ (10.5). The chapter also turns the story from the past to the future. The gods ask not for one more rescue but that their enemies be destroyed ‘in just this way’ (11.39), and the Goddess answers with a promise that runs into later ages — into the time of Kṛṣṇa (11.41–42) and through five descents — and ends as a rule: whenever oppression arises, she will descend (11.54–55). Along the way the prophecy gives the name Durgā its story (11.49–50). Her speech continues into chapter 12.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
The hymn is built of vocatives, and the chapter is the best place in the text to learn them: ī-stems shorten the final vowel (नारायणि, गौरि, लक्ष्मि, ऐन्द्रि), ā-stems end in -e (शिवे, लज्जे, श्रद्धे, चामुण्डे), i-stems take guṇa (शक्ते, नियते), and two stem-form vocatives, पुष्टि and भूति (11.22–23), are kept as printed. Most vocatives are compounds — upapada tatpuruṣas of the agent in -हरा, -दा, -धरा, -धारिणी, -प्रदायिनी and bahuvrīhis of weapon and mount — and an instrumental of form stands among them (माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण, 11.14; नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण, 11.18; शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण, 11.20). The refrain is नमः अस्तु ते, the third-person imperative of √अस् with the dative. Prayers use the imperative (प्रसीद, पाहि, त्राहि, परिपालय, नय) and the third-person imperative of blessing (पातु, भवतु), with the danger in the ablative (भयेभ्यः, 11.24; भीतेः, 11.26; पापेभ्यः, 11.27) and the purpose in the dative (शुभाय, 11.28). Rhetorical questions with का carry the confessions of 11.6–7, 11.30 and 11.31. The gods’ request uses the gerundive with an instrumental agent (त्वया कार्यम्, 11.39). The prophecy is in the future tense throughout (उत्पत्स्येते, नाशयिष्यामि, हनिष्यामि, सम्भविष्यामि, भरिष्यामि, स्तोष्यन्ति, करिष्यामि), with Purāṇic forms (वधिष्यामि, 11.49, 11.53; the parasmaipada निरीक्षिष्यामि, 11.47; the ordinal अष्टाविंशतिमे, 11.41), names given with इति and a double accusative (मां … रक्तदन्तिकाम्, 11.45), and correlative clauses that run across verses (यदा … तदा, 11.50–51 and 11.52–53; the distributive यदा यदा … तदा तदा, 11.54–55).
The teaching, as distinct from the narrative
Literally, the gods thank the Goddess who has saved them, she offers a boon, and she foretells the forms in which she will come again. As teaching, the Śākta tradition reads the hymn as a summary of the whole text: every power — of each god (the Mothers, 11.13–21), of knowledge and of women (11.6), of time (11.9), of creation, continuance and destruction (11.11) — is one Goddess, and the refrain gives Viṣṇu’s name, Nārāyaṇī, to the consort of Śiva (11.10). The same power binds and frees: she is the Māyā that bewilders and, when gracious, the cause of liberation (11.5), the lamp of discernment and the darkness of ‘mine’ (11.31). The hymn begins and ends with grace to those who come for refuge (11.3, 11.35), and says that those who take refuge in her become a refuge for others (11.29, 11.33). The tradition compares the closing promise (11.54–55) with the Bhagavadgītā’s promise of descent (4.7–8); the chapter itself names no such source. These readings are the tradition’s, and the commentary presents them as such; the chapter’s own words are a hymn and a prophecy.
Carita
Uttama Carita (the third episode, chapters 5–13), in which this chapter turns from the battles to the gods’ thanksgiving. In the recitation tradition its presiding form is Mahāsarasvatī; this is the Gita Press viniyoga (printed before chapter 5), not a statement of the Purāṇa text. In the Gita Press pāṭha chapter 11 has no viniyoga of its own, only a dhyāna, of Bhuvaneśī: ‘I worship Bhuvaneśī, bright as the rising sun, crowned with the moon, high-breasted, three-eyed, smiling, her hands showing the boon, the goad, the noose and the gesture that dispels fear’ (बालरविद्युतिमिन्दुकिरीटां … वरदाङ्कुशपाशाभीतिकरां प्रभजे भुवनेशीम्). The dhyāna is liturgy; the chapter itself never names Bhuvaneśī.
Recited before the chapter

These lines belong to the recitation (the Gita Press pāṭha), not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras.

Dhyāna

ॐ बालरविद्युतिमिन्दुकिरीटांतुङ्गकुचां नयनत्रययुक्ताम्।स्मेरमुखीं वरदाङ्कुशपाशाभीतिकरां प्रभजे भुवनेशीम्॥

ॐ bālaravidyutimindukirīṭāṃtuṅgakucāṃ nayanatrayayuktām |smeramukhīṃ varadāṅkuśapāśābhītikarāṃ prabhaje bhuvaneśīm ||

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after i, before a vowel, is sounded as r.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas, still telling the story to King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi, goes on from the death of Śumbha at the end of chapter 10. What follows is the gods’ hymn of thanksgiving (11.2–35) and the Goddess’s boon, with her promise of future descents (11.37–55).

Traditional interpretation

In the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter is preceded by a dhyāna of Bhuvaneśī (बालरविद्युतिमिन्दुकिरीटां …): the Goddess bright as the morning sun, crowned with the moon, three-eyed, holding goad and noose and showing the gestures of boon and fearlessness. The ॐ that opens this mantra in recitation belongs to the same pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa. The chapter stands in the third carita, which the pāṭha assigns to Mahāsarasvatī.

Verse 11.2

Śloka · upajāti (triṣṭubh)
‹›

देव्या हते तत्र महासुरेन्द्रेसेन्द्राः सुरा वह्निपुरोगमास्ताम्।कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुरिष्टलाभाद्विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः॥⁠२⁠॥

devyā hate tatra mahāsurendresendrāḥ surā vahnipurogamāstām |kātyāyanīṃ tuṣṭuvuriṣṭalābhādvikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देव्या · हते · तत्र · महासुरेन्द्रेसेन्द्राः · सुराः · वह्निपुरोगमाः · ताम्कात्यायनीम् · तुष्टुवुः · इष्टलाभात्विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः

devyā · hate · tatra · mahāsurendresendrāḥ · surāḥ · vahnipurogamāḥ · tāmkātyāyanīm · tuṣṭuvuḥ · iṣṭalābhātvikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ

Word by word

  1. देव्याdevyāby the Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); agent of the participle हते
  2. हतेhateslainpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. loc. sg.; with महासुरेन्द्रे forms a locative absolute
  3. तत्रtatrathere, then (on that battlefield)indeclinable (adverb of place, from तद् + tral)
  4. महासुरेन्द्रेmahāsurendrethe great lord of the asuras (Śumbha)m. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the locative absolute
  5. सेन्द्राःsendrāḥtogether with Indram. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुराः
  6. सुराःsurāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of सुर; subject of तुष्टुवुः
  7. वह्निपुरोगमाःvahnipurogamāḥled by Vahni (Agni)m. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुराः
  8. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; with कात्यायनीम्
  9. कात्यायनीम्kātyāyanīmKātyāyanī, the Goddessf. acc. sg. of कात्यायनी (ī-stem); object of तुष्टुवुः
  10. तुष्टुवुःtuṣṭuvuḥpraised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  11. इष्टलाभात्iṣṭalābhātbecause they had gained what they wishedm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of cause
  12. विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाःvikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥtheir radiant lotus-faces lighting up the quartersm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुराः

Translation

When the great lord of the asuras had been slain there by the Goddess, the gods together with Indra, led by Vahni, praised that Kātyāyanī, because they had gained their desire — they whose radiant lotus-faces made the quarters bright.

In readable EnglishWhen the Goddess had slain the mighty lord of the asuras, the gods, with Indra among them and Agni at their head, their faces shining like opened lotuses and lighting up every quarter for joy at their wish fulfilled, sang the praise of Kātyāyanī.

‘For joy’ renders the ablative of cause इष्टलाभात् (‘because of gaining what they wished’), which the bright faces express.

Textual verification / uncertain: विकाशि-/विकाशित- are printed with ś (वि + √काश् ‘to shine’). Printings often write विकासि-/विकासित- with s (वि + √कस् ‘to open, blossom’), and the two spellings are frequently confused in manuscripts. The Gita Press reading is kept; either root gives the image of faces opening and shining like lotuses.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • महासुरेन्द्रे mahāsurendre → महासुर + इन्द्रे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + i → e. The same in सेन्द्राः (स + इन्द्राः).

  • सुरा वह्निपुरोगमास्ताम् surā vahnipurogamāstām → सुराः + वह्निपुरोगमाः + ताम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    -āḥ before the voiced v becomes -ā (the y from the visarga is dropped before a consonant); -āḥ before t becomes -ās.

  • तुष्टुवुरिष्टलाभाद् tuṣṭuvuriṣṭalābhād → तुष्टुवुः + इष्टलाभात्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after u, before a vowel, is sounded as r.

  • इष्टलाभाद् विकाशि iṣṭalābhād vikāśi → इष्टलाभात् + विकाशि…

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced v, across the line break; the Gita Press writes the d with a virāma.

  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ → विकाशि + वक्त्र + अब्ज + विकाशित + आशाः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā (वक्त्राब्ज) and a + ā → ā (विकाशिताशाः).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरेन्द्रे mahāsurendre

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member महासुर is a karmadhāraya, with महत् → महा)

    Members
    महासुर + इन्द्र
    Vigraha
    महान्तः असुराः महासुराः; महासुराणाम् इन्द्रः महासुरेन्द्रः, तस्मिन् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ mahāsurāḥ; mahāsurāṇām indraḥ mahāsurendraḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “the lord of the great asuras”
    In the verse
    Śumbha, whose death closes chapter 10

  • सेन्द्राः sendrāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi: सह becomes स)

    Members
    स (= सह) + इन्द्र
    Vigraha
    इन्द्रेण सह वर्तमानाः indreṇa saha vartamānāḥ
    Literally
    “being with Indra”
    In the verse
    the gods with Indra among them

  • वह्निपुरोगमाः vahnipurogamāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (पुरोगम ‘going in front’ is itself an upapada tatpuruṣa: पुरः गच्छति इति)

    Members
    वह्नि + पुरोगम
    Vigraha
    वह्निः पुरोगमः येषां ते vahniḥ purogamaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “having Vahni as the one who goes in front”
    In the verse
    with Agni at their head

  • इष्टलाभात् iṣṭalābhāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    इष्ट + लाभ
    Vigraha
    इष्टस्य लाभः, तस्मात् iṣṭasya lābhaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the gaining of what was desired”
    In the verse
    because their wish — the end of the asuras and the return of their heaven — was fulfilled

  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā relation and a karmadhāraya (विकाशिवक्त्राब्ज)

    Members
    विकाशि + वक्त्राब्ज + विकाशित + आशा
    Vigraha
    विकाशीनि वक्त्राब्जानि विकाशिवक्त्राब्जानि; विकाशिवक्त्राब्जैः विकाशिताः आशाः यैः ते vikāśīni vaktrābjāni vikāśivaktrābjāni; vikāśivaktrābjaiḥ vikāśitāḥ āśāḥ yaiḥ te
    Literally
    “by whom the quarters were made bright with radiant face-lotuses”
    In the verse
    their faces, shining like opened lotuses, lit up every direction

  • वक्त्राब्ज vaktrābja

    karmadhāraya (rūpaka: ‘the face that is a lotus’)

    Members
    वक्त्र + अब्ज
    Vigraha
    वक्त्रम् एव अब्जम् vaktram eva abjam
    Literally
    “face-lotus”
    In the verse
    a face as fresh and open as a lotus
    Alternative
    Also read as an upamita karmadhāraya, वक्त्रम् अब्जम् इव, ‘a face like a lotus’; the image is the same either way.
Morphology & anvaya

  • देव्या devyā

    Instrumental singular of देवी (देवी + आ → देव्या, ī → y before the vowel). The ablative/genitive देव्याः would also be written देव्या before the voiced h; the case is fixed by the sense, as the agent of the passive participle हते.

  • तुष्टुवुः tuṣṭuvuḥ

    Perfect of √स्तु: reduplicated तु-ष्टु-, the s of the root turning to ṣ after the reduplicating u, with the 3rd plural ending -उः (स्तु → तुष्टुव् before the vowel). Compare प्रतुष्टुवुः in 5.7.

  • हते hate

    Past passive participle हत (√हन् loses its nasal before -ta), here locative singular in a locative absolute: देव्या हते महासुरेन्द्रे ‘the great asura-lord having been slain by the Goddess’.

  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ

    विकाशिन् ‘shining, opening’ (वि + √काश् with the agent suffix -in) and विकाशित ‘made bright’ (its causative participle) frame the image: the faces shine, and by their shining the quarters are lit.

Anvaya · prose order

तत्र देव्या महासुरेन्द्रे हते (सति), इष्टलाभात् विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः वह्निपुरोगमाः सेन्द्राः सुराः तां कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुः।

tatra devyā mahāsurendre hate (sati), iṣṭalābhāt vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ vahnipurogamāḥ sendrāḥ surāḥ tāṃ kātyāyanīṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ |

A locative absolute sets the time (देव्या हते … महासुरेन्द्रे); the main clause is सुराः … तुष्टुवुः with its object तां कात्यायनीम्; इष्टलाभात् gives the cause and the long bahuvrīhi describes the gods.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The narrative frame of the hymn. Śumbha has fallen (10.27); the gods, led by Agni and with Indra among them, praise the Goddess under the name Kātyāyanī, the name she already bore on the battlefield (8.29). Their joy is pictured in their faces, which shine like opened lotuses and light up the directions. Verses 11.2–6 are in upajāti (lines of indravajrā and upendravajrā, eleven syllables).

Traditional interpretation

This is the fourth of the great hymns of the Devī Māhātmya, after Brahmā’s hymn (1.73–87), the gods’ hymn in chapter 4 and the hymn of chapter 5. From its refrain नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते (11.8 onward) it is known as the Nārāyaṇī Stuti, and it is often recited by itself. Agni at the head of the gods recalls his Vedic role as their priest and messenger; the text itself gives no reason.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names the Goddess Kātyāyanī (name 556, कात्यायनी). The name is traditionally connected with the sage Kātyāyana; the Devī Māhātmya does not explain it. The two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.3

Śloka · upajāti (triṣṭubh)
‹›

देवि प्रपन्नार्तिहरे प्रसीदप्रसीद मातर्जगतोऽखिलस्य।प्रसीद विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वंत्वमीश्वरी देवि चराचरस्य॥⁠३⁠॥

devi prapannārtihare prasīdaprasīda mātarjagato'khilasya |prasīda viśveśvari pāhi viśvaṃtvamīśvarī devi carācarasya ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवि · प्रपन्नार्तिहरे · प्रसीदप्रसीद · मातः · जगतः · अखिलस्यप्रसीद · विश्वेश्वरि · पाहि · विश्वम्त्वम् · ईश्वरी · देवि · चराचरस्य

devi · prapannārtihare · prasīdaprasīda · mātaḥ · jagataḥ · akhilasyaprasīda · viśveśvari · pāhi · viśvamtvam · īśvarī · devi · carācarasya

Word by word

  1. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  2. प्रपन्नार्तिहरेprapannārtihareO you who take away the distress of those who come to you for refugef. voc. sg. of प्रपन्नार्तिहरा; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P, present stem सीद- ‘to sit’; with प्र ‘to be pleased, gracious’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  4. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousas above: loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana
  5. मातःmātaḥO Motherf. voc. sg. of मातृ (ṛ-stem)
  6. जगतःjagataḥof the worldn. gen. sg. of जगत्; depends on मातः
  7. अखिलस्यakhilasyawhole, entiren. gen. sg. of अखिल; qualifies जगतः
  8. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousas above: loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana
  9. विश्वेश्वरिviśveśvariO Sovereign of the universef. voc. sg. of विश्वेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. पाहिpāhiprotect√पा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. विश्वम्viśvamthe universen. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of पाहि
  12. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject (असि understood)
  13. ईश्वरीīśvarīthe ruler, the sovereignf. nom. sg. of ईश्वरी (feminine of ईश्वर); predicate
  14. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  15. चराचरस्यcarācarasyaof all that moves and does not moven. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on ईश्वरी

Translation

O Goddess, remover of the distress of those who take refuge, be gracious! Be gracious, O Mother of the whole world! Be gracious, O Sovereign of the universe; protect the universe! You, O Goddess, are the ruler of the moving and the unmoving.

In readable EnglishO Goddess, who take away the sorrow of all who come to you for refuge, be gracious! Be gracious, Mother of the whole world! Be gracious, Queen of the universe, and protect it! For you, O Goddess, are sovereign over all that moves and all that is still.

‘For’ makes explicit the link the last line has with the prayers before it; the Sanskrit simply states it.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रपन्नार्तिहरे prapannārtihare → प्रपन्न + आर्ति + हरे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • मातर्जगतोऽखिलस्य mātarjagato'khilasya → मातः + जगतः + अखिलस्य

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    The visarga of the vocative मातः stands for r (मातर्) and reappears before the voiced j. Then -aḥ before a: the visarga becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (जगतोऽखिलस्य).

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari → विश्व + ईश्वरि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e.

  • त्वमीश्वरी tvamīśvarī → त्वम् + ईश्वरी

    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant

    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written together with it; contrast विश्वं at the end of the line above.

  • चराचरस्य carācarasya → चर + अचरस्य

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रपन्नार्तिहरे prapannārtihare

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-हर ‘taking away’, from √हृ) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (प्रपन्नार्ति)

    Members
    प्रपन्न + आर्ति + हर
    Vigraha
    प्रपन्नानाम् आर्तिः प्रपन्नार्तिः; प्रपन्नार्तिं हरति इति प्रपन्नार्तिहरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ prapannānām ārtiḥ prapannārtiḥ; prapannārtiṃ harati iti prapannārtiharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “remover of the affliction of those who have taken refuge”
    In the verse
    she who ends the suffering of whoever turns to her

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    विश्व + ईश्वरी
    Vigraha
    विश्वस्य ईश्वरी viśvasya īśvarī
    Literally
    “sovereign of the universe”
    In the verse
    ruler of all

  • चराचरस्य carācarasya

    samāhāra dvandva

    Members
    चर + अचर
    Vigraha
    चरं च अचरं च, तयोः समाहारः चराचरम्, तस्य caraṃ ca acaraṃ ca, tayoḥ samāhāraḥ carācaram, tasya
    Literally
    “of the moving and the unmoving”
    In the verse
    of every creature and thing, animate and inanimate
    Alternative
    Could be taken as an itaretara dvandva (चराणि च अचराणि च); the singular shows the collective (samāhāra) sense.
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रसीद prasīda

    Imperative of प्र + √सद्: the root takes the present stem सीद- (bhvādi). With प्र it means ‘be pleased, be gracious’. It is said three times, opening three of the four lines.

  • मातः mātaḥ

    Vocative of मातृ: the stem in -ṛ takes guṇa (मातर्) and loses its final r to visarga in pause; the r returns before a voiced sound (मातर्जगतः).

  • पाहि pāhi

    Imperative of √पा ‘protect’ (adādi): the bare root with the ending -हि.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) प्रपन्नार्तिहरे देवि, प्रसीद। (हे) अखिलस्य जगतः मातः, प्रसीद। (हे) विश्वेश्वरि, प्रसीद, विश्वं पाहि। (हे) देवि, त्वं चराचरस्य ईश्वरी (असि)।

(he) prapannārtihare devi, prasīda | (he) akhilasya jagataḥ mātaḥ, prasīda | (he) viśveśvari, prasīda, viśvaṃ pāhi | (he) devi, tvaṃ carācarasya īśvarī (asi) |

Four short sentences, three of them prayers in the imperative (प्रसीद, प्रसीद, प्रसीद … पाहि), each with its own vocative; the last is a statement with असि understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn opens with a threefold prayer, प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’, and each time names the Goddess by a relation: to those who seek refuge, to the world as its mother, to the universe as its sovereign. The last line gives the ground of the prayer: she rules all that moves and all that does not.

Traditional interpretation

The first epithet, प्रपन्नार्तिहरा, puts the hymn’s theme at its head: she is first approached as the one who takes away the distress of the प्रपन्न, those who have come to her for shelter. The theme returns in the refrain verse 11.12 (शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे) and in the gods’ closing prayer for protection (11.24–28, 11.34–35).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the gods have just been rescued, yet their first word is not thanks but ‘be gracious’. Gratitude here takes the form of asking to remain under the same care.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names ईश्वरी (271) and चराचरजगन्नाथा (244), ‘ruler of the moving and unmoving world’, which say what the last line of this verse says. The likeness is shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.4

Śloka · upajāti (triṣṭubh)
‹›

आधारभूता जगतस्त्वमेकामहीस्वरूपेण यतः स्थितासि।अपां स्वरूपस्थितया त्वयैत-दाप्यायते कृत्स्नमलङ्घ्यवीर्ये॥⁠४⁠॥

ādhārabhūtā jagatastvamekāmahīsvarūpeṇa yataḥ sthitāsi |apāṃ svarūpasthitayā tvayaita-dāpyāyate kṛtsnamalaṅghyavīrye ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

आधारभूता · जगतः · त्वम् · एकामहीस्वरूपेण · यतः · स्थिता · असिअपाम् · स्वरूपस्थितया · त्वया · एतत्आप्यायते · कृत्स्नम् · अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये

ādhārabhūtā · jagataḥ · tvam · ekāmahīsvarūpeṇa · yataḥ · sthitā · asiapām · svarūpasthitayā · tvayā · etatāpyāyate · kṛtsnam · alaṅghyavīrye

Word by word

  1. आधारभूताādhārabhūtābeing the supportf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of त्वम्
  2. जगतःjagataḥof the worldn. gen. sg. of जगत्; depends on आधार-
  3. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  4. एकाekāalone, the onef. nom. sg. of एक; qualifies त्वम्
  5. महीस्वरूपेणmahīsvarūpeṇain the form of the earthn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
  6. यतःyataḥbecause, sinceindeclinable (relative adverb, यद् + tasil)
  7. स्थिताsthitāabiding, establishedpast participle (kta) of √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), f. nom. sg.; with असि
  8. असिasiyou are√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. अपाम्apāmof the watersf. gen. pl. of अप् (used only in the plural); depends on स्वरूप-
  10. स्वरूपस्थितयाsvarūpasthitayāabiding in the form (of the waters)f. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वया
  11. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; the agent through whom the world is nourished
  12. एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject of आप्यायते, with कृत्स्नम्
  13. आप्यायतेāpyāyateis nourished, swells, is replenishedआ + √प्यै (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to swell, grow’; present stem प्याय-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  14. कृत्स्नम्kṛtsnamwhole, entiren. nom. sg. of कृत्स्न; qualifies एतत्
  15. अलङ्घ्यवीर्येalaṅghyavīryeO you whose might cannot be overcomef. voc. sg. of अलङ्घ्यवीर्या; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

You alone are the support of the world, since you abide in the form of the earth. By you, abiding in the form of the waters, this whole (world) is nourished, O you whose might cannot be overcome.

In readable EnglishYou alone are the ground on which the world rests, for you abide as the earth. And as the waters you nourish this whole world, O Goddess of invincible power.

The tr turns ‘by you … this whole is nourished’ into an active sentence (‘you nourish’); the vocative is rendered ‘O Goddess of invincible power’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • जगतस्त्वमेका jagatastvamekā → जगतः + त्वम् + एका

    visarga sandhi; m before a vowel · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s. The final m of त्वम् stays m before the vowel of एका and is written with it.

  • स्थितासि sthitāsi → स्थिता + असि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • अपां स्वरूप apāṃ svarūpa → अपाम् + स्वरूप…

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • त्वयैतदाप्यायते tvayaitadāpyāyate → त्वया + एतत् + आप्यायते

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); consonant sandhi (voicing) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    ā + e → ai; then the final t of एतत् becomes d before the vowel of आप्यायते. The word एतत् is broken across the printed lines (त्वयैत- / दाप्यायते); it belongs to the third line, where it begins.

  • कृत्स्नमलङ्घ्यवीर्ये kṛtsnamalaṅghyavīrye → कृत्स्नम् + अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये

    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant

    Final m before a vowel is kept and written together with it.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • आधारभूता ādhārabhūtā

    karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, existing as’ makes the first member a predicate)

    Members
    आधार + भूत
    Vigraha
    आधारः भूता ādhāraḥ bhūtā
    Literally
    “being the support”
    In the verse
    she who is the ground on which the world rests
    Alternative
    Compounds in -भूत are also treated as a supsupā compound (‘any case with any case’); the sense, ‘being the support’, is the same.

  • महीस्वरूपेण mahīsvarūpeṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मही + स्वरूप
    Vigraha
    मह्याः स्वरूपम्, तेन mahyāḥ svarūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the form of the earth”
    In the verse
    as the earth itself

  • स्वरूपस्थितया svarūpasthitayā

    saptamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    स्वरूप + स्थित
    Vigraha
    स्वरूपे स्थिता, तया svarūpe sthitā, tayā
    Literally
    “abiding in the form”
    In the verse
    existing as (the waters); the genitive अपाम् outside the compound depends on its first member स्वरूप

  • अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये alaṅghyavīrye

    bahuvrīhi (its first member अलङ्घ्य is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न लङ्घ्यम्)

    Members
    अलङ्घ्य + वीर्य
    Vigraha
    अलङ्घ्यं वीर्यं यस्याः सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ alaṅghyaṃ vīryaṃ yasyāḥ sā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “whose might is not to be overstepped”
    In the verse
    O you of invincible power
Morphology & anvaya

  • आप्यायते āpyāyate

    Present ātmanepada of आ + √प्यै (प्याय-): ‘swells, is filled, thrives’. It is intransitive, not a passive; त्वया names the one through whom the world is nourished.

  • अपाम् apām

    Genitive plural of अप् ‘water’, a feminine noun used only in the plural (nom. आपः, instr. अद्भिः, gen. अपाम्).

  • अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये alaṅghyavīrye

    लङ्घ्य is a gerundive (√लङ्घ् ‘to leap over’ + -ya): ‘to be overstepped’; with the negative अ-, ‘not to be overstepped’, i.e. irresistible.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये, त्वम् एका जगतः आधारभूता (असि), यतः महीस्वरूपेण स्थिता असि। अपां स्वरूपस्थितया त्वया एतत् कृत्स्नम् आप्यायते।

(he) alaṅghyavīrye, tvam ekā jagataḥ ādhārabhūtā (asi), yataḥ mahīsvarūpeṇa sthitā asi | apāṃ svarūpasthitayā tvayā etat kṛtsnam āpyāyate |

Two sentences: the first states that she alone is the support of the world and gives the reason in a यतः clause; the second, with the intransitive verb आप्यायते, says the whole world is nourished through her as water. The vocative stands at the end.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods name two of the elements as her forms. As earth she holds the world up; as water she makes it swell and thrive. The word यतः makes the first line a reason: she is the world’s support because she is the earth.

Traditional interpretation

The verse continues the teaching of Brahmā’s hymn, that the Goddess is not only the power behind the world but its very substance (1.76: त्वयैतत्पाल्यते, ‘by you this is protected’). Earth and water are named because they are the ground and the nourishment of life; the tradition reads them as standing for all the elements.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the ground under one’s feet and the water one drinks are named here as the Goddess herself. Reverence begins with what is most ordinary and most needed.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names मही (718), ‘the earth’, and सर्वाधारा (659), ‘the support of all’. The agreement is in shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.5

Śloka · indravajrā
‹›

त्वं वैष्णवी शक्तिरनन्तवीर्याविश्वस्य बीजं परमासि माया।सम्मोहितं देवि समस्तमेतत्त्वं वै प्रसन्ना भुवि मुक्तिहेतुः॥⁠५⁠॥

tvaṃ vaiṣṇavī śaktiranantavīryāviśvasya bījaṃ paramāsi māyā |sammohitaṃ devi samastametattvaṃ vai prasannā bhuvi muktihetuḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

त्वम् · वैष्णवी · शक्तिः · अनन्तवीर्याविश्वस्य · बीजम् · परमा · असि · मायासम्मोहितम् · देवि · समस्तम् · एतत्त्वम् · वै · प्रसन्ना · भुवि · मुक्तिहेतुः

tvam · vaiṣṇavī · śaktiḥ · anantavīryāviśvasya · bījam · paramā · asi · māyāsammohitam · devi · samastam · etattvam · vai · prasannā · bhuvi · muktihetuḥ

Word by word

  1. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  2. वैष्णवीvaiṣṇavīof Viṣṇu, Vaiṣṇavīf. nom. sg. of वैष्णवी (taddhita from विष्णु: ‘belonging to Viṣṇu’, with feminine -ī); qualifies शक्तिः
  3. शक्तिःśaktiḥpower, Śaktif. nom. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); predicate
  4. अनन्तवीर्याanantavīryāof boundless mightf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शक्तिः
  5. विश्वस्यviśvasyaof the universen. gen. sg. of विश्व; depends on बीजम्
  6. बीजम्bījamthe seedn. nom. sg. of बीज; predicate
  7. परमाparamāsupremef. nom. sg. of परम; qualifies माया
  8. असिasiyou are√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. मायाmāyāMāyā, the power that veils and enchantsf. nom. sg. of माया; predicate
  10. सम्मोहितम्sammohitamis held in delusion, bewilderedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of सम् + √मुह् (divādi, 4P ‘to be confused’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of एतत्
  11. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  12. समस्तम्samastamall, entiren. nom. sg. of समस्त; qualifies एतत्
  13. एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
  14. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  15. वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  16. प्रसन्नाprasannāwhen pleased, graciouspast participle (kta) of प्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be pleased’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies त्वम्
  17. भुविbhuvion earthf. loc. sg. of भू (ū-stem)
  18. मुक्तिहेतुःmuktihetuḥthe cause of liberationm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate

Translation

You are the power of Viṣṇu, of boundless might; you are the seed of the universe, the supreme Māyā. All this, O Goddess, is held in delusion; you indeed, when pleased, are the cause of liberation on earth.

In readable EnglishYou are Viṣṇu’s own power, boundless in might, the seed of the universe, the supreme Māyā. By you, O Goddess, this whole world is held in enchantment; and you, when you are gracious, are the cause of liberation on earth.

‘By you’ in the third sentence is supplied: the participle सम्मोहितम् implies an agent, and the verse has just named her as Māyā.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शक्तिरनन्तवीर्या śaktiranantavīryā → शक्तिः + अनन्तवीर्या

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after i, before a vowel, is sounded as r.

  • परमासि paramāsi → परमा + असि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • सम्मोहितं देवि sammohitaṃ devi → सम्मोहितम् + देवि

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra. The same gives त्वं वैष्णवी, बीजं परमा and त्वं वै; before the vowel of एतत् the m of समस्तम् stays m (समस्तमेतत्).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अनन्तवीर्या anantavīryā

    bahuvrīhi (its first member अनन्त is itself a nañ-bahuvrīhi: न विद्यते अन्तः यस्य)

    Members
    अनन्त + वीर्य
    Vigraha
    अनन्तं वीर्यं यस्याः सा anantaṃ vīryaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “whose might has no end”
    In the verse
    of boundless power

  • मुक्तिहेतुः muktihetuḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मुक्ति + हेतु
    Vigraha
    मुक्तेः हेतुः mukteḥ hetuḥ
    Literally
    “the cause of release”
    In the verse
    the one through whom beings are set free
Morphology & anvaya

  • सम्मोहितम् sammohitam

    मोहित is the past participle of the causative मोहयति ‘causes to be bewildered’: the world ‘has been bewildered’ — by her, as the previous line implies. Compare 1.55, तया सम्मोह्यते जगत्.

  • मुक्तिहेतुः muktihetuḥ

    A tatpuruṣa keeps the gender of its last member: हेतु is masculine, so ‘you (f.) are the cause’ is त्वं … मुक्तिहेतुः, as in 4.9 (या मुक्तिहेतुः).

  • वैष्णवी vaiṣṇavī

    Taddhita: विष्णु + aṇ with vṛddhi of the first syllable (वैष्णव) and feminine -ī: ‘belonging to Viṣṇu, Viṣṇu’s’.

Anvaya · prose order

त्वम् अनन्तवीर्या वैष्णवी शक्तिः (असि)। (त्वं) विश्वस्य बीजं परमा माया असि। (हे) देवि, एतत् समस्तं (त्वया) सम्मोहितम्। त्वं वै प्रसन्ना (सती) भुवि मुक्तिहेतुः (भवसि)।

tvam anantavīryā vaiṣṇavī śaktiḥ (asi) | (tvaṃ) viśvasya bījaṃ paramā māyā asi | (he) devi, etat samastaṃ (tvayā) sammohitam | tvaṃ vai prasannā (satī) bhuvi muktihetuḥ (bhavasi) |

Four predications about the Goddess: Viṣṇu’s power, the seed of the world, the supreme Māyā (verb असि), and, when gracious, the cause of liberation. Between them stands the effect of her Māyā: the whole world is bewildered.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The verse holds both sides of the Goddess together: as Māyā she keeps the whole world bewildered, and when she is pleased she is the cause of release. Binding and freeing come from the same power.

Traditional interpretation

This is the teaching of the first carita repeated in the gods’ mouths: Mahāmāyā, Hari’s power, bewilders the world (1.55), and when gracious (प्रसन्ना) she becomes the cause of liberation (1.57). ‘Vaiṣṇavī śakti’ recalls the name Viṣṇumāyā under which the gods praised her in chapter 5 (5.14–16).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: what clouds the mind and what clears it are not two rival forces here. The same reality appears as confusion while it is resisted and as freedom once it is met with devotion.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names वैष्णवी (892), विष्णुमाया (339), महामाया (215) and मुक्तिदा (736), ‘giver of liberation’. The names belong to a shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.6

Śloka · upajāti (triṣṭubh)
‹›

विद्याः समस्तास्तव देवि भेदाःस्त्रियः समस्ताः सकला जगत्सु।त्वयैकया पूरितमम्बयैतत्का ते स्तुतिः स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः॥⁠६⁠॥

vidyāḥ samastāstava devi bhedāḥstriyaḥ samastāḥ sakalā jagatsu |tvayaikayā pūritamambayaitatkā te stutiḥ stavyaparā paroktiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

विद्याः · समस्ताः · तव · देवि · भेदाःस्त्रियः · समस्ताः · सकलाः · जगत्सुत्वया · एकया · पूरितम् · अम्बया · एतत्का · ते · स्तुतिः · स्तव्यपरा · परोक्तिः

vidyāḥ · samastāḥ · tava · devi · bhedāḥstriyaḥ · samastāḥ · sakalāḥ · jagatsutvayā · ekayā · pūritam · ambayā · etatkā · te · stutiḥ · stavyaparā · paroktiḥ

Word by word

  1. विद्याःvidyāḥthe branches of knowledgef. nom. pl. of विद्या; subject
  2. समस्ताःsamastāḥallf. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies विद्याः
  3. तवtavayoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, gen. sg.; depends on भेदाः
  4. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  5. भेदाःbhedāḥdistinct forms, divisionsm. nom. pl. of भेद; predicate (also understood with स्त्रियः)
  6. स्त्रियःstriyaḥwomenf. nom. pl. of स्त्री; second subject
  7. समस्ताःsamastāḥallf. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies स्त्रियः
  8. सकलाःsakalāḥevery one, entiref. nom. pl. of सकल; qualifies स्त्रियः
  9. जगत्सुjagatsuin the worldsn. loc. pl. of जगत्
  10. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of पूरितम्
  11. एकयाekayāalone, singlef. instr. sg. of एक; qualifies त्वया
  12. पूरितम्pūritamis filledpast passive participle (kta) of the causative पूरयति (√पॄ, ‘to fill’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of एतत्
  13. अम्बयाambayāby the Motherf. instr. sg. of अम्बा; in apposition to त्वया
  14. एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
  15. काkāwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies स्तुतिः
  16. तेteof you, for yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, gen. sg. (enclitic); depends on स्तुतिः
  17. स्तुतिःstutiḥpraisef. nom. sg. of स्तुति (i-stem); subject
  18. स्तव्यपराstavyaparādirected to what is to be praisedf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies परोक्तिः
  19. परोक्तिःparoktiḥan exalted utterancef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to स्तुतिः

Translation

All branches of knowledge, O Goddess, are your distinct forms; all women in the worlds, every one of them. By you, the one Mother, this (world) is filled. What praise is there of you — (praise being) an exalted utterance directed to what is to be praised?

In readable EnglishEvery branch of knowledge, O Goddess, is a form of you, and so is every woman in all the worlds. You alone, the Mother, fill this whole world. What praise can there be of you? Praise is lofty speech about something to be praised.

The last pāda is read as a definition of praise: praise speaks of something as its object, and the verse has just said there is nothing she does not fill. The other reading (‘you who are beyond the praiseworthy, the supreme Word’) is given under Samāsa and in the flags.

Textual verification / uncertain: The last pāda, स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः, is read in two ways: as a definition of praise in apposition to स्तुतिः (‘an exalted utterance directed to what is to be praised’), followed here because both words are nominative like स्तुतिः; or as epithets of the Goddess (‘beyond the praiseworthy, the supreme Word’), which needs त्वम् to be understood. Both readings are traditional in translation; the point of the verse — no praise can match her — is the same.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तास्तव samastāstava → समस्ताः + तव

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s. Before s the visarga is kept (विद्याः समस्ताः, स्त्रियः समस्ताः, समस्ताः सकलाः, स्तुतिः स्तव्यपरा).

  • सकला जगत्सु sakalā jagatsu → सकलाः + जगत्सु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound becomes -ā (the y from the visarga is dropped before a consonant).

  • त्वयैकया tvayaikayā → त्वया + एकया

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai. The same in अम्बयैतत् (अम्बया + एतत्).

  • पूरितमम्बयैतत् pūritamambayaitat → पूरितम् + अम्बया + एतत्

    no change (m before a vowel); vṛddhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant; वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    The m of पूरितम् is kept before the vowel and written with it; then ā + e → ai.

  • परोक्तिः paroktiḥ → परा + उक्तिः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    In the compound the feminine परा takes the masculine stem form पर (a karmadhāraya), and a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • स्तव्यपरा stavyaparā

    bahuvrīhi (पर at the end of a compound: ‘having … as its chief concern’)

    Members
    स्तव्य + पर
    Vigraha
    स्तव्यं परं प्रधानं यस्याः सा stavyaṃ paraṃ pradhānaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “having what is to be praised as its chief concern”
    In the verse
    speech whose whole business is something worth praising — the definition of a hymn
    Alternative
    Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa, स्तव्येभ्यः परा, ‘beyond the things that can be praised’, and referred to the Goddess (‘you who are beyond the praiseworthy’). But the word is nominative and agrees with स्तुतिः, not with the genitive ते; the bahuvrīhi keeps the grammar of the line.

  • परोक्तिः paroktiḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    पर + उक्ति
    Vigraha
    परा उक्तिः parā uktiḥ
    Literally
    “a high utterance”
    In the verse
    lofty, excellent speech
    Alternative
    Also read as परा उक्तिः in the sense of ‘the supreme Word’ (the Goddess as speech itself); again the agreement with स्तुतिः favours ‘exalted utterance’.
Morphology & anvaya

  • स्त्रियः striyaḥ

    Nominative plural of स्त्री: the ī becomes iy before a vowel ending (स्त्रि-य्-अः) instead of y, an irregularity of this word.

  • जगत्सु jagatsu

    Locative plural of जगत्: the ending -su is added directly to the stem in t.

  • स्तव्यपरा stavyaparā

    स्तव्य is a gerundive of √स्तु with -ya (yat): the root vowel takes guṇa (स्तो-) and o becomes av before the y (स्तव्य), ‘to be praised’.

  • पूरितम् pūritam

    Participle of the causative stem पूरय- (√पॄ ‘fill’) with -ita: ‘made full, filled’. Its agent is त्वया एकया … अम्बया, ‘by you alone, the Mother’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, समस्ताः विद्याः तव भेदाः (सन्ति); जगत्सु समस्ताः सकलाः स्त्रियः (तव भेदाः)। त्वया एकया अम्बया एतत् पूरितम्। ते का स्तुतिः (स्यात्)? (स्तुतिः हि) स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः।

(he) devi, samastāḥ vidyāḥ tava bhedāḥ (santi); jagatsu samastāḥ sakalāḥ striyaḥ (tava bhedāḥ) | tvayā ekayā ambayā etat pūritam | te kā stutiḥ (syāt)? (stutiḥ hi) stavyaparā paroktiḥ |

Two nominal sentences (all knowledge, all women are her forms); a passive one (the world is filled by her alone); then a question and, in apposition, what praise is: the last two nominatives define स्तुतिः.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Every form of knowledge and every woman in every world are named as her own forms, and the whole world is filled by her alone. The verse then asks what praise could be offered to such a one: praise speaks of an object, and there is nothing outside her to speak of.

Traditional interpretation

The Śākta tradition has drawn from this verse its teaching that every woman is to be honoured as a form of the Goddess. The ‘branches of knowledge’ are traditionally understood as all the sciences, sacred and secular. The doubt about praise is a classic theme of hymns: the hymn goes on praising while confessing that praise cannot reach her (compare 4.4 and 4.6, and the close of Brahmā’s hymn, 1.83–85: कस्त्वां स्तोतुमिहेश्वरः).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the verse places the sacred in the women one actually meets and in every honest form of learning. Its reverence is meant to be practised in ordinary life, not kept for the shrine.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names विद्या (549) and विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी (402), ‘whose form is both knowledge and ignorance’. The idea is shared Śākta thought; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.7

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सर्वभूता यदा देवी स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी।त्वं स्तुता स्तुतये का वा भवन्तु परमोक्तयः॥⁠७⁠॥

sarvabhūtā yadā devī svargamuktipradāyinī |tvaṃ stutā stutaye kā vā bhavantu paramoktayaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सर्वभूता · यदा · देवी · स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनीत्वम् · स्तुता · स्तुतये · काः · वा · भवन्तु · परमोक्तयः

sarvabhūtā · yadā · devī · svargamuktipradāyinītvam · stutā · stutaye · kāḥ · vā · bhavantu · paramoktayaḥ

Word by word

  1. सर्वभूताsarvabhūtāwho have become all thingsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम् … देवी
  2. यदाyadāwhen, sinceindeclinable (relative adverb of time)
  3. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; in apposition to त्वम्
  4. स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनीsvargamuktipradāyinīthe giver of heaven and liberationf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम् … देवी
  5. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of स्तुता (असि understood)
  6. स्तुताstutāare praised, have been praisedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; predicate
  7. स्तुतयेstutayefor praisef. dat. sg. of स्तुति (i-stem); dative of purpose
  8. काःkāḥwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. pl.; qualifies परमोक्तयः
  9. वाvāthen, indeed (in a question)indeclinable (particle, here strengthening the question)
  10. भवन्तुbhavantucould be, should be√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada; in a question, ‘what could be?’
  11. परमोक्तयःparamoktayaḥsupreme utterances, loftier wordsf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of भवन्तु

Translation

When you, the Goddess who have become all things, the giver of heaven and liberation, are (thus) praised, what supreme utterances could there be for praise?

In readable EnglishWhen you, the Goddess who are all that is, who grant both heaven and liberation, have been praised as that, what loftier words could be left to praise you?

‘As that’ makes explicit that the praise consists in the epithets just given; the Sanskrit says only ‘you are praised’. ‘Left’ renders the force of the rhetorical question.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वं स्तुता tvaṃ stutā → त्वम् + स्तुता

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • का वा kā vā → काः + वा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound becomes -ā. The printed का is therefore the plural काः, agreeing with परमोक्तयः and भवन्तु, not the singular का.

  • परमोक्तयः paramoktayaḥ → परम + उक्तयः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूता sarvabhūtā

    karmadhāraya (भूत ‘become, being’ with the first member as predicate, as in आधारभूता, 11.4)

    Members
    सर्व + भूत
    Vigraha
    सर्वं भूता sarvaṃ bhūtā
    Literally
    “become all”
    In the verse
    she who is everything that exists
    Alternative
    Also taken as ‘she who is all beings’ (सर्वाणि भूतानि यस्याः स्वरूपम्, a bahuvrīhi); but भूत at the end of such compounds usually means ‘being, become’, and the sense is the same.

  • स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी svargamuktipradāyinī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-प्रदायिन्, from प्र + √दा with -in) containing an itaretara dvandva (स्वर्गमुक्ती)

    Members
    स्वर्ग + मुक्ति + प्रदायिन्
    Vigraha
    स्वर्गः च मुक्तिः च स्वर्गमुक्ती; ते प्रददाति इति स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी svargaḥ ca muktiḥ ca svargamuktī; te pradadāti iti svargamuktipradāyinī
    Literally
    “she who gives heaven and liberation”
    In the verse
    the giver of both worldly-heavenly reward and final release

  • परमोक्तयः paramoktayaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    परम + उक्ति
    Vigraha
    परमाः उक्तयः paramāḥ uktayaḥ
    Literally
    “supreme utterances”
    In the verse
    words higher still, more exalted praise
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रदायिनी pradāyinī

    From प्र + √दा with the agent suffix -in: before it the root in ā takes y (दा → दाय्), giving प्रदायिन्, feminine प्रदायिनी ‘giver’.

  • भवन्तु bhavantu

    An imperative used in a rhetorical question: ‘what loftier words should there be?’ — i.e. none could be.

  • स्तुतये stutaye

    Dative of स्तुति: i-stems take guṇa before the dative ending (स्तुते + ए → स्तुतये). The dative expresses purpose: ‘for (the sake of) praise’.

Anvaya · prose order

यदा सर्वभूता स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी देवी त्वं स्तुता (असि), (तदा) स्तुतये काः वा परमोक्तयः भवन्तु?

yadā sarvabhūtā svargamuktipradāyinī devī tvaṃ stutā (asi), (tadā) stutaye kāḥ vā paramoktayaḥ bhavantu?

A यदा clause (with तदा understood) and a rhetorical question: once she has been named as all that is and as the giver of heaven and liberation, no further words for praise remain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The upajāti stanzas end and the anuṣṭubh verses begin. The gods close their first argument: to say that she has become all things, and that she grants both heaven and liberation, is already the whole of praise; no finer words remain.

Traditional interpretation

The verse completes the thought of 11.6. Since everything is her form, naming her as everything is the highest praise. From the next verse the hymn turns from statement to salutation, ending each verse with नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name स्वर्गापवर्गदा (764), ‘giver of heaven and liberation’, which the next verse here also uses (स्वर्गापवर्गदे, 11.8). The two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.8

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सर्वस्य बुद्धिरूपेण जनस्य हृदि संस्थिते।स्वर्गापवर्गदे देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠८⁠॥

sarvasya buddhirūpeṇa janasya hṛdi saṃsthite |svargāpavargade devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सर्वस्य · बुद्धिरूपेण · जनस्य · हृदि · संस्थितेस्वर्गापवर्गदे · देवि · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

sarvasya · buddhirūpeṇa · janasya · hṛdi · saṃsthitesvargāpavargade · devi · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. सर्वस्यsarvasyaof everypronominal adjective सर्व, m. gen. sg.; qualifies जनस्य
  2. बुद्धिरूपेणbuddhirūpeṇain the form of understandingn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
  3. जनस्यjanasyaperson, beingm. gen. sg. of जन; depends on हृदि
  4. हृदिhṛdiin the heartn. loc. sg. of हृद् (used for हृदय)
  5. संस्थितेsaṃsthiteO you who abidef. voc. sg. of संस्थिता, past participle (kta) of सम् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’)
  6. स्वर्गापवर्गदेsvargāpavargadeO giver of heaven and final releasef. voc. sg. of स्वर्गापवर्गदा; compound (see Samāsa)
  7. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  8. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी (feminine of नारायण, with -ī: ‘she who belongs to, or is the power of, Nārāyaṇa’)
  9. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस् (s-stem); subject of अस्तु
  10. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic form); dative with नमः

Translation

O you who abide in the heart of every person in the form of understanding, giver of heaven and final release, O Goddess, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishYou dwell as understanding in the heart of every being, and you grant both heaven and final release: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocative epithets are turned into statements (‘You dwell …, you grant …’) and the address is gathered at the end.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • संस्थिते saṃsthite → सम् + स्थिते

    anusvāra sandhi with a prefix · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    The m of the prefix सम् becomes anusvāra before s.

  • स्वर्गापवर्गदे svargāpavargade → स्वर्ग + अपवर्ग + दे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: the s (as रु) becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha). This junction closes every verse of the refrain.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • बुद्धिरूपेण buddhirūpeṇa

    karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa), as in the yā devī series (5.20-22)

    Members
    बुद्धि + रूप
    Vigraha
    बुद्धिः एव रूपम्, तेन buddhiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the form that is understanding”
    In the verse
    as the intellect, the discerning mind
    Alternative
    Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, बुद्धेः रूपम् ‘the form of understanding’; the sense is the same.

  • स्वर्गापवर्गदे svargāpavargade

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-द ‘giving’, from √दा) containing an itaretara dvandva (स्वर्गापवर्गौ)

    Members
    स्वर्ग + अपवर्ग + द
    Vigraha
    स्वर्गः च अपवर्गः च स्वर्गापवर्गौ; तौ ददाति इति स्वर्गापवर्गदा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ svargaḥ ca apavargaḥ ca svargāpavargau; tau dadāti iti svargāpavargadā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “giver of heaven and release”
    In the verse
    she who grants both the reward of heaven and final liberation (अपवर्ग = मोक्ष)
Morphology & anvaya

  • नारायणि nārāyaṇi

    Vocative of नारायणी: ī-stems shorten the final vowel in the vocative (नारायणी → नारायणि). The feminine of नारायण names the Goddess as Nārāyaṇa’s power, or Nārāyaṇa in feminine form.

  • अस्तु astu

    Imperative third person of √अस्: ‘let (homage) be’. With नमः and the dative ते it forms the standard formula of salutation.

  • हृदि hṛdi

    Locative of the consonant stem हृद्, which supplies the oblique cases of हृदय ‘heart’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सर्वस्य जनस्य हृदि बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिते, स्वर्गापवर्गदे देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sarvasya janasya hṛdi buddhirūpeṇa saṃsthite, svargāpavargade devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

A chain of vocatives (संस्थिते … स्वर्गापवर्गदे … देवि … नारायणि) and one main clause, नमः अस्तु ते ‘homage be to you’ — the pattern of every refrain verse.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first verse of the refrain. The Goddess is greeted as the understanding (buddhi) that lives in every heart, and as the giver of heaven and final release; the line नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते, ‘O Nārāyaṇī, homage to you’, now closes every verse to 11.23.

Traditional interpretation

The refrain gives the hymn its name, Nārāyaṇī Stuti. The thought of the first line repeats the yā devī stanza of chapter 5, या देवी सर्वभूतेषु बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिता (5.20–22), and the second line returns at the end of the text, where the Goddess, worshipped, is भोगस्वर्गापवर्गदा (13.5).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the Goddess is placed first not in heaven but in the mind’s power to understand. Clear understanding, on this view, is already her presence, and the way to both the good life and release runs through it.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has both names: स्वर्गापवर्गदा (764) and नारायणी (298), and also महाबुद्धिः (223). The agreement is in shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.9

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण परिणामप्रदायिनि।विश्वस्योपरतौ शक्ते नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠९⁠॥

kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa pariṇāmapradāyini |viśvasyoparatau śakte nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण · परिणामप्रदायिनिविश्वस्य · उपरतौ · शक्ते · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa · pariṇāmapradāyiniviśvasya · uparatau · śakte · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेणkalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇain the form of kalās, kāṣṭhās and the other units of timen. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
  2. परिणामप्रदायिनिpariṇāmapradāyiniO you who bring about changef. voc. sg. of परिणामप्रदायिनी; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. विश्वस्यviśvasyaof the universen. gen. sg. of विश्व; depends on उपरतौ
  4. उपरतौuparatauin the ceasing, in the dissolutionf. loc. sg. of उपरति (i-stem; उप + √रम् ‘to cease’ + -ti)
  5. शक्तेśakteO Power (able to bring it about)f. voc. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); see Morph for शक्ता
  6. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  7. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  8. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who, in the form of kalās, kāṣṭhās and the rest, bring about change; O Power in the dissolution of the universe, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishAs the moments and measures of time you bring all things through their changes, and yours is the power that brings the universe to rest: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Moments and measures of time’ renders कला, काष्ठा and the rest, which are small units of time; ‘brings to rest’ renders उपरति ‘cessation’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa → कला + काष्ठा + आदि + रूपेण

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā (काष्ठा + आदि).

  • विश्वस्योपरतौ viśvasyoparatau → विश्वस्य + उपरतौ

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, containing a bahuvrīhi (-आदि ‘and the rest’) built on an itaretara dvandva (कलाकाष्ठे)

    Members
    कला + काष्ठा + आदि + रूप
    Vigraha
    कला च काष्ठा च कलाकाष्ठे; कलाकाष्ठे आदी येषां ते कलाकाष्ठादयः; तेषां रूपम्, तेन kalā ca kāṣṭhā ca kalākāṣṭhe; kalākāṣṭhe ādī yeṣāṃ te kalākāṣṭhādayaḥ; teṣāṃ rūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the form of kalā, kāṣṭhā and the rest”
    In the verse
    as time itself, in its measured units

  • परिणामप्रदायिनि pariṇāmapradāyini

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    परिणाम + प्रदायिन्
    Vigraha
    परिणामं प्रददाति इति परिणामप्रदायिनी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ pariṇāmaṃ pradadāti iti pariṇāmapradāyinī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “giver of transformation”
    In the verse
    she who brings every thing through its changes: growth, ripening, decay
Morphology & anvaya

  • शक्ते śakte

    The vocative शक्ते belongs equally to शक्ति ‘power’ (i-stems take guṇa: शक्ति → शक्ते) and to शक्ता ‘able’ (ā-stems: शक्ता → शक्ते). As शक्ता with the locative it means ‘O you who are able to bring about the world’s dissolution’; as शक्ति, ‘O Power at work in the world’s dissolution’. The hymn calls her शक्ति in 11.5 and शक्तिभूते in 11.11, which favours शक्ति; the sense is close either way.

  • उपरतौ uparatau

    Locative of उपरति (√रम् with उप ‘to cease’; the nasal is lost before -ti): ‘in the cessation’, i.e. at the dissolution of the universe.

  • परिणामप्रदायिनि pariṇāmapradāyini

    Vocative of the feminine in -inī (प्रदायिन् + ī), shortened to -ini in address.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण परिणामप्रदायिनि, विश्वस्य उपरतौ शक्ते नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa pariṇāmapradāyini, viśvasya uparatau śakte nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Vocatives (परिणामप्रदायिनि, शक्ते, नारायणि) followed by the refrain; the instrumental कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण says how she brings change, the locative विश्वस्य उपरतौ where her power is shown.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Goddess is time. As its units — kāṣṭhā, kalā and the rest — she brings every thing through its changes, and she is the power by which the universe at last comes to rest.

Traditional interpretation

In the classical reckoning of time a kāṣṭhā is eighteen blinks (nimeṣa) and thirty kāṣṭhās make a kalā; the verse names the smallest measures to say that no moment is outside her. परिणाम, ‘transformation’, is the Sāṃkhya word for the change of prakṛti, and the text has already called her the supreme prakṛti (4.7, 1.78).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: change and ending are named here among her gifts. The same power that sustains things also lets them pass, and both are met with salutation.

Verse 11.10

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये शिवे सर्वार्थसाधिके।शरण्ये त्र्यम्बके गौरि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१०⁠॥

sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye śive sarvārthasādhike |śaraṇye tryambake gauri nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये · शिवे · सर्वार्थसाधिकेशरण्ये · त्र्यम्बके · गौरि · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye · śive · sarvārthasādhikeśaraṇye · tryambake · gauri · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्येsarvamaṅgalamaṅgalyeO auspiciousness of all that is auspiciousf. voc. sg. of सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्या; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. शिवेśiveO auspicious one, O Śivāf. voc. sg. of शिवा
  3. सर्वार्थसाधिकेsarvārthasādhikeO you who accomplish every aimf. voc. sg. of सर्वार्थसाधिका; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. शरण्येśaraṇyeO refuge, O you who give shelterf. voc. sg. of शरण्या (taddhita from शरण with -ya: ‘fit for refuge, protecting’)
  5. त्र्यम्बकेtryambakeO three-eyed onef. voc. sg. of त्र्यम्बका; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. गौरिgauriO Gaurī, O fair onef. voc. sg. of गौरी
  7. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  8. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  9. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O auspiciousness of all that is auspicious, O Śivā, accomplisher of every aim, O refuge, O three-eyed one, O Gaurī, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishBlessing of all that is blessed, auspicious Śivā, who bring every aim to fulfilment, our refuge, three-eyed Gaurī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

शिवे is rendered both by its meaning ‘auspicious’ and as the name Śivā; the vocatives are grouped into phrases.

Textual verification / uncertain: Variant (corrections.json, status ‘Textual Verification Required’): GRETIL and Freund read सर्वमङ्गलमाङ्गल्ये, with the vṛddhi form माङ्गल्य (‘auspiciousness’). The Gita Press सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये, the form generally recited, is kept. The metre is unaffected (both are eight syllables, with the sixth heavy either way), and the sense — she is the auspiciousness of all auspicious things — is the same.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike → सर्व + अर्थ + साधिके

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • त्र्यम्बके tryambake → त्रि + अम्बके

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before a vowel becomes y.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8. Between the vocatives of this verse there is no junction to make: each ends in a vowel and the next begins with a consonant.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, its first member a karmadhāraya (सर्वमङ्गल)

    Members
    सर्व + मङ्गल + मङ्गल्य
    Vigraha
    सर्वाणि मङ्गलानि सर्वमङ्गलानि; सर्वमङ्गलानां मङ्गल्या सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्या, तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarvāṇi maṅgalāni sarvamaṅgalāni; sarvamaṅgalānāṃ maṅgalyā sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalyā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “the auspicious one of all auspicious things”
    In the verse
    she who makes every auspicious thing auspicious — the source of all blessing
    Alternative
    Also read with a superlative sense, ‘the most auspicious among all auspicious things’; a partitive (‘among’) relation is expressed by the locative, सर्वमङ्गलेषु मङ्गल्या, a saptamī tatpuruṣa. The source-sense fits the verse’s run of epithets of blessing.

  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-साधिका, agent noun of the causative of √साध्) containing a karmadhāraya (सर्वार्थ)

    Members
    सर्व + अर्थ + साधिका
    Vigraha
    सर्वे अर्थाः सर्वार्थाः; सर्वार्थान् साधयति इति सर्वार्थसाधिका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarve arthāḥ sarvārthāḥ; sarvārthān sādhayati iti sarvārthasādhikā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “accomplisher of all aims”
    In the verse
    she who brings every purpose of her devotees to fulfilment

  • त्र्यम्बके tryambake

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    त्रि + अम्बक
    Vigraha
    त्रीणि अम्बकानि यस्याः सा त्र्यम्बका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ trīṇi ambakāni yasyāḥ sā tryambakā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she who has three eyes”
    In the verse
    the three-eyed one, the consort of Tryambaka (Śiva)
    Alternative
    अम्बक is ‘eye’; the name is sometimes also connected with अम्बा ‘mother’, but the grammar of the word supports ‘three-eyed’.
Morphology & anvaya

  • सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye

    मङ्गल्य is a taddhita adjective from मङ्गल with -ya, ‘auspicious, bringing good fortune’; feminine मङ्गल्या, vocative -ल्ये. The variant माङ्गल्य (with vṛddhi) is the abstract noun ‘auspiciousness’ — see the flags.

  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike

    साधक ‘one who accomplishes’ (causative of √साध् with -aka); in the feminine the a before k becomes i: साधिका.

  • गौरि gauri

    Vocative of गौरी (‘the fair one’, the name of Pārvatī): the final ī is shortened in address.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये शिवे सर्वार्थसाधिके शरण्ये त्र्यम्बके गौरि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye śive sarvārthasādhike śaraṇye tryambake gauri nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Seven vocatives in a row, then the refrain नमः अस्तु ते.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A verse made only of names. She is the blessing in all that is blessed, Śivā, the one who fulfils every aim, the refuge, the three-eyed Gaurī — and, in the refrain, Nārāyaṇī.

Traditional interpretation

This is the most widely recited verse of the Devī Māhātmya; it is said on its own as a verse of blessing at the beginning and end of worship and on auspicious occasions. Its names belong to Śiva’s consort (Śivā, Tryambakā, Gaurī), while the refrain names her as Nārāyaṇī, the power of Viṣṇu. The tradition reads this as the text’s teaching that the powers of all the gods are one Goddess (as in her birth from the gods’ combined radiance in chapter 2).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: a verse of pure address, asking nothing. It is recited at beginnings and endings because it names the good before any request is made.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has सर्वमङ्गला (200), शिवा (53), त्र्यम्बका (762), गौरी (635) and नारायणी (298). Most names of this verse are among its names; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.11

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते सनातनि।गुणाश्रये गुणमये नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠११⁠॥

sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte sanātani |guṇāśraye guṇamaye nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् · शक्तिभूते · सनातनिगुणाश्रये · गुणमये · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

sṛṣṭisthitivināśānām · śaktibhūte · sanātaniguṇāśraye · guṇamaye · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम्sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāmof creation, preservation and destructionm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on शक्ति-
  2. शक्तिभूतेśaktibhūteO you who are the powerf. voc. sg. of शक्तिभूता; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. सनातनिsanātaniO eternal onef. voc. sg. of सनातनी (feminine of सनातन ‘everlasting’)
  4. गुणाश्रयेguṇāśrayeO resting-place of the guṇasf. voc. sg. of गुणाश्रया; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. गुणमयेguṇamayeO you who consist of the guṇasf. voc. sg. of गुणमयी (taddhita: गुण + -maya ‘consisting of’, feminine -ī)
  6. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  7. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  8. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who are the power of creation, preservation and destruction, O eternal one, O resting-place of the guṇas, O you who consist of the guṇas, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishEternal one, you are the power that creates, sustains and destroys; the guṇas rest in you and are made of you: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocative epithets are turned into statements. ‘Are made of you’ renders गुणमये (‘consisting of the guṇas’) from the other side; a closer rendering is ‘you are made of the guṇas’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte → सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् + शक्तिभूते

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • गुणाश्रये guṇāśraye → गुण + आश्रये

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् sṛṣṭisthitivināśānām

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    सृष्टि + स्थिति + विनाश
    Vigraha
    सृष्टिः च स्थितिः च विनाशः च सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशाः, तेषाम् sṛṣṭiḥ ca sthitiḥ ca vināśaḥ ca sṛṣṭisthitivināśāḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of creation, continuance and destruction”
    In the verse
    of the three acts of the world: its making, its keeping and its ending
    Alternative
    The compound takes the gender of its last member (विनाश, m.) and the plural for three members.

  • शक्तिभूते śaktibhūte

    karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, existing as’, as in आधारभूता, 11.4)

    Members
    शक्ति + भूत
    Vigraha
    शक्तिः भूता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaktiḥ bhūtā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “being the power”
    In the verse
    you who are the very power behind creation, preservation and dissolution

  • गुणाश्रये guṇāśraye

    bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa)

    Members
    गुण + आश्रय
    Vigraha
    गुणानाम् आश्रयः यस्यां सा गुणाश्रया, तत्सम्बुद्धौ guṇānām āśrayaḥ yasyāṃ sā guṇāśrayā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she in whom the guṇas have their resting-place”
    In the verse
    the ground on which the three guṇas — sattva, rajas, tamas — rest
    Alternative
    Often translated as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, गुणानाम् आश्रयः ‘support of the guṇas’; but a tatpuruṣa keeps the gender of आश्रय (m.), and the feminine vocative -आश्रये shows the bahuvrīhi. The meaning is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • गुणमये guṇamaye

    -मय (mayaṭ) means ‘made of, consisting of’; its feminine is -मयी (vocative -मये). गुणाश्रये and गुणमये together say that she both supports the guṇas and is made of them.

  • सनातनि sanātani

    सनातन ‘eternal’ (from सना ‘always’) with feminine -ī; vocative with the ī shortened.

  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् sṛṣṭisthitivināśānām

    Genitive plural of an a-stem dvandva: -आनाम्. It depends on the first member of the next word (शक्ति-), a construction common in verse.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते सनातनि गुणाश्रये गुणमये नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte sanātani guṇāśraye guṇamaye nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Vocatives (शक्तिभूते, सनातनि, गुणाश्रये, गुणमये, नारायणि) followed by the refrain; the genitive dvandva depends on शक्ति-.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Goddess is the single power behind the three acts of the world — creation, continuance and destruction — and the eternal ground of the three guṇas, which both rest in her and make up her form.

Traditional interpretation

The verse gathers the teaching of Brahmā’s hymn: in creation she is creation, in preserving she is preservation, at the end she is dissolution (1.76–77), and she is the prakṛti that brings forth the three guṇas (1.78, गुणत्रयविभाविनी). The tradition links the three acts with the three guṇas (rajas, sattva, tamas) and with Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra, all of whom act through her power. The word सनातनी returns in 12.39.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has सृष्टिकर्त्री (264), संहारिणी (268), त्रिगुणात्मिका (763) and also गुणातीता (961), ‘beyond the guṇas’. The two texts are separate works; together the names show the Śākta view that she is both within and beyond the guṇas.

Verse 11.12

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे।सर्वस्यार्तिहरे देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१२⁠॥

śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe |sarvasyārtihare devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणेसर्वस्य · आर्तिहरे · देवि · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇesarvasya · ārtihare · devi · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणेśaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇeO you who are wholly intent on saving the wretched and afflicted who come to you for refugef. voc. sg. of -परायणा; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. सर्वस्यsarvasyaof all, of everyonepronominal adjective सर्व, m./n. gen. sg.; depends on आर्ति-
  3. आर्तिहरेārtihareO remover of distressf. voc. sg. of आर्तिहरा; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who are wholly intent on rescuing the wretched and afflicted who have come for refuge, O Goddess who take away the distress of all, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishYou live to rescue the helpless and suffering who come to you for shelter, and you take away the sorrow of all: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocatives are turned into statements; ‘live to rescue’ renders -परायणे, ‘wholly intent on’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe → शरण + आगत + दीन + आर्त + परित्राण + पर + अयने

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha, three times) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā (शरणागत, दीनार्त) and a + a → ā (परायण). The n of अयन becomes ṇ after the r of पर (परायण), by the rule of retroflexion inside a word.

  • सर्वस्यार्तिहरे sarvasyārtihare → सर्वस्य + आर्तिहरे

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe

    bahuvrīhi (-परायण ‘having … as highest goal’), containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (…परित्राण), a karmadhāraya (शरणागतदीनार्त), a dvitīyā tatpuruṣa (शरणागत) and a dvandva or karmadhāraya (दीनार्त)

    Members
    शरणागतदीनार्त + परित्राण + परायण
    Vigraha
    शरणम् आगताः शरणागताः; दीनाः च ते आर्ताः च दीनार्ताः; शरणागताः दीनार्ताः शरणागतदीनार्ताः; तेषां परित्राणम्; तत् परम् अयनं यस्याः सा शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaraṇam āgatāḥ śaraṇāgatāḥ; dīnāḥ ca te ārtāḥ ca dīnārtāḥ; śaraṇāgatāḥ dīnārtāḥ śaraṇāgatadīnārtāḥ; teṣāṃ paritrāṇam; tat param ayanaṃ yasyāḥ sā śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she whose highest aim is the rescue of the wretched and afflicted who have come for refuge”
    In the verse
    she who lives to save the helpless who turn to her
    Alternative
    शरणागतदीनार्त can also be a three-member dvandva, ‘those who have taken refuge, the wretched and the afflicted’ (three groups instead of one). The karmadhāraya, one group described three ways, is the more common reading; the sense differs little.

  • आर्तिहरे ārtihare

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    आर्ति + हर
    Vigraha
    आर्तिं हरति इति आर्तिहरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ ārtiṃ harati iti ārtiharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “remover of distress”
    In the verse
    as in 11.3 (प्रपन्नार्तिहरे); the genitive सर्वस्य outside the compound depends on its first member आर्ति
Morphology & anvaya

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe

    A single compound fills the whole first line (eight syllables). परायण at the end of a compound means ‘wholly devoted to, intent on’ (lit. ‘having … as the highest resort’). परित्राण is the action noun of परि + √त्रै ‘to rescue’.

  • आर्तिहरे ārtihare

    -हर is the agent of √हृ ‘to take away’ in compounds, feminine -हरा; vocative -हरे.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे, सर्वस्य आर्तिहरे देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe, sarvasya ārtihare devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two vocative phrases and the refrain; the first line is a single compound.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning
