The Goddess is greeted as the one whose whole purpose is to rescue those who come to her in helplessness and pain, and who takes away the distress of all.

Traditional interpretation

The verse returns to the first word of the hymn: प्रपन्नार्तिहरे (11.3) becomes सर्वस्यार्तिहरे, and the prapanna, the one who has come for refuge, becomes the शरणागत. The tradition values the verse as a prayer of surrender: it asks for nothing but names her as the refuge of the helpless.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the verse does not ask the one who comes for refuge to be worthy, only to be in need and to come. Refuge is described from the side of the one who gives it.

Verse 11.13

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि।कौशाम्भःक्षरिके देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१३⁠॥

haṃsayuktavimānasthe brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi |kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे · ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणिकौशाम्भःक्षरिके · देवि · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

haṃsayuktavimānasthe · brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇikauśāmbhaḥkṣarike · devi · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. हंसयुक्तविमानस्थेhaṃsayuktavimānastheO you who are seated in the celestial car yoked with swansf. voc. sg. of हंसयुक्तविमानस्था; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणिbrahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇiO you who bear the form of Brahmāṇīf. voc. sg. of ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणी; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. कौशाम्भःक्षरिकेkauśāmbhaḥkṣarikeO you who sprinkle water with kuśa grassf. voc. sg. of कौशाम्भःक्षरिका; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who are seated in the celestial car yoked with swans, who bear the form of Brahmāṇī, who sprinkle water with kuśa grass, O Goddess, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishRiding the swan-drawn chariot in the form of Brahmāṇī, sprinkling the water hallowed with kuśa grass: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Hallowed with kuśa’ interprets कौश- (‘of kuśa’): in rites, water is sprinkled with a bunch of kuśa grass.

Textual verification / uncertain: क्षरिका is a rare formation (from √क्षर् ‘to flow, let flow’); the sense ‘sprinkler of water’ is clear from the context and from 8.33, but the exact derivation is not certain.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike → कौश + अम्भः + क्षरिके

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga kept · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    a + a → ā (कौशाम्भः). The s-stem अम्भस् shows visarga before the unvoiced k of क्षरिका, kept as printed.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे haṃsayuktavimānasthe

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-स्थ ‘standing, seated’), containing a karmadhāraya (हंसयुक्तविमान) and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (हंसयुक्त)

    Members
    हंस + युक्त + विमान + स्थ
    Vigraha
    हंसैः युक्तम् हंसयुक्तम्; हंसयुक्तं च तत् विमानं च हंसयुक्तविमानम्; हंसयुक्तविमाने तिष्ठति इति हंसयुक्तविमानस्था, तत्सम्बुद्धौ haṃsaiḥ yuktam haṃsayuktam; haṃsayuktaṃ ca tat vimānaṃ ca haṃsayuktavimānam; haṃsayuktavimāne tiṣṭhati iti haṃsayuktavimānasthā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she who stands in the car yoked with swans”
    In the verse
    Brahmāṇī, riding the swan-drawn chariot of Brahmā

  • ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-धारिन् ‘bearing’) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    ब्रह्माणी + रूप + धारिन्
    Vigraha
    ब्रह्माण्याः रूपम् ब्रह्माणीरूपम्; तत् धारयति इति ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ brahmāṇyāḥ rūpam brahmāṇīrūpam; tat dhārayati iti brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “bearer of the form of Brahmāṇī”
    In the verse
    you who take the form of Brahmā’s Śakti

  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike

    upapada tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (कौशाम्भस्)

    Members
    कौश + अम्भस् + क्षरिका
    Vigraha
    कौशम् अम्भः कौशाम्भः; तत् क्षरयति इति कौशाम्भःक्षरिका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kauśam ambhaḥ kauśāmbhaḥ; tat kṣarayati iti kauśāmbhaḥkṣarikā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she who makes kuśa-water flow”
    In the verse
    you who sprinkle water consecrated with kuśa grass
Morphology & anvaya

  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike

    कौश is a taddhita from कुश (with vṛddhi): ‘of kuśa grass’, i.e. water sprinkled with or sanctified by kuśa. क्षरिका is an agent noun from √क्षर् ‘to flow’ (here ‘to make flow, sprinkle’) with the suffix -aka, feminine -ikā; the formation is uncommon, and the word is known chiefly from this verse.

  • ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi

    ब्रह्माणी is the feminine of ब्रह्मन् formed with -ānī (like इन्द्राणी, रुद्राणी): Brahmā’s consort or power. -धारिन् (√धृ with -in) ‘bearing’, feminine -धारिणी.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि कौशाम्भःक्षरिके देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) haṃsayuktavimānasthe brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Three descriptive vocatives naming Brahmāṇī, then देवि नारायणि and the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

With this verse the hymn turns to the Mātṛkās, the Śaktis of the gods who fought beside the Goddess against Raktabīja’s army (chapter 8): Brahmāṇī here, then Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Nārasiṃhī, Aindrī, Śivadūtī and Cāmuṇḍā (11.14–21). Each is greeted as a form of the one Nārāyaṇī.

Traditional interpretation

The picture is the one given in the battle: Brahmāṇī came on a car drawn by swans, with rosary and water-pot (8.15), and struck down the asuras by sprinkling water from her kamaṇḍalu (8.33), water made pure by mantra (9.38). The hymn turns her weapon into an image of blessing: the sprinkling of consecrated water.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the name ब्रह्माणी (821). The Mātṛkās are a shared Śākta group; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.14

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि।माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१४⁠॥

triśūlacandrāhidhare mahāvṛṣabhavāhini |māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे · महावृषभवाहिनिमाहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

triśūlacandrāhidhare · mahāvṛṣabhavāhinimāheśvarīsvarūpeṇa · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरेtriśūlacandrāhidhareO bearer of the trident, the moon and the serpentf. voc. sg. of त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरा; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. महावृषभवाहिनिmahāvṛṣabhavāhiniO rider of the great bullf. voc. sg. of महावृषभवाहिनी; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेणmāheśvarīsvarūpeṇain the form of Māheśvarīn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner (‘you who are in Māheśvarī’s form’)
  4. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  5. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  6. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O bearer of the trident, the moon and the serpent, O rider of the great bull, O Nārāyaṇī (who are) in the form of Māheśvarī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishBearing the trident, the crescent moon and the serpent, riding the great bull in the form of Māheśvarī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Crescent’ is added to ‘moon’ (चन्द्र), as Māheśvarī is described in 8.16 with the moon’s digit (चन्द्ररेखा).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे triśūlacandrāhidhare → त्रिशूल + चन्द्र + अहि + धरे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā (चन्द्र + अहि → चन्द्राहि).

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे triśūlacandrāhidhare

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-धर ‘bearing’) containing an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    त्रिशूल + चन्द्र + अहि + धर
    Vigraha
    त्रिशूलं च चन्द्रः च अहिः च त्रिशूलचन्द्राहयः; तान् धरति इति त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ triśūlaṃ ca candraḥ ca ahiḥ ca triśūlacandrāhayaḥ; tān dharati iti triśūlacandrāhidharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “bearer of trident, moon and serpent”
    In the verse
    you who carry Śiva’s emblems: the trident in hand, the crescent moon on the head, the serpent as ornament

  • महावृषभवाहिनि mahāvṛṣabhavāhini

    upapada tatpuruṣa (-वाहिन्, from the causative of √वह्: ‘one who has (it) carry her, rider’) containing a karmadhāraya (महावृषभ)

    Members
    महावृषभ + वाहिन्
    Vigraha
    महान् च असौ वृषभः च महावृषभः; महावृषभं वाहयति इति महावृषभवाहिनी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahān ca asau vṛṣabhaḥ ca mahāvṛṣabhaḥ; mahāvṛṣabhaṃ vāhayati iti mahāvṛṣabhavāhinī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she who rides the great bull”
    In the verse
    riding Nandin, Śiva’s bull
    Alternative
    Also analysed with the possessive suffix -in on वाह ‘mount’: ‘having the great bull as her mount’ (महावृषभः वाहः अस्याः अस्ति). The sense is the same.

  • माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    माहेश्वरी + स्वरूप
    Vigraha
    माहेश्वर्याः स्वरूपम्, तेन māheśvaryāḥ svarūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the form of Māheśvarī”
    In the verse
    as Māheśvarī, the Śakti of Maheśvara (Śiva)
Morphology & anvaya

  • माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa

    An instrumental, not a vocative: the verse has no vocative ‘O Māheśvarī’ but says ‘(you who are) in the form of Māheśvarī’. The refrain’s नारायणि is the only name in the address.

  • त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे triśūlacandrāhidhare

    -धर (√धृ with -a) ‘bearing, holding’, feminine -धरा; vocative -धरे.

  • महावृषभवाहिनि mahāvṛṣabhavāhini

    महत् ‘great’ becomes महा at the head of a karmadhāraya (a rule of compound formation, not of sandhi), as in महासुर (11.2).

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि, माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण (स्थिते) नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) triśūlacandrāhidhare mahāvṛṣabhavāhini, māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa (sthite) nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two vocatives, an instrumental of manner (with a participle such as स्थिते understood), and the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second of the Mātṛkās: Māheśvarī, Śiva’s Śakti, with his emblems — the trident, the crescent moon and the serpent — and riding his great bull.

Traditional interpretation

So she came to the battle in chapter 8: mounted on the bull, holding the trident, with great serpents for bracelets and the moon’s crescent as her ornament (8.16), and she slew asuras with her trident (8.34, 8.49). The hymn salutes the same figure as a form of Nārāyaṇī.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the name माहेश्वरी (208). The two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.15

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मयूरकुक्कुटवृते महाशक्तिधरेऽनघे।कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१५⁠॥

mayūrakukkuṭavṛte mahāśaktidhare'naghe |kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मयूरकुक्कुटवृते · महाशक्तिधरे · अनघेकौमारीरूपसंस्थाने · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

mayūrakukkuṭavṛte · mahāśaktidhare · anaghekaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. मयूरकुक्कुटवृतेmayūrakukkuṭavṛteO you who are attended by peacocks and cocksf. voc. sg. of मयूरकुक्कुटवृता; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. महाशक्तिधरेmahāśaktidhareO bearer of the great spearf. voc. sg. of महाशक्तिधरा; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. अनघेanagheO sinless onef. voc. sg. of अनघा; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. कौमारीरूपसंस्थानेkaumārīrūpasaṃsthāneO you whose shape is the form of Kaumārīf. voc. sg. of कौमारीरूपसंस्थाना; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who are attended by peacocks and cocks, bearer of the great spear, O sinless one, you whose shape is the form of Kaumārī, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishAttended by peacock and cock, holding the mighty spear, spotless one in the form of Kaumārī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • महाशक्तिधरेऽनघे mahāśaktidhare'naghe → महाशक्तिधरे + अनघे

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a of अनघे is elided (avagraha).

  • संस्थाने saṃsthāne → सम् + स्थाने

    anusvāra sandhi with a prefix · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    The m of the prefix सम् becomes anusvāra before s.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • मयूरकुक्कुटवृते mayūrakukkuṭavṛte

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    मयूर + कुक्कुट + वृत
    Vigraha
    मयूराः च कुक्कुटाः च मयूरकुक्कुटाः; तैः वृता मयूरकुक्कुटवृता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mayūrāḥ ca kukkuṭāḥ ca mayūrakukkuṭāḥ; taiḥ vṛtā mayūrakukkuṭavṛtā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “surrounded by peacocks and cocks”
    In the verse
    attended by the birds of Skanda: the peacock his mount, the cock his banner

  • महाशक्तिधरे mahāśaktidhare

    upapada tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (महाशक्ति)

    Members
    महाशक्ति + धर
    Vigraha
    महती चासौ शक्तिः च महाशक्तिः; तां धरति इति महाशक्तिधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī cāsau śaktiḥ ca mahāśaktiḥ; tāṃ dharati iti mahāśaktidharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “bearer of the great śakti”
    In the verse
    you who hold the great spear (शक्ति), Skanda’s weapon
    Alternative
    शक्ति means both ‘spear’ and ‘power’; ‘bearer of great power’ is possible, but Kaumārī’s weapon in the battle is the śakti-spear (8.17, शक्तिहस्ता), which fixes the first sense. The pun is natural in a hymn to the Śakti.

  • अनघे anaghe

    bahuvrīhi (nañ-bahuvrīhi)

    Members
    अन् (= न) + अघ
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते अघं यस्याः सा अनघा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ na vidyate aghaṃ yasyāḥ sā anaghā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she in whom there is no sin”
    In the verse
    O spotless, faultless one

  • कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कौमारीरूप)

    Members
    कौमारीरूप + संस्थान
    Vigraha
    कौमार्याः रूपम् कौमारीरूपम्; कौमारीरूपं संस्थानं यस्याः सा कौमारीरूपसंस्थाना, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kaumāryāḥ rūpam kaumārīrūpam; kaumārīrūpaṃ saṃsthānaṃ yasyāḥ sā kaumārīrūpasaṃsthānā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she whose configuration is the form of Kaumārī”
    In the verse
    you who appear as Kaumārī, the Śakti of Kumāra (Skanda)
    Alternative
    संस्थान can also be ‘abiding’: ‘she whose abiding is in Kaumārī’s form’ (कौमारीरूपे संस्थानं यस्याः). The sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • मयूरकुक्कुटवृते mayūrakukkuṭavṛte

    वृत is the past participle of √वृ (svādi, ‘to cover, surround’): ‘surrounded, attended’; feminine -वृता, vocative -वृते.

  • कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne

    कौमारी is a taddhita from कुमार (with vṛddhi and feminine -ī): ‘belonging to Kumāra’, i.e. Skanda’s Śakti.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) मयूरकुक्कुटवृते महाशक्तिधरे अनघे कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) mayūrakukkuṭavṛte mahāśaktidhare anaghe kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Four vocative epithets of Kaumārī, then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The third Mātṛkā: Kaumārī, the Śakti of Kumāra (Skanda), with his birds, the peacock and the cock, and his great spear.

Traditional interpretation

In the battle Kaumārī came spear in hand, riding a fine peacock, in the form of Guha (8.17), and slew asuras with her spear (8.34, 8.49; 9.38). The peacock is Skanda’s mount and the cock his banner in Purāṇic iconography.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: अनघा, ‘sinless’, stands in the middle of a warrior’s description. The force that fights here is pictured as clean of malice.

Verse 11.16

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे।प्रसीद वैष्णवीरूपे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१६⁠॥

śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe |prasīda vaiṣṇavīrūpe nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधेप्रसीद · वैष्णवीरूपे · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudheprasīda · vaiṣṇavīrūpe · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधेśaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudheO you who have taken up the supreme weapons — conch, discus, mace and the Śārṅga bowf. voc. sg. of -परमायुधा; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P, present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. वैष्णवीरूपेvaiṣṇavīrūpeO you who are in the form of Vaiṣṇavīf. voc. sg. of वैष्णवीरूपा; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  5. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  6. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who have taken up the supreme weapons — conch, discus, mace and Śārṅga — be gracious, O you in the form of Vaiṣṇavī, O Nārāyaṇī; homage be to you.

In readable EnglishHolding the conch, the discus, the mace and the bow Śārṅga, those supreme weapons, in the form of Vaiṣṇavī — be gracious, O Nārāyaṇī; salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • परमायुधे paramāyudhe → परम + आयुधे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe

    bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्ग) and a karmadhāraya (परमायुध)

    Members
    शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्ग + गृहीत + परमायुध
    Vigraha
    शङ्खः च चक्रं च गदा च शार्ङ्गं च शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गाणि; परमाणि आयुधानि परमायुधानि; शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गाणि (एव) परमायुधानि गृहीतानि यया सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaṅkhaḥ ca cakraṃ ca gadā ca śārṅgaṃ ca śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgāṇi; paramāṇi āyudhāni paramāyudhāni; śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgāṇi (eva) paramāyudhāni gṛhītāni yayā sā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she by whom the supreme weapons, conch, discus, mace and Śārṅga, have been taken up”
    In the verse
    you who carry Viṣṇu’s weapons
    Alternative
    The participle गृहीत stands between the two groups. The compound may also be divided as शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीता (‘who has taken conch … Śārṅga’, with the participle placed second, as is allowed in such bahuvrīhis) and परमायुधा (‘whose weapons are supreme’); the sense is the same.

  • वैष्णवीरूपे vaiṣṇavīrūpe

    bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa)

    Members
    वैष्णवी + रूप
    Vigraha
    वैष्णव्याः रूपं यस्याः सा वैष्णवीरूपा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ vaiṣṇavyāḥ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā vaiṣṇavīrūpā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she whose form is (that of) Vaiṣṇavī”
    In the verse
    you who appear as Vaiṣṇavī, Viṣṇu’s Śakti
Morphology & anvaya

  • शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe

    गृहीत is the past participle of √ग्रह् (kryādi, ‘to seize’): the ra of the root becomes ṛ (samprasāraṇa) and the connecting vowel is long (गृह् + ई + त). शार्ङ्ग, Viṣṇu’s bow, is a taddhita from शृङ्ग ‘horn’: ‘made of horn’.

  • प्रसीद prasīda

    As in 11.3: the only imperative in the refrain verses so far besides अस्तु, a direct prayer inside the salutation.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे वैष्णवीरूपे नारायणि, प्रसीद; ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe vaiṣṇavīrūpe nārāyaṇi, prasīda; te namaḥ astu |

A compound vocative fills the first line; the second adds a prayer (प्रसीद) to the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fourth Mātṛkā: Vaiṣṇavī, the Śakti of Viṣṇu, holding his four weapons — conch, discus, mace and the bow Śārṅga. In this verse alone of the Mātṛkā series the gods add a prayer, प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’.

Traditional interpretation

In the battle Vaiṣṇavī came seated on Garuḍa, with conch, discus, mace, Śārṅga bow and sword in her hands (8.18), and her discus struck and pierced Raktabīja (8.47–48). Here the name of the refrain and the name of the form coincide: Nārāyaṇī is greeted as Vaiṣṇavī, the power of Nārāyaṇa himself (compare 11.5, वैष्णवी शक्तिः).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names वैष्णवी (892) and विष्णुरूपिणी (893). The two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.17

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे।वराहरूपिणि शिवे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१७⁠॥

gṛhītogramahācakre daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare |varāharūpiṇi śive nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे · दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरेवराहरूपिणि · शिवे · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

gṛhītogramahācakre · daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdharevarāharūpiṇi · śive · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रेgṛhītogramahācakreO you who have taken up the fierce great discusf. voc. sg. of गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रा; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरेdaṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhareO you who lifted up the earth on your tuskf. voc. sg. of दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरा; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. वराहरूपिणिvarāharūpiṇiO you who have the form of a boarf. voc. sg. of वराहरूपिणी (वराहरूप + possessive -in, feminine -inī)
  4. शिवेśiveO auspicious onef. voc. sg. of शिवा
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who have taken up the fierce great discus, who raised the earth with your tusk, O you who have the form of a boar, O auspicious one, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishWielding the fierce great discus, raising the earth upon your tusk, auspicious one in the form of the Boar: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे gṛhītogramahācakre → गृहीत + उग्र + महाचक्रे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृत daṃṣṭroddhṛta → दंष्ट्रा + उद्धृत

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa); h after a stop · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); झयो होऽन्यतरस्याम् (8.4.62)

    ā + u → o. Inside उद्धृत (उद् + हृत) the h after d becomes dh.

  • वसुंधरे vasuṃdhare → वसुम् + धरे

    anusvāra sandhi inside a compound · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    वसुंधरा takes the augment m after वसु (वसुम्-धरा); the m is written as anusvāra before dh.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे gṛhītogramahācakre

    bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (उग्रमहाचक्र, itself containing the karmadhāraya महाचक्र)

    Members
    गृहीत + उग्र + महाचक्र
    Vigraha
    उग्रं च तत् महाचक्रं च उग्रमहाचक्रम्; गृहीतम् उग्रमहाचक्रं यया सा गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ ugraṃ ca tat mahācakraṃ ca ugramahācakram; gṛhītam ugramahācakraṃ yayā sā gṛhītogramahācakrā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she by whom the fierce great discus has been taken up”
    In the verse
    you who wield the terrible great discus

  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare

    bahuvrīhi (containing a tṛtīyā relation: दंष्ट्रया उद्धृता)

    Members
    दंष्ट्रा + उद्धृत + वसुंधरा
    Vigraha
    दंष्ट्रया उद्धृता वसुंधरा यया सा दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ daṃṣṭrayā uddhṛtā vasuṃdharā yayā sā daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she by whom the earth was raised with a tusk”
    In the verse
    you who, as the Boar, lifted the earth from the waters on your tusk

  • वसुंधरे vasuṃdhare

    upapada tatpuruṣa (with the augment m on the first member)

    Members
    वसु + धरा
    Vigraha
    वसूनि धारयति इति वसुंधरा vasūni dhārayati iti vasuṃdharā
    Literally
    “bearer of wealth”
    In the verse
    the earth

  • वराहरूपिणि varāharūpiṇi

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (वराहरूप) with the possessive suffix -in

    Members
    वराह + रूप (+ -इन्)
    Vigraha
    वराहस्य रूपम् वराहरूपम्; तत् अस्याः अस्ति इति वराहरूपिणी varāhasya rūpam varāharūpam; tat asyāḥ asti iti varāharūpiṇī
    Literally
    “she who has the boar’s form”
    In the verse
    Vārāhī, the Śakti of the Boar
Morphology & anvaya

  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare

    उद्धृत is the past participle of उद् + √हृ ‘to lift up’ (उद् + हृत, with the h aspirating the d: उद्धृत).

  • वराहरूपिणि varāharūpiṇi

    रूपिन् ‘having the form of’ (रूप + -in, possessive), feminine रूपिणी, vocative -रूपिणि.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे वराहरूपिणि शिवे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) gṛhītogramahācakre daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare varāharūpiṇi śive nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Four vocatives of Vārāhī, then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fifth Mātṛkā: Vārāhī, the Śakti of the Boar. She is greeted with the Boar’s great deed: lifting the earth on its tusk.

Traditional interpretation

In the battle the Śakti of Hari, who bears the form of the sacrificial Boar, came in a boar’s body (8.19) and fought beside the others (8.49; 9.39, where her snout crushes asuras). The hymn gives her the Boar’s own deed, the raising of the earth from the waters, so that the deed of Viṣṇu’s avatāra is praised as hers.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also knows Vārāhī as a warrior power: name 76, विशुक्रप्राणहरणवाराहीवीर्यनन्दिता, ‘delighted by the valour of Vārāhī, who took Viśukra’s life’. The battles are different and the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.18

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण हन्तुं दैत्यान् कृतोद्यमे।त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१८⁠॥

nṛsiṃharūpeṇogreṇa hantuṃ daityān kṛtodyame |trailokyatrāṇasahite nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

नृसिंहरूपेण · उग्रेण · हन्तुम् · दैत्यान् · कृतोद्यमेत्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

nṛsiṃharūpeṇa · ugreṇa · hantum · daityān · kṛtodyametrailokyatrāṇasahite · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. नृसिंहरूपेणnṛsiṃharūpeṇain the form of the Man-Lionn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
  2. उग्रेणugreṇafierce, terriblen. instr. sg. of उग्र; qualifies नृसिंहरूपेण
  3. हन्तुम्hantumto slayinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’)
  4. दैत्यान्daityānthe Daityasm. acc. pl. of दैत्य; object of हन्तुम्
  5. कृतोद्यमेkṛtodyameO you who set out with resolvef. voc. sg. of कृतोद्यमा; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहितेtrailokyatrāṇasahiteO you who are bent on protecting the three worldsf. voc. sg. of त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिता; compound (see Samāsa)
  7. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  8. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  9. अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

Translation

O you who, in the fierce form of the Man-Lion, set out with resolve to slay the Daityas, O you who are bent on protecting the three worlds, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

In readable EnglishIn the terrible form of the Man-Lion you rose to slay the demons, intent on saving the three worlds: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocatives are turned into statements.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण nṛsiṃharūpeṇogreṇa → नृसिंहरूपेण + उग्रेण

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • हन्तुं दैत्यान् hantuṃ daityān → हन्तुम् + दैत्यान्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • कृतोद्यमे kṛtodyame → कृत + उद्यमे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.8.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नृसिंहरूपेण nṛsiṃharūpeṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (नृसिंह is itself a karmadhāraya: ना एव सिंहः, or नृषु सिंहः — ‘the man who is a lion’)

    Members
    नृसिंह + रूप
    Vigraha
    नृसिंहस्य रूपम्, तेन nṛsiṃhasya rūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the form of the Man-Lion”
    In the verse
    as Nārasiṃhī, the Śakti of Viṣṇu’s Man-Lion

  • कृतोद्यमे kṛtodyame

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    कृत + उद्यम
    Vigraha
    कृतः उद्यमः यया सा कृतोद्यमा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kṛtaḥ udyamaḥ yayā sā kṛtodyamā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she by whom effort has been made”
    In the verse
    you who have set yourself to the task

  • त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते trailokyatrāṇasahite

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    त्रैलोक्य + त्राण + सहित
    Vigraha
    त्रैलोक्यस्य त्राणम् त्रैलोक्यत्राणम्; तेन सहिता त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ trailokyasya trāṇam trailokyatrāṇam; tena sahitā trailokyatrāṇasahitā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “joined with the protection of the three worlds”
    In the verse
    you who are wholly engaged in saving the three worlds
Morphology & anvaya

  • हन्तुम् hantum

    Infinitive of purpose (√हन् + tum): ‘in order to slay’, depending on कृतोद्यमे.

  • त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते trailokyatrāṇasahite

    त्रैलोक्य is a taddhita (with vṛddhi) from त्रिलोक ‘the three worlds’: ‘the triad of worlds’. सहित ‘accompanied by, joined with’ (सह + हित) here means ‘devoted to’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) उग्रेण नृसिंहरूपेण दैत्यान् हन्तुं कृतोद्यमे, त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) ugreṇa nṛsiṃharūpeṇa daityān hantuṃ kṛtodyame, trailokyatrāṇasahite nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

The first line is one vocative phrase built on कृतोद्यमे, with an instrumental of manner and an infinitive of purpose; the second adds a further vocative and the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The sixth Mātṛkā: Nārasiṃhī, the Śakti of the Man-Lion, in her fierce form, set on destroying the Daityas to protect the three worlds.

Traditional interpretation

In the battle Nārasiṃhī came in a body like Nṛsiṃha’s, scattering the stars with the toss of her mane (8.20), and roamed the field tearing asuras with her claws (8.37). As in 11.17, the purpose of Viṣṇu’s avatāra — to protect the worlds from the Daityas — is praised as the Goddess’s own.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has a related idea in name 80, कराङ्गुलिनखोत्पन्ननारायणदशाकृतिः, ‘from whose fingernails arose the ten forms of Nārāyaṇa’: there too Viṣṇu’s avatāras, the Boar and the Man-Lion among them, come from the Goddess. The two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.19

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

किरीटिनि महावज्रे सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले।वृत्रप्राणहरे चैन्द्रि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠१९⁠॥

kirīṭini mahāvajre sahasranayanojjvale |vṛtraprāṇahare caindri nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

किरीटिनि · महावज्रे · सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वलेवृत्रप्राणहरे · च · ऐन्द्रि · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

kirīṭini · mahāvajre · sahasranayanojjvalevṛtraprāṇahare · ca · aindri · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. किरीटिनिkirīṭiniO crowned one, wearer of the diademf. voc. sg. of किरीटिनी (feminine of किरीटिन् ‘having a diadem’, possessive taddhita -इन् on किरीट)
  2. महावज्रेmahāvajreO you of the great thunderboltf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वलेsahasranayanojjvaleO you who blaze with a thousand eyesf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. वृत्रप्राणहरेvṛtraprāṇahareO taker of Vṛtra’s lifef. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); adds the last epithet and the name ऐन्द्रि to the series
  6. ऐन्द्रिaindriO Aindrī, power of Indraf. voc. sg. of ऐन्द्री (taddhita अण् on इन्द्र ‘belonging to Indra’, feminine in -ī)
  7. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी (ī-stem, feminine of नारायण)
  8. नमःnamaḥhomage, salutationn. nom. sg. of नमस् (s-stem); subject of अस्तु
  9. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.; dative with नमः

Translation

O crowned one, O you of the great thunderbolt, O you who blaze with a thousand eyes, and O Aindrī, taker of Vṛtra’s life — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishCrowned, wielding the great thunderbolt, ablaze with a thousand eyes, Aindrī who took the life of Vṛtra — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले sahasranayanojjvale → सहस्रनयन + उज्ज्वले

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • चैन्द्रि caindri → च + ऐन्द्रि

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + ai → ai: the vowel of the particle disappears into the ai. Read च ऐन्द्रि, not a form of इन्द्र.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: the visarga (as ru) becomes u, a + u gives o, and the initial a of अस्तु is absorbed into the o (avagraha). The same junction closes every stanza of the refrain (11.8–23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महावज्रे mahāvajre

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    महत् + वज्र
    Vigraha
    महत् वज्रं यस्याः सा (महावज्रा), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahat vajraṃ yasyāḥ sā (mahāvajrā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she whose thunderbolt is great”
    In the verse
    Aindrī with Indra’s vajra in hand (8.21 वज्रहस्ता)

  • सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले sahasranayanojjvale

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a dvigu (सहस्रनयन: a numeral compound standing before a further member)

    Members
    सहस्रनयन + उज्ज्वल
    Vigraha
    सहस्रं नयनानि सहस्रनयनानि; सहस्रनयनैः उज्ज्वला sahasraṃ nayanāni sahasranayanāni; sahasranayanaiḥ ujjvalā
    Literally
    “blazing with a thousand eyes”
    In the verse
    bearing Indra’s thousand eyes (8.21 सहस्रनयना)
    Alternative
    Also a bahuvrīhi, सहस्रं नयनानि उज्ज्वलानि यस्याः सा ‘whose thousand eyes blaze’. The sense is the same; the tatpuruṣa keeps उज्ज्वला as the word that describes her.

  • वृत्रप्राणहरे vṛtraprāṇahare

    upapada tatpuruṣa (हर = √हृ with the agent affix a after its object) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (वृत्रप्राण)

    Members
    वृत्र + प्राण + हर
    Vigraha
    वृत्रस्य प्राणाः वृत्रप्राणाः; वृत्रप्राणान् हरति इति वृत्रप्राणहरा vṛtrasya prāṇāḥ vṛtraprāṇāḥ; vṛtraprāṇān harati iti vṛtraprāṇaharā
    Literally
    “she who takes away the life-breaths of Vṛtra”
    In the verse
    slayer of Vṛtra — Indra’s great deed, ascribed to his power
Morphology & anvaya

  • किरीटिनि kirīṭini

    किरीट ‘diadem’ + the possessive -इन् gives किरीटिन् ‘crowned’; the feminine adds -ī (किरीटिनी), and the vocative shortens it: किरीटिनि.

  • ऐन्द्रि aindri

    Taddhita: इन्द्र + अ (अण्) with vṛddhi of the first vowel (इ → ऐ) gives ऐन्द्र ‘of Indra’; feminine ऐन्द्री, vocative ऐन्द्रि. वैष्णवी, माहेश्वरी and कौमारी in the stanzas before are formed the same way.

  • अस्तु astu

    Imperative third singular of √अस्: with नमः ‘let there be homage’; the dative ते names the one honoured.

Anvaya · prose order

हे किरीटिनि महावज्रे सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले वृत्रप्राणहरे च ऐन्द्रि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he kirīṭini mahāvajre sahasranayanojjvale vṛtraprāṇahare ca aindri nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

A chain of vocatives ending in the name ऐन्द्रि; च adds the last epithet. The refrain नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते is the main clause.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The seventh of the Mātṛkā stanzas (11.13–21). The Goddess is praised as Aindrī, Indra’s power, as she came to the battlefield in 8.21 (वज्रहस्ता … गजराजोपरि स्थिता … सहस्रनयना यथा शक्रः) and struck Raktabīja with her thunderbolt (8.42, 8.46). Every attribute is Indra’s: the diadem, the vajra, the thousand eyes, the killing of Vṛtra.

Traditional interpretation

In 8.13–14 the powers of the gods leave their bodies with the same form, ornaments and mounts as the gods themselves. The hymn names each Mātṛkā by her god’s deed: Vārāhī lifts the earth on her tusk (11.17), Nārasiṃhī strikes the daityas (11.18), Aindrī slays Vṛtra. The Vedic victory of Indra is credited to the power through which he won it.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name वज्रिणी (name 944), ‘she who holds the thunderbolt’. The image is shared; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.20

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण हतदैत्यमहाबले।घोररूपे महारावे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२०⁠॥

śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa hatadaityamahābale |ghorarūpe mahārāve nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण · हतदैत्यमहाबलेघोररूपे · महारावे · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa · hatadaityamahābaleghorarūpe · mahārāve · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. शिवदूतीस्वरूपेणśivadūtīsvarūpeṇain the form of Śivadūtīn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner, ‘in the form of’
  2. हतदैत्यमहाबलेhatadaityamahābaleO you by whom the great army of the daityas was slainf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. घोररूपेghorarūpeO you of terrible formf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. महारावेmahārāveO you of the mighty roarf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

(You who are) in the form of Śivadūtī, O you by whom the great army of the daityas was slain, O you of terrible form, O you of the mighty roar — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishAppearing as Śivadūtī, destroyer of the daityas’ mighty host, terrible of form, loud of roar — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Appearing’ supplies the participle implied by the instrumental of form.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa

    karmadhāraya (the first member names the form)

    Members
    शिवदूती + स्वरूप
    Vigraha
    शिवदूती एव स्वरूपम्, तेन śivadūtī eva svarūpam, tena
    Literally
    “by the own-form that is Śivadūtī”
    In the verse
    as Śivadūtī, the form that sent Śiva as messenger (8.24–28)

  • शिवदूती śivadūtī

    bahuvrīhi (feminine in -ī)

    Members
    शिव + दूत
    Vigraha
    शिवः दूतः यस्याः सा śivaḥ dūtaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose messenger is Śiva”
    In the verse
    the name given in 8.28: यतो नियुक्तो दौत्येन तया देव्या शिवः स्वयम्

  • हतदैत्यमहाबले hatadaityamahābale

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दैत्यमहाबल) and a karmadhāraya (महाबल); महत् takes the form महा- before the noun it qualifies

    Members
    हत + दैत्य + महाबल
    Vigraha
    महत् बलं महाबलम्; दैत्यानां महाबलं दैत्यमहाबलम्; हतं दैत्यमहाबलं यया सा mahat balaṃ mahābalam; daityānāṃ mahābalaṃ daityamahābalam; hataṃ daityamahābalaṃ yayā sā
    Literally
    “she by whom the great army of the daityas was slain”
    In the verse
    destroyer of the asura host (9.23, 9.37, 9.41)
    Alternative
    बल can also mean ‘strength’: हताः दैत्याः यया सा च महाबला च, ‘slayer of the daityas, of great might’. The compound is printed as one word, and बल ‘army’ with हत ‘slain’ reads more naturally; both senses fit the narrative.

  • घोररूपे ghorarūpe

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    घोर + रूप
    Vigraha
    घोरं रूपं यस्याः सा ghoraṃ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose form is terrible”
    In the verse
    fearsome to behold (8.23 अतिभीषणा … अत्युग्रा)

  • महारावे mahārāve

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    महत् + राव
    Vigraha
    महान् रावः यस्याः सा mahān rāvaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose roar is great”
    In the verse
    the howl of a hundred jackals (8.23 शिवाशतनिनादिनी) and her laughter (9.23)
Morphology & anvaya

  • शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa

    Instrumental of manner among vocatives, as माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण in 11.14: ‘(you who appear) in the form of Śivadūtī’.

  • हतदैत्यमहाबले hatadaityamahābale

    हत is the past participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’); as the first member of a bahuvrīhi it gives the passive sense ‘by whom … was slain’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण (स्थिते) हतदैत्यमहाबले घोररूपे महारावे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa (sthite) hatadaityamahābale ghorarūpe mahārāve nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

The instrumental phrase describes the manner of her appearance; three bahuvrīhi vocatives follow; then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The eighth Mātṛkā stanza praises Śivadūtī, the fierce power that sprang from Caṇḍikā’s own body, howling like a hundred jackals (8.23). She sent Śiva as her envoy to Śumbha and Niśumbha (8.24–27) and so was named Śivadūtī, ‘she who has Śiva as messenger’ (8.28). Her laughter terrified the asuras (8.38, 9.23) and she devoured them (9.37, 9.41).

Traditional interpretation

Unlike the six Mātṛkās before her, Śivadūtī is no god’s power but the Devī’s own: she comes from Caṇḍikā’s body, not from a god’s. The stanza thus moves from the gods’ śaktis back to the Goddess herself, completed by Cāmuṇḍā in 11.21.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name शिवदूती (name 405). The name and its sense are shared Śākta usage; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 11.21

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

दंष्ट्राकरालवदने शिरोमालाविभूषणे।चामुण्डे मुण्डमथने नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२१⁠॥

daṃṣṭrākarālavadane śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe |cāmuṇḍe muṇḍamathane nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

दंष्ट्राकरालवदने · शिरोमालाविभूषणेचामुण्डे · मुण्डमथने · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

daṃṣṭrākarālavadane · śiromālāvibhūṣaṇecāmuṇḍe · muṇḍamathane · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. दंष्ट्राकरालवदनेdaṃṣṭrākarālavadaneO you whose face is dreadful with tusksf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. शिरोमालाविभूषणेśiromālāvibhūṣaṇeO you adorned with a garland of headsf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. चामुण्डेcāmuṇḍeO Cāmuṇḍāf. voc. sg. of चामुण्डा (proper name)
  4. मुण्डमथनेmuṇḍamathaneO crusher of Muṇḍaf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  6. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  7. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

O you whose face is dreadful with tusks, O you whose ornament is a garland of heads, O Cāmuṇḍā, O crusher of Muṇḍa — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishFanged and fearful of face, garlanded with heads, Cāmuṇḍā, slayer of Muṇḍa — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शिरोमाला śiromālā → शिरस् + माला

    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    The final s of the stem शिरस् becomes ru, then u before the voiced m, and a + u gives o: शिरोमाला.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दंष्ट्राकरालवदने daṃṣṭrākarālavadane

    bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (दंष्ट्राकराल)

    Members
    दंष्ट्राकराल + वदन
    Vigraha
    दंष्ट्राभिः करालम् दंष्ट्राकरालम्; दंष्ट्राकरालं वदनं यस्याः सा daṃṣṭrābhiḥ karālam daṃṣṭrākarālam; daṃṣṭrākarālaṃ vadanaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose face is frightful with tusks”
    In the verse
    Kālī’s dreadful, gaping mouth (7.6 करालवदना, 7.8 अतिविस्तारवदना)

  • शिरोमालाविभूषणे śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (शिरोमाला)

    Members
    शिरोमाला + विभूषण
    Vigraha
    शिरसां माला शिरोमाला; शिरोमाला विभूषणं यस्याः सा śirasāṃ mālā śiromālā; śiromālā vibhūṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose ornament is a garland of heads”
    In the verse
    wearing a garland of heads, as Kālī appears in 7.7 (नरमालाविभूषणा)

  • मुण्डमथने muṇḍamathane

    upapada tatpuruṣa (मथन = √मथ् with the agent affix -अन)

    Members
    मुण्ड + मथन
    Vigraha
    मुण्डं मथ्नाति इति मुण्डमथना muṇḍaṃ mathnāti iti muṇḍamathanā
    Literally
    “she who crushes Muṇḍa”
    In the verse
    slayer of the general Muṇḍa (7.21)
    Alternative
    Also a bahuvrīhi: मुण्डस्य मथनं यया सा, ‘she by whom the crushing of Muṇḍa (was done)’; or with मुण्ड in its common-noun sense ‘shaven head’, ‘crusher of heads’. The proper name fits the pairing with चामुण्डे.
Morphology & anvaya

  • चामुण्डे cāmuṇḍe

    Vocative of चामुण्डा. The text gives the name’s origin in 7.27: because Kālī brought the heads of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, she would be known as Cāmuṇḍā (यस्माच्चण्डं च मुण्डं च … चामुण्डेति ततो लोके ख्याता देवि भविष्यसि). It is a name, not a regular compound.

  • मुण्डमथने muṇḍamathane

    मथन is an agent noun from √मथ् / मन्थ् (‘to churn, crush’) in -अन; feminine मथना, vocative मथने.

Anvaya · prose order

हे दंष्ट्राकरालवदने शिरोमालाविभूषणे मुण्डमथने चामुण्डे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he daṃṣṭrākarālavadane śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe muṇḍamathane cāmuṇḍe nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two bahuvrīhi vocatives describing her appearance, the name with its explanatory epithet, then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last Mātṛkā stanza praises Cāmuṇḍā, the Kālī who sprang from Ambikā’s brow (7.5–7), killed Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (7.20–21) and received her name for it (7.27). In the Raktabīja battle she drank the demon’s blood so that no new asura could rise from it (8.53–61). The epithets are those of her first appearance: a gaping fanged mouth and a garland of heads.

Traditional interpretation

With Cāmuṇḍā the series of nine forms (Brahmāṇī to Cāmuṇḍā, 11.13–21) is complete. In the Śākta tradition these forms are worshipped as the Mātṛkās, the ‘Mothers’, with Cāmuṇḍā often counted among them; the hymn presents them all as forms of one Nārāyaṇī.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names her चण्डमुण्डासुरनिषूदिनी (name 756), ‘destroyer of the asuras Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa’ — the same story in a different text.

Verse 11.22

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

लक्ष्मि लज्जे महाविद्ये श्रद्धे पुष्टिस्वधे ध्रुवे।महारात्रि महाऽविद्ये नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२२⁠॥

lakṣmi lajje mahāvidye śraddhe puṣṭisvadhe dhruve |mahārātri mahā'vidye nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

लक्ष्मि · लज्जे · महाविद्ये · श्रद्धे · पुष्टि · स्वधे · ध्रुवेमहारात्रि · महाऽविद्ये · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

lakṣmi · lajje · mahāvidye · śraddhe · puṣṭi · svadhe · dhruvemahārātri · mahā'vidye · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. लक्ष्मिlakṣmiO Lakṣmī, fortunef. voc. sg. of लक्ष्मी (ī-stem; nom. लक्ष्मीः)
  2. लज्जेlajjeO modestyf. voc. sg. of लज्जा
  3. महाविद्येmahāvidyeO great knowledgef. voc. sg. of महाविद्या; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. श्रद्धेśraddheO faithf. voc. sg. of श्रद्धा
  5. पुष्टिpuṣṭiO nourishment, increasef. voc. sg. of पुष्टि (i-stem); the regular vocative is पुष्टे — here the stem form, as भूति in 11.23 (see flags)
  6. स्वधेsvadheO Svadhā, the offering to the ancestorsf. voc. sg. of स्वधा
  7. ध्रुवेdhruveO steadfast onef. voc. sg. of ध्रुवा (feminine of the adjective ध्रुव ‘fixed, constant’)
  8. महारात्रिmahārātriO great nightf. voc. sg. of महारात्री (ī-stem by-form of रात्रि); compound (see Samāsa)
  9. महाऽविद्येmahā'vidyeO great nesciencef. voc. sg. of the compound महा + अविद्या (written महाऽविद्या); compound (see Samāsa)
  10. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  11. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  12. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  13. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

O Lakṣmī, O Modesty, O Great Knowledge, O Faith, O Nourishment, O Svadhā, O Steadfast One, O Great Night, O Great Nescience — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishFortune, Modesty, Great Knowledge, Faith, Nourishment, Svadhā, the Steadfast; Great Night and Great Ignorance — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

Textual verification / uncertain: Variant (recorded): महाऽविद्ये (Gita Press, kept) against महाविद्ये (GRETIL, Freund) — two readings, ‘great nescience’ or ‘great knowledge’; both traditional. The digital text prints पुष्टिस्वधे as one word; it is taken here as two vocatives (पुष्टि, a stem-form vocative for पुष्टे, and स्वधे), since a dvandva of two names would not stand in the vocative singular. If the joining is deliberate, the alternative is an irregular compound vocative of ‘Puṣṭi-Svadhā’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • महाऽविद्ये mahā'vidye → महा + अविद्ये

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā. The Gita Press writes an avagraha to show that an a has merged into the ā: mahā + avidyā, not mahāvidyā. The sound is the same either way (see the variant).

  • पुष्टिस्वधे puṣṭisvadhe → पुष्टि + स्वधे

    no sandhi (vowel before consonant) · —

    The two vocatives are printed without a space; nothing changes at the junction.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महाविद्ये mahāvidye

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + विद्या
    Vigraha
    महती विद्या (महाविद्या), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī vidyā (mahāvidyā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “great knowledge”
    In the verse
    the supreme knowledge that frees (cf. 1.57 सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता)

  • महारात्रि mahārātri

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + रात्री
    Vigraha
    महती रात्री (महारात्री), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī rātrī (mahārātrī), tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “great night”
    In the verse
    the night of dissolution, as in Brahmā’s hymn (1.79 कालरात्रिर्महारात्रिर्मोहरात्रिश्च दारुणा)

  • महाऽविद्ये mahā'vidye

    karmadhāraya containing a nañ tatpuruṣa (अविद्या)

    Members
    महत् + अविद्या
    Vigraha
    महती अविद्या (महाविद्या), तत्सम्बुद्धौ; अविद्या = न विद्या mahatī avidyā (mahāvidyā), tatsambuddhau; avidyā = na vidyā
    Literally
    “great nescience”
    In the verse
    the ignorance that binds, also her power (cf. 1.58 संसारबन्धहेतुश्च सैव)
    Alternative
    Without the avagraha (GRETIL, Freund) the word is महाविद्ये again, ‘great knowledge’, repeating the first line. The Gita Press reading pairs it with महारात्रि and balances महाविद्ये in the first line.
Morphology & anvaya

  • लक्ष्मि lakṣmi

    लक्ष्मी is an ī-stem of the type that keeps visarga in the nominative (लक्ष्मीः); the vocative shortens the ī: लक्ष्मि. Contrast the ā-stems of the line, whose vocative ends in -e (लज्जे, श्रद्धे, स्वधे).

  • पुष्टि puṣṭi

    The i-stem vocative should be पुष्टे (guṇa of the final). The printed पुष्टि (written together with स्वधे) keeps the stem form, as भूति does in 11.23; Purāṇic verse allows such vocatives. The metre is unaffected.

  • महारात्रि mahārātri

    रात्रि has a by-form रात्री (ī-stem); the vocative of महारात्री is महारात्रि. (Of the i-stem, used in 1.79, it would be महारात्रे.)

Anvaya · prose order

हे लक्ष्मि लज्जे महाविद्ये श्रद्धे पुष्टि स्वधे ध्रुवे महारात्रि महाऽविद्ये नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he lakṣmi lajje mahāvidye śraddhe puṣṭi svadhe dhruve mahārātri mahā'vidye nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Nine names in the vocative, each a power the Goddess is; then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

After the nine warrior forms the hymn turns to abstract powers. Most of these names were already given her by Brahmā (1.73 स्वधा, 1.77 महाविद्या, 1.79 महारात्रिः, 1.80 लज्जा पुष्टिः) and by the gods (4.5 श्रीः … श्रद्धा … लज्जा; 5.44–58 लज्जा, श्रद्धा, लक्ष्मी).

Direct textual meaning

The line ends on a pair of opposites: महाविद्या ‘great knowledge’ at the start of the first line, महाऽविद्या ‘great nescience’ at the end of the second. The Gita Press marks the second with an avagraha; other witnesses (GRETIL, Freund) read महाविद्ये twice. Both readings are traditional (corrections.json, variant).

Traditional interpretation

The Gita Press reading states a teaching the text gives from the start: the same Mahāmāyā is the knowledge that liberates and the power that binds (1.57–58 सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता … संसारबन्धहेतुश्च सैव). Knowledge and ignorance are not two goddesses.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has महाविद्या (name 584) and विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी (name 402), ‘whose form is both knowledge and ignorance’, and also लज्जा (740), पुष्टिः (444) and स्वधा (536). The pairing of knowledge and nescience is a shared Śākta idea; the texts are separate works.

Verse 11.23

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मेधे सरस्वति वरे भूति बाभ्रवि तामसि।नियते त्वं प्रसीदेशे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२३⁠॥

medhe sarasvati vare bhūti bābhravi tāmasi |niyate tvaṃ prasīdeśe nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मेधे · सरस्वति · वरे · भूति · बाभ्रवि · तामसिनियते · त्वम् · प्रसीद · ईशे · नारायणि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

medhe · sarasvati · vare · bhūti · bābhravi · tāmasiniyate · tvam · prasīda · īśe · nārāyaṇi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. मेधेmedheO Medhā, intelligencef. voc. sg. of मेधा
  2. सरस्वतिsarasvatiO Sarasvatīf. voc. sg. of सरस्वती (ī-stem)
  3. वरेvareO excellent onef. voc. sg. of वरा (feminine of the adjective वर ‘best, choice’)
  4. भूतिbhūtiO prosperity, powerf. voc. sg. of भूति (i-stem); the regular vocative is भूते — here the stem form (see Morph)
  5. बाभ्रविbābhraviO Bābhravī, the tawny onef. voc. sg. of बाभ्रवी (taddhita on बभ्रु ‘tawny’, feminine in -ī)
  6. तामसिtāmasiO Tāmasī, you of the nature of tamasf. voc. sg. of तामसी (taddhita अण् on तमस्, feminine in -ī)
  7. नियतेniyateO Niyati, ordaining powerf. voc. sg. of नियति (i-stem, regular vocative); see Morph for the alternative
  8. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of प्रसीद
  9. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sit, settle’; present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. ईशेīśeO sovereign Ladyf. voc. sg. of ईशा (feminine of ईश ‘ruler’)
  11. नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
  12. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  13. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  14. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

O Medhā, O Sarasvatī, O excellent one, O Prosperity, O Bābhravī, O Tāmasī, O Niyati — you, be gracious, O sovereign Lady; O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishIntelligence, Sarasvatī, most excellent, Prosperity, tawny Bābhravī, dark Tāmasī, Ordaining Power — be gracious, sovereign Lady. Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

बाभ्रवि and तामसि are rendered with their literal sense (‘tawny’, ‘dark’) as well as the name.

Textual verification / uncertain: भूति (and पुष्टि in 11.22) are stem-form vocatives, not the regular भूते; kept as printed. नियते may be read as Niyati ‘destiny’ (chosen) or नियता ‘the self-restrained’. बाभ्रवी: lexically ‘tawny’ (from बभ्रु) or ‘belonging to Babhru’; the specific mythological reference is not stated in the text.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रसीदेशे prasīdeśe → प्रसीद + ईशे

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e. The imperative and the vocative are written as one.

  • त्वं प्रसीदेशे tvaṃ prasīdeśe → त्वम् + प्रसीद

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before the consonant p becomes anusvāra.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • भूति bhūti

    The i-stem vocative would be भूते. The text has भूति, a stem-form vocative found in Purāṇic verse (compare पुष्टि in 11.22). The syllable count is the same either way.

  • नियते niyate

    Taken as the regular vocative of नियति (नि + √यम्, kṛt -ति) ‘restraint, fixed order, destiny’, which fits a list of powers (मेधा, भूति). It can also be the vocative of नियता (kta of नि + √यम्) ‘self-restrained one’; the form is the same.

  • प्रसीद prasīda

    Imperative of प्र + √सद्: the root takes the present stem सीद- (सद् → सीद), and with प्र means ‘be pleased, be gracious’.

  • तामसि tāmasi

    Taddhita: तमस् + अ with vṛddhi (त → ता) gives तामस ‘belonging to tamas’; feminine तामसी, vocative तामसि.

Anvaya · prose order

हे मेधे सरस्वति वरे भूति बाभ्रवि तामसि नियते ईशे, त्वं प्रसीद। (हे) नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he medhe sarasvati vare bhūti bābhravi tāmasi niyate īśe, tvaṃ prasīda | (he) nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Seven vocatives, a prayer (त्वं प्रसीद) with an eighth vocative (ईशे) set inside it, then the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The list of powers continues: intellect and speech (मेधा, सरस्वती), prosperity (भूति), and two names of her darker nature (बाभ्रवी, तामसी), closing with Niyati, the power that fixes the order of things. In the middle of the list the gods break into a direct prayer: त्वं प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’.

Traditional interpretation

Medhā and Sarasvatī recall the gods’ hymn of chapter 4 (4.11 मेधासि देवि विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा). Tāmasī is the Goddess as tamas, the power of sleep and dissolution: Brahmā’s hymn calls her the bearer of the three guṇas (1.78 गुणत्रयविभाविनी), and in the first carita she is Viṣṇu’s yoganidrā, his sleep (1.66, 1.69–71).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has मेधा (name 538) and सरस्वती (name 704). Shared names; separate works.

Verse 11.24

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सर्वस्वरूपे सर्वेशे सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते।भयेभ्यस्त्राहि नो देवि दुर्गे देवि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२४⁠॥

sarvasvarūpe sarveśe sarvaśaktisamanvite |bhayebhyastrāhi no devi durge devi namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सर्वस्वरूपे · सर्वेशे · सर्वशक्तिसमन्वितेभयेभ्यः · त्राहि · नः · देवि · दुर्गे · देवि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

sarvasvarūpe · sarveśe · sarvaśaktisamanvitebhayebhyaḥ · trāhi · naḥ · devi · durge · devi · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. सर्वस्वरूपेsarvasvarūpeO you whose own form is everythingf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. सर्वेशेsarveśeO Lady of allf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. सर्वशक्तिसमन्वितेsarvaśaktisamanviteO you endowed with every powerf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. भयेभ्यःbhayebhyaḥfrom fears, from dangersn. abl. pl. of भय; ablative of what one is protected from
  5. त्राहिtrāhiprotect, save√त्रा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’; by-form of √त्रै, bhvādi 1Ā), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. नःnaḥusfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of त्राहि
  7. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  8. दुर्गेdurgeO Durgāf. voc. sg. of दुर्गा
  9. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी; repeated with the name
  10. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  11. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

O you whose form is all, O Lady of all, O you endowed with every power — protect us from fears, O Devī; O Durgā, O Devī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishForm of all things, Lady of all, possessed of every power — protect us from every fear, Devī. Durgā, Devī, salutation to you.

‘every fear’ renders the plural भयेभ्यः.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वेशे sarveśe → सर्व + ईशे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e.

  • भयेभ्यस्त्राहि bhayebhyastrāhi → भयेभ्यः + त्राहि

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • नो देवि no devi → नः + देवि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वस्वरूपे sarvasvarūpe

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    सर्व + स्वरूप
    Vigraha
    सर्वं स्वरूपं यस्याः सा (सर्वस्वरूपा), तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarvaṃ svarūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā (sarvasvarūpā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she whose own form is all”
    In the verse
    the Goddess who is everything that exists (cf. 1.82 सदसद्वाखिलात्मिके)
    Alternative
    As a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वस्य स्वरूपम्, ‘the true nature of all’) the compound would be neuter, with the vocative सर्वस्वरूप; the feminine vocative -e shows the bahuvrīhi.

  • सर्वेशे sarveśe

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    सर्व + ईशा
    Vigraha
    सर्वस्य ईशा sarvasya īśā
    Literally
    “ruler of all”
    In the verse
    sovereign over everything

  • सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते sarvaśaktisamanvite

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (सर्वशक्ति)

    Members
    सर्वशक्ति + समन्वित
    Vigraha
    सर्वाः शक्तयः सर्वशक्तयः; सर्वशक्तिभिः समन्विता sarvāḥ śaktayaḥ sarvaśaktayaḥ; sarvaśaktibhiḥ samanvitā
    Literally
    “endowed with all powers”
    In the verse
    possessing every power — the gods’ powers gathered in her (2.10–19, 8.12–21)
Morphology & anvaya

  • त्राहि trāhi

    Imperative second singular with the ending -हि, as in पाहि and याहि: √त्रा is the athematic by-form of the root that also appears as त्रायते, त्रायस्व.

  • भयेभ्यः bhayebhyaḥ

    Ablative plural: with verbs of protecting, the danger one is protected from stands in the ablative.

  • सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते sarvaśaktisamanvite

    समन्वित is the past participle (kta) of सम् + अनु + √इ (adādi 2P ‘to go’): ‘gone along with’, hence ‘accompanied by, endowed with’, taking the instrumental.

Anvaya · prose order

हे सर्वस्वरूपे सर्वेशे सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते देवि, नः भयेभ्यः त्राहि। हे दुर्गे देवि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he sarvasvarūpe sarveśe sarvaśaktisamanvite devi, naḥ bhayebhyaḥ trāhi | he durge devi, te namaḥ astu |

Three bahuvrīhi and tatpuruṣa vocatives, then a prayer (त्राहि नः) and a new refrain addressed to Durgā.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The refrain changes. After sixteen stanzas ending नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते (11.8–23), the gods address her as Durgā and ask for protection from fear. From here to 11.28 each stanza is a prayer for protection, as at the end of the gods’ hymn in chapter 4 (4.24–27 शूलेन पाहि नो देवि …).

Traditional interpretation

The name Durgā is explained later in this chapter: the Devī foretells that she will slay the asura Durgama and be known as Durgā Devī (11.49–50). Traditionally this stanza is among the most widely recited of the Saptaśatī, used on its own as a prayer for freedom from fear.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has दुर्गा (name 190), सर्वेश्वरी (name 202), सर्वशक्तिमयी (name 199) and भयापहा (name 121), ‘remover of fear’. Shared names and ideas; the works are separate.

Verse 11.25

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एतत्ते वदनं सौम्यं लोचनत्रयभूषितम्।पातु नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः कात्यायनि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२५⁠॥

etatte vadanaṃ saumyaṃ locanatrayabhūṣitam |pātu naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ kātyāyani namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एतत् · ते · वदनम् · सौम्यम् · लोचनत्रयभूषितम्पातु · नः · सर्वभीतिभ्यः · कात्यायनि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

etat · te · vadanam · saumyam · locanatrayabhūṣitampātu · naḥ · sarvabhītibhyaḥ · kātyāyani · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies वदनम्
  2. तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  3. वदनम्vadanamfacen. nom. sg. of वदन; subject of पातु
  4. सौम्यम्saumyamgentle, benignn. nom. sg. of सौम्य (taddhita from सोम ‘moon’: ‘moon-like, mild’); qualifies वदनम्
  5. लोचनत्रयभूषितम्locanatrayabhūṣitamadorned with three eyesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वदनम्
  6. पातुpātumay (it) protect√पा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. नःnaḥusअस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of पातु
  8. सर्वभीतिभ्यःsarvabhītibhyaḥfrom all fearsf. abl. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  9. कात्यायनिkātyāyaniO Kātyāyanīf. voc. sg. of कात्यायनी (patronymic feminine, ‘of the Kātyāyana line’)
  10. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  11. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

May this gentle face of yours, adorned with three eyes, protect us from all fears; O Kātyāyanī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishMay this gentle face of yours, graced with three eyes, protect us from every fear. Kātyāyanī, salutation to you.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एतत्ते etatte → एतत् + ते

    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)

    Final t before the unvoiced t stays t; the two words are only written together.

  • वदनं सौम्यं लोचन vadanaṃ saumyaṃ locana → वदनम् + सौम्यम् + लोचन…

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ → नः + सर्वभीतिभ्यः

    visarga before a sibilant · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before s the visarga is kept (the optional alternative to assimilation).

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • लोचनत्रयभूषितम् locanatrayabhūṣitam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (लोचनत्रय)

    Members
    लोचनत्रय + भूषित
    Vigraha
    लोचनानां त्रयम् लोचनत्रयम्; लोचनत्रयेण भूषितम् locanānāṃ trayam locanatrayam; locanatrayeṇa bhūṣitam
    Literally
    “adorned with a triad of eyes”
    In the verse
    the three-eyed face of the Goddess

  • सर्वभीतिभ्यः sarvabhītibhyaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + भीति
    Vigraha
    सर्वाः भीतयः, ताभ्यः sarvāḥ bhītayaḥ, tābhyaḥ
    Literally
    “from all fears”
    In the verse
    from every danger
Morphology & anvaya

  • पातु pātu

    Imperative third singular of √पा ‘to protect’ (पाति, पातु): the face, not the Goddess, is its subject — the start of a series in which her face, trident, bell and sword are asked to protect.

  • भूषितम् bhūṣitam

    Not a separate word: भूषित, the past participle (kta) of √भूष् (curādi 10 ‘to adorn’), is the last member of लोचनत्रयभूषितम् and takes the gender of वदनम्.

Anvaya · prose order

हे कात्यायनि, एतत् लोचनत्रयभूषितं सौम्यं ते वदनं नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः पातु। ते नमः अस्तु।

he kātyāyani, etat locanatrayabhūṣitaṃ saumyaṃ te vadanaṃ naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ pātu | te namaḥ astu |

The subject (वदनम् with three qualifiers) and the imperative पातु; then the refrain to Kātyāyanī.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first of four stanzas (11.25–28) in which the Goddess’s own attributes — her face, trident, bell and sword — are asked to protect. The gods began this hymn by praising her as Kātyāyanī (11.2 कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुः), and the asuras sought her under that name (8.29 यत्र कात्यायनी स्थिता).

Traditional interpretation

The gentle face is asked first, the fierce weapons after. The gods of chapter 4 prayed in the same order of thought: ‘protect us with your gentle forms and with your terrible ones’ (4.26 सौम्यानि यानि रूपाणि … यानि चात्यर्थघोराणि तै रक्षास्मान्).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has कात्यायनी (name 556) and त्र्यम्बका (name 762), ‘the three-eyed’. Shared names; separate works.

Verse 11.26

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ज्वालाकरालमत्युग्रमशेषासुरसूदनम्।त्रिशूलं पातु नो भीतेर्भद्रकालि नमोऽस्तु ते॥⁠२६⁠॥

jvālākarālamatyugramaśeṣāsurasūdanam |triśūlaṃ pātu no bhīterbhadrakāli namo'stu te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ज्वालाकरालम् · अत्युग्रम् · अशेषासुरसूदनम्त्रिशूलम् · पातु · नः · भीतेः · भद्रकालि · नमः · अस्तु · ते

jvālākarālam · atyugram · aśeṣāsurasūdanamtriśūlam · pātu · naḥ · bhīteḥ · bhadrakāli · namaḥ · astu · te

Word by word

  1. ज्वालाकरालम्jvālākarālamterrible with flamesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्रिशूलम्
  2. अत्युग्रम्atyugramexceedingly fiercen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्रिशूलम्
  3. अशेषासुरसूदनम्aśeṣāsurasūdanamdestroyer of all the asurasn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्रिशूलम्
  4. त्रिशूलम्triśūlamtridentn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa) used as a noun; subject of पातु
  5. पातुpātumay (it) protect√पा (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. नःnaḥusअस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of पातु
  7. भीतेःbhīteḥfrom fearf. abl. sg. of भीति (i-stem)
  8. भद्रकालिbhadrakāliO Bhadrakālīf. voc. sg. of भद्रकाली; compound (see Samāsa)
  9. नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
  10. अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

May the trident — dreadful with flames, exceedingly fierce, destroyer of the asuras without remainder — protect us from fear; O Bhadrakālī, let homage be to you.

In readable EnglishMay your trident, fearsome with flames, fiercer than fierce, slayer of every asura, protect us from fear. Bhadrakālī, salutation to you.

‘your’ is supplied from the context of the series (ते in 11.25 and 11.28).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ज्वालाकरालमत्युग्रमशेषासुरसूदनम् jvālākarālamatyugramaśeṣāsurasūdanam → ज्वालाकरालम् + अत्युग्रम् + अशेषासुरसूदनम्

    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant

    Both final m’s stand before vowels and are written together with them; the whole first line is one written unit.

  • अत्युग्रम् atyugram → अति + उग्रम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the unlike vowel u becomes y.

  • अशेषासुर aśeṣāsura → अशेष + असुर

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • नो भीतेर्भद्रकालि no bhīterbhadrakāli → नः + भीतेः + भद्रकालि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114)

    नः before the voiced bh becomes नो. In भीतेः the visarga follows e, not a or ā, so its ru stays as r before the voiced bh: भीतेर्भद्रकालि.

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    As in 11.19.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • ज्वालाकरालम् jvālākarālam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    ज्वाला + कराल
    Vigraha
    ज्वालाभिः करालम् jvālābhiḥ karālam
    Literally
    “dreadful with flames”
    In the verse
    a trident blazing with fire

  • अत्युग्रम् atyugram

    prādi tatpuruṣa (the prefix अति as first member)

    Members
    अति + उग्र
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन उग्रम् atiśayena ugram
    Literally
    “beyond fierce”
    In the verse
    exceedingly fierce

  • अशेषासुरसूदनम् aśeṣāsurasūdanam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa with the agent noun सूदन, containing a karmadhāraya (अशेषासुर); अशेष is itself a nañ bahuvrīhi (न विद्यते शेषः यस्य)

    Members
    अशेष + असुर + सूदन
    Vigraha
    अशेषाः असुराः अशेषासुराः; अशेषासुराणां सूदनम् aśeṣāḥ asurāḥ aśeṣāsurāḥ; aśeṣāsurāṇāṃ sūdanam
    Literally
    “destroyer of the asuras without remainder”
    In the verse
    the trident that slew every asura

  • त्रिशूलम् triśūlam

    bahuvrīhi (used as a noun)

    Members
    त्रि + शूल
    Vigraha
    त्रीणि शूलानि यस्य तत् trīṇi śūlāni yasya tat
    Literally
    “that which has three points”
    In the verse
    the trident, weapon of Māheśvarī (11.14) and of the Devī (4.24)

  • भद्रकालि bhadrakāli

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    भद्रा + काली
    Vigraha
    भद्रा चासौ काली च (भद्रकाली), तत्सम्बुद्धौ bhadrā cāsau kālī ca (bhadrakālī), tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “auspicious Kālī”
    In the verse
    the dark, fierce Goddess who is kindly to her devotees
    Alternative
    Also read ‘Kālī who brings good (भद्रम्)’; the compound’s form is the same. In a karmadhāraya the feminine first member takes its masculine form (भद्र-, not भद्रा-).
Morphology & anvaya

  • भीतेः bhīteḥ

    Ablative singular of the i-stem भीति ‘fear’ (गुण of the final + -ः); the same form serves as the genitive. Here it is ‘from fear’, with पातु.

  • अशेषासुरसूदनम् aśeṣāsurasūdanam

    सूदन is an agent noun in -अन from √सूद् (‘to destroy’), as in मधुसूदन; neuter here to agree with त्रिशूलम्.

Anvaya · prose order

हे भद्रकालि, ज्वालाकरालम् अत्युग्रम् अशेषासुरसूदनं (ते) त्रिशूलं नः भीतेः पातु। ते नमः अस्तु।

he bhadrakāli, jvālākarālam atyugram aśeṣāsurasūdanaṃ (te) triśūlaṃ naḥ bhīteḥ pātu | te namaḥ astu |

The first line is three qualifiers of त्रिशूलम्, the subject of पातु; then the refrain to Bhadrakālī.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The trident is asked to protect. It is Māheśvarī’s weapon (11.14 त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे; 8.49 माहेश्वरी त्रिशूलेन) and the first weapon named in the gods’ prayer of chapter 4 (4.24 शूलेन पाहि नो देवि). The name Bhadrakālī closed the account of the second carita’s hymn (4.39 तथेत्युक्त्वा भद्रकाली बभूवान्तर्हिता नृप).

Traditional interpretation

The name joins two opposites: भद्र, ‘auspicious’, and काली, ‘the dark one’. The weapon that destroyed every asura is the protection of those who take refuge in her; the fierce form is fearsome only to what is hostile.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the prayer is not ‘protect us from enemies’ but ‘protect us from fear’ (भीतेः). The danger named is the inner state as much as the outer threat.

Verse 11.27

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

हिनस्ति दैत्यतेजांसि स्वनेनापूर्य या जगत्।सा घण्टा पातु नो देवि पापेभ्योऽनः सुतानिव॥⁠२७⁠॥

hinasti daityatejāṃsi svanenāpūrya yā jagat |sā ghaṇṭā pātu no devi pāpebhyo'naḥ sutāniva ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

हिनस्ति · दैत्यतेजांसि · स्वनेन · आपूर्य · या · जगत्सा · घण्टा · पातु · नः · देवि · पापेभ्यः · नः · सुतान् · इव

hinasti · daityatejāṃsi · svanena · āpūrya · yā · jagatsā · ghaṇṭā · pātu · naḥ · devi · pāpebhyaḥ · naḥ · sutān · iva

Word by word

  1. हिनस्तिhinastidestroys, strikes down√हिंस् (rudhādi, 7P ‘to injure, destroy’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  2. दैत्यतेजांसिdaityatejāṃsithe fiery strength of the daityasn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of हिनस्ति
  3. स्वनेनsvanenawith its soundm. instr. sg. of स्वन ‘sound’; instrument of आपूर्य
  4. आपूर्यāpūryahaving filledआ + √पूर् (curādi, 10 ‘to fill’), absolutive (ल्यप्)
  5. याyāwhichrelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of हिनस्ति; anticipates घण्टा
  6. जगत्jagatthe worldn. acc. sg. of जगत्; object of आपूर्य
  7. साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative of या; qualifies घण्टा
  8. घण्टाghaṇṭābellf. nom. sg. of घण्टा; subject of पातु
  9. पातुpātumay (it) protect√पा (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. नःnaḥusअस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of पातु
  11. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  12. पापेभ्यःpāpebhyaḥfrom evils, from sinsn. abl. pl. of पाप; ablative with पातु
  13. नःnaḥourअस्मद्, enclitic gen. pl.; with पापेभ्यः (see Morph for the alternative)
  14. सुतान्sutānchildren, sonsm. acc. pl. of सुत; standard of comparison with इव
  15. इवivaas, likeindeclinable (particle of comparison)

Translation

Which, having filled the world with its sound, destroys the fiery strength of the daityas — may that bell, O Devī, protect us from our sins, as children.

In readable EnglishYour bell, whose sound fills the world and breaks the daityas’ strength — may that bell, Devī, guard us from our sins, as children are guarded.

‘as children are guarded’ renders सुतान् इव; the Sanskrit does not name who guards them. ‘Your’ is supplied from the series.

Textual verification / uncertain: The Gita Press prints पापेभ्योऽनः with an avagraha (also in its appendix); grammar has पापेभ्यः + नः, and no letter is elided. The avagraha is kept as printed and recorded as an orthographic variant (corrections.json); the witnesses read पापेभ्यो नः. The missing space after देवि in the digital text has been corrected.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वनेनापूर्य svanenāpūrya → स्वनेन + आपूर्य

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • नो देवि no devi → नः + देवि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o. The digital text writes देवि and पापेभ्यो together without a space; nothing changes there (i before p).

  • पापेभ्योऽनः pāpebhyo'naḥ → पापेभ्यः + नः

    visarga sandhi (with an avagraha in print) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o: पापेभ्यो नः. The text prints an avagraha (पापेभ्योऽनः) as though an a had been elided after the o, but no word अनः fits the sentence, and the metre counts eight syllables either way (the avagraha is silent). It is read as पापेभ्यो नः (see flags).

  • सुतानिव sutāniva → सुतान् + इव

    final n before a vowel · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply

    The n is doubled only after a short vowel; after the long ā of सुतान् it simply joins the next vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्यतेजांसि daityatejāṃsi

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दैत्य + तेजस्
    Vigraha
    दैत्यानां तेजांसि daityānāṃ tejāṃsi
    Literally
    “the energies of the daityas”
    In the verse
    the fiery might of the demon hosts
Morphology & anvaya

  • हिनस्ति hinasti

    √हिंस् is a rudhādi (seventh-class) root: the strong present inserts न before the final consonant, and the root’s own nasal falls away: हि-न-स्-ति (pl. हिंसन्ति).

  • आपूर्य āpūrya

    Absolutive of the prefixed causative-type stem आ-पूरय-: with a prefix the absolutive takes -य (ल्यप्) and the stem’s -अय- is dropped: आपूर्य ‘having filled’.

  • दैत्यतेजांसि daityatejāṃsi

    Neuter s-stem plural: तेजस् → तेजांसि, with lengthened vowel and the nasal before s (as मनांसि, यशांसि).

  • नः naḥ

    The enclitic नः occurs twice. The first is the object of पातु (‘protect us’); the second is taken as genitive with पापेभ्यः (‘from our sins’). It may also be the object repeated for emphasis; the sense hardly changes.

Anvaya · prose order

हे देवि, या (घण्टा) स्वनेन जगत् आपूर्य दैत्यतेजांसि हिनस्ति, सा घण्टा नः पापेभ्यः नः पातु, सुतान् इव।

he devi, yā (ghaṇṭā) svanena jagat āpūrya daityatejāṃsi hinasti, sā ghaṇṭā naḥ pāpebhyaḥ naḥ pātu, sutān iva |

A relative clause (या … हिनस्ति, with the absolutive phrase स्वनेन जगत् आपूर्य) describes the bell; the main clause is सा घण्टा … पातु; सुतान् इव is a comparison.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The bell is asked to protect. In the battle the Devī’s bell swelled the lion’s roar (8.9 घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादमम्बिका चोपबृंहयत्), and in the fight with Śumbha she filled the quarters with its sound, ‘which destroyed the strength of all the daitya armies’ (9.20 निजघण्टास्वनेन … समस्तदैत्यसैन्यानां तेजोवधविधायिना). The stanza recalls that line almost word for word (हिनस्ति दैत्यतेजांसि).

Traditional interpretation

The sound that undid the demons’ strength is now asked to guard against sins: the outer enemy of the narrative becomes the inner enemy of the worshipper.
