The gods asked the same in chapter 4 (4.24 घण्टास्वनेन नः पाहि). A bell is rung in the rite of worship, and the hymn gives that sound a protective sense.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: ‘as children’ makes the protection parental, not military — the same power that terrifies the hostile is tender toward its own.

Verse 11.28

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितस्ते करोज्ज्वलः।शुभाय खड्गो भवतु चण्डिके त्वां नता वयम्॥⁠२८⁠॥

asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaste karojjvalaḥ |śubhāya khaḍgo bhavatu caṇḍike tvāṃ natā vayam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितः · ते · करोज्ज्वलःशुभाय · खड्गः · भवतु · चण्डिके · त्वाम् · नताः · वयम्

asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥ · te · karojjvalaḥśubhāya · khaḍgaḥ · bhavatu · caṇḍike · tvām · natāḥ · vayam

Word by word

  1. असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितःasurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥsmeared with the mire of the asuras’ blood and fatm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies खड्गः
  2. तेteyourयुष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.; with खड्गः
  3. करोज्ज्वलःkarojjvalaḥgleaming in (your) handm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies खड्गः
  4. शुभायśubhāyafor (our) goodn. dat. sg. of शुभ; dative of purpose
  5. खड्गःkhaḍgaḥswordm. nom. sg. of खड्ग; subject of भवतु
  6. भवतुbhavatumay (it) be√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. चण्डिकेcaṇḍikeO Caṇḍikāf. voc. sg. of चण्डिका
  8. त्वाम्tvāmto youयुष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of नताः
  9. नताःnatāḥbowedpast participle (kta) of √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. nom. pl.; predicate of वयम् (अस्मः understood)
  10. वयम्vayamweअस्मद्, nom. pl.

Translation

Smeared with the mire of the asuras’ blood and fat, gleaming in your hand — may your sword be for (our) good, O Caṇḍikā; we are bowed to you.

In readable EnglishYour sword, flashing in your hand and smeared with the blood and fat of the asuras — may it be for our good, Caṇḍikā. We bow to you.

‘our’ is supplied: शुभाय names no one.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • असुरासृग्वसा asurāsṛgvasā → असुर + असृक् + वसा

    vowel and consonant sandhi inside a compound · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    a + a → ā (असुरासृक्); the final k of असृक् becomes g before the voiced v.

  • चर्चितस्ते carcitaste → चर्चितः + ते

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • करोज्ज्वलः karojjvalaḥ → कर + उज्ज्वलः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • खड्गो भवतु khaḍgo bhavatu → खड्गः + भवतु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.

  • त्वां नता वयम् tvāṃ natā vayam → त्वाम् + नताः + वयम्

    anusvāra and visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    Final m before n becomes anusvāra. -āḥ before the voiced v: the visarga (as ru) becomes y, which is dropped before a consonant: नता वयम्.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितः asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (असुरासृग्वसापङ्क, असृग्वसापङ्क) and an itaretara dvandva (असृग्वसा)

    Members
    असुर + असृक् + वसा + पङ्क + चर्चित
    Vigraha
    असृक् च वसा च असृग्वसे; असृग्वसयोः पङ्कः असृग्वसापङ्कः; असुराणाम् असृग्वसापङ्कः असुरासृग्वसापङ्कः; तेन चर्चितः asṛk ca vasā ca asṛgvase; asṛgvasayoḥ paṅkaḥ asṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ; asurāṇām asṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ; tena carcitaḥ
    Literally
    “smeared with the mud of the blood and fat of the asuras”
    In the verse
    the sword still wet from the battle
    Alternative
    असृग्वसापङ्क may also be a karmadhāraya of metaphor (असृग्वसे एव पङ्कः, ‘the mire that is blood and fat’); the meaning is the same.

  • करोज्ज्वलः karojjvalaḥ

    saptamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    कर + उज्ज्वल
    Vigraha
    करे उज्ज्वलः kare ujjvalaḥ
    Literally
    “shining in the hand”
    In the verse
    flashing in the Goddess’s hand
    Alternative
    Also a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, करेण उज्ज्वलः, ‘bright through (being held in) the hand’; the saptamī is simpler.
Morphology & anvaya

  • नताः natāḥ

    Past participle of √नम् in the active sense (an intransitive root of motion and posture): ‘(we are) bowed’, with the accusative त्वाम् of the one bowed to. The copula अस्मः is understood.

  • शुभाय śubhāya

    Dative of purpose: ‘for good, to (our) welfare’ — what the sword is to serve.

  • भवतु bhavatu

    Imperative third singular of √भू, the thematic present भव- + -तु: ‘may it be’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे चण्डिके, असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितः करोज्ज्वलः ते खड्गः (नः) शुभाय भवतु। वयं त्वां नताः (स्मः)।

he caṇḍike, asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥ karojjvalaḥ te khaḍgaḥ (naḥ) śubhāya bhavatu | vayaṃ tvāṃ natāḥ (smaḥ) |

A benediction (खड्गः … शुभाय भवतु) with two qualifiers of the sword, and a closing statement (वयं त्वां नताः) in place of the refrain.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last of the protection stanzas asks the sword to be ‘for good’. It is the weapon with which the Devī cut down Muṇḍa (7.21) and beheaded Niśumbha (9.33, 9.36), and which the gods named among her weapons in 4.27 (खड्गशूलगदादीनि … तैरस्मान् रक्ष सर्वतः). The refrain becomes a plain statement: ‘we are bowed to you’.

Traditional interpretation

A sword still wet with blood is asked for auspiciousness. The gods of chapter 4 already saw the paradox: her weapons purify even the enemies they strike, who go to the higher worlds (4.18–19 लोकान् प्रयान्तु रिपवोऽपि हि शस्त्रपूता). The violence is directed at what threatens the world.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has चण्डिका (name 755). The name is shared; the works are separate.

Verse 11.29

Śloka · indravajrā
‹›

रोगानशेषानपहंसि तुष्टारुष्टा तु कामान् सकलानभीष्टान्।त्वामाश्रितानां न विपन्नराणांत्वामाश्रिता ह्याश्रयतां प्रयान्ति॥⁠२९⁠॥

rogānaśeṣānapahaṃsi tuṣṭāruṣṭā tu kāmān sakalānabhīṣṭān |tvāmāśritānāṃ na vipannarāṇāṃtvāmāśritā hyāśrayatāṃ prayānti ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

रोगान् · अशेषान् · अपहंसि · तुष्टारुष्टा · तु · कामान् · सकलान् · अभीष्टान्त्वाम् · आश्रितानाम् · न · विपत् · नराणाम्त्वाम् · आश्रिताः · हि · आश्रयताम् · प्रयान्ति

rogān · aśeṣān · apahaṃsi · tuṣṭāruṣṭā · tu · kāmān · sakalān · abhīṣṭāntvām · āśritānām · na · vipat · narāṇāmtvām · āśritāḥ · hi · āśrayatām · prayānti

Word by word

  1. रोगान्rogāndiseasesm. acc. pl. of रोग; object of अपहंसि
  2. अशेषान्aśeṣānall, without remainderm. acc. pl. of अशेष; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रोगान्
  3. अपहंसिapahaṃsiyou strike away, destroyअप + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  4. तुष्टाtuṣṭā(when) pleasedpast participle (kta) of √तुष् (divādi, 4P ‘to be pleased’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with the implied त्वम्
  5. रुष्टाruṣṭā(when) angeredpast participle (kta) of √रुष् (divādi, 4P ‘to be angry’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with the implied त्वम्
  6. तुtubutindeclinable (adversative particle)
  7. कामान्kāmāndesires, the things wished form. acc. pl. of काम; object of अपहंसि (understood from the first line)
  8. सकलान्sakalānallm. acc. pl. of सकल; qualifies कामान्
  9. अभीष्टान्abhīṣṭānlonged for, cherishedpast participle (kta) of अभि + √इष् (tudādi, 6P ‘to wish’), m. acc. pl.; qualifies कामान्
  10. त्वाम्tvāmyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of आश्रितानाम्
  11. आश्रितानाम्āśritānāmwho have taken refuge (in)past participle (kta) of आ + √श्रि (bhvādi, 1U ‘to resort to’), m. gen. pl.; qualifies नराणाम्
  12. नnano, notindeclinable (negation)
  13. विपत्vipatcalamity, misfortunef. nom. sg. of विपद् (वि + √पद् with the zero affix क्विप्); अस्ति understood
  14. नराणाम्narāṇāmfor men, of peoplem. gen. pl. of नर; genitive of the one affected
  15. त्वाम्tvāmyouयुष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of आश्रिताः
  16. आश्रिताःāśritāḥthose who have taken refuge (in)past participle (kta) of आ + √श्रि, m. nom. pl.; subject of प्रयान्ति
  17. हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  18. आश्रयताम्āśrayatāmthe state of being a refugef. acc. sg. of आश्रयता (abstract noun: आश्रय + taddhita -ता); object of प्रयान्ति
  19. प्रयान्तिprayāntiattain, go toप्र + √या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Pleased, you strike away diseases without remainder; but angered, all longed-for desires. For men who have taken refuge in you there is no calamity; those who have taken refuge in you indeed attain the state of being a refuge.

In readable EnglishPleased, you take away every illness; angered, every cherished hope. No misfortune befalls those who take refuge in you; those who take refuge in you become a refuge themselves.

‘become a refuge’ renders आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति ‘attain refuge-hood’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • रोगानशेषानपहंसि rogānaśeṣānapahaṃsi → रोगान् + अशेषान् + अपहंसि

    final n before a vowel · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply

    After the long ā the final n is not doubled; it simply joins the next vowel. The same in सकलानभीष्टान्.

  • विपन्नराणां vipannarāṇāṃ → विपत् + नराणाम्

    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39), यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t (d) becomes the nasal n before the nasal n: विपन्नराणाम्. Not the participle विपन्न: read न विपत् नराणाम्.

  • त्वामाश्रिता ह्याश्रयतां tvāmāśritā hyāśrayatāṃ → त्वाम् + आश्रिताः + हि + आश्रयताम्

    visarga and vowel sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final m joins the vowel; -āḥ before the voiced h loses its visarga (ru → y, dropped before a consonant); the i of हि becomes y before ā: ह्याश्रयताम्.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अशेषान् aśeṣān

    nañ bahuvrīhi

    Members
    अ (नञ्) + शेष
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते शेषः येषां ते, तान् na vidyate śeṣaḥ yeṣāṃ te, tān
    Literally
    “those of which no remainder exists”
    In the verse
    all, every one
Morphology & anvaya

  • अपहंसि apahaṃsi

    Present second singular of अप + √हन्: हन् + सि → हंसि, the n becoming anusvāra before s inside the word. With अप ‘away’: ‘you strike away, remove’. It governs both lines: रोगान् … अपहंसि, and (अपहंसि) कामान्.

  • तुष्टा tuṣṭā

    Past participles of intransitive roots used in the active sense, with the force of a conditional: तुष्टा ‘(you,) when pleased’, रुष्टा ‘when angry’.

  • आश्रयताम् āśrayatām

    Abstract noun in -ता on आश्रय ‘refuge’: ‘refuge-hood’. With प्रयान्ति: ‘they attain the state of being a refuge’, i.e. they become a shelter for others.

  • विपत् vipat

    विपद् is a root noun (वि + √पद् ‘to fall apart’) with no visible affix; its final d is pronounced t at a pause, so the padaccheda gives विपत्.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे देवि, त्वं) तुष्टा (सती) अशेषान् रोगान् अपहंसि, रुष्टा तु सकलान् अभीष्टान् कामान् (अपहंसि)। त्वाम् आश्रितानां नराणां विपत् न (भवति)। त्वाम् आश्रिताः हि आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति।

(he devi, tvaṃ) tuṣṭā (satī) aśeṣān rogān apahaṃsi, ruṣṭā tu sakalān abhīṣṭān kāmān (apahaṃsi) | tvām āśritānāṃ narāṇāṃ vipat na (bhavati) | tvām āśritāḥ hi āśrayatāṃ prayānti |

Two balanced clauses with one verb (अपहंसि), conditioned by तुष्टा and रुष्टा; then two statements about those who take refuge in her.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn returns to the long metre of its opening stanzas (11.2–6; here indravajrā, eleven syllables a line). The first half sets out her power over fortune: her pleasure removes disease, her anger removes everything one longs for. The second half states the reward of refuge: no calamity, and more — the one who takes refuge becomes a refuge.

Traditional interpretation

Traditionally this stanza is recited on its own as a prayer for freedom from illness. The last line is read as the fruit of devotion: the devotee’s protection reaches beyond himself to those who in turn depend on him.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: refuge is not only received but passed on. Those who are held become able to hold others.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls her भवरोगघ्नी (name 842), ‘destroyer of the disease of worldly existence’, and रोगपर्वतदम्भोलिः (name 748), ‘a thunderbolt to the mountain of disease’. The idea is shared; the works are separate.

Verse 11.30

Śloka · indravajrā
‹›

एतत्कृतं यत्कदनं त्वयाद्यधर्मद्विषां देवि महासुराणाम्।रूपैरनेकैर्बहुधाऽऽत्ममूर्तिंकृत्वाम्बिके तत्प्रकरोति कान्या॥⁠३०⁠॥

etatkṛtaṃ yatkadanaṃ tvayādyadharmadviṣāṃ devi mahāsurāṇām |rūpairanekairbahudhā''tmamūrtiṃkṛtvāmbike tatprakaroti kānyā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एतत् · कृतम् · यत् · कदनम् · त्वया · अद्यधर्मद्विषाम् · देवि · महासुराणाम्रूपैः · अनेकैः · बहुधा · आत्ममूर्तिम्कृत्वा · अम्बिके · तत् · प्रकरोति · का · अन्या

etat · kṛtam · yat · kadanam · tvayā · adyadharmadviṣām · devi · mahāsurāṇāmrūpaiḥ · anekaiḥ · bahudhā · ātmamūrtimkṛtvā · ambike · tat · prakaroti · kā · anyā

Word by word

  1. एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies कदनम्
  2. कृतम्kṛtamdone, wroughtpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of कदनम्
  3. यत्yatwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; agrees with कदनम्
  4. कदनम्kadanamslaughter, carnagen. nom. sg. of कदन
  5. त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of the passive कृतम्
  6. अद्यadyatoday, nowindeclinable (adverb of time)
  7. धर्मद्विषाम्dharmadviṣāmof the haters of dharmam. gen. pl. of धर्मद्विष्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महासुराणाम्
  8. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  9. महासुराणाम्mahāsurāṇāmof the great asurasm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); objective genitive with कदनम्
  10. रूपैःrūpaiḥby formsn. instr. pl. of रूप
  11. अनेकैःanekaiḥmanyn. instr. pl. of अनेक; qualifies रूपैः
  12. बहुधाbahudhāmanifold, in many waysindeclinable (adverb; taddhita -धा on बहु)
  13. आत्ममूर्तिम्ātmamūrtimyour own bodyf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of कृत्वा
  14. कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving madeabsolutive (ktvā) of √कृ
  15. अम्बिकेambikeO Ambikāf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका
  16. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; object of प्रकरोति; resumes कदनम्
  17. प्रकरोतिprakarotiaccomplishes, doesप्र + √कृ (tanādi, 8U), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  18. काkāwho, what (woman)interrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; subject of प्रकरोति
  19. अन्याanyāotherpronominal adjective अन्य, f. nom. sg.; with का

Translation

This slaughter of the great asuras, haters of dharma, which was wrought by you today, O Devī, having made your own body manifold through many forms — O Ambikā, what other (woman) does that?

In readable EnglishToday, Devī, you made your own form manifold in many shapes and wrought this slaughter of the great asuras who hated dharma. Ambikā, who else could do such a thing?

The passive is turned active; the rhetorical present प्रकरोति is rendered ‘could do’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एतत्कृतं यत्कदनं etatkṛtaṃ yatkadanaṃ → एतत् + कृतम् + यत् + कदनम्

    no change; anusvāra · खरि च (8.4.55); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final t before the unvoiced k stays t; the words are only written together. Final m becomes anusvāra before a consonant.

  • त्वयाद्य tvayādya → त्वया + अद्य

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā. The same in कृत्वाम्बिके and कान्या.

  • रूपैरनेकैर्बहुधा rūpairanekairbahudhā → रूपैः + अनेकैः + बहुधा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai (not a or ā) the visarga, as ru, stays r before a vowel and before a voiced consonant.

  • बहुधाऽऽत्ममूर्तिं bahudhā''tmamūrtiṃ → बहुधा + आत्ममूर्तिम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that the long ā of आत्म- has merged into the ā of बहुधा; it is silent.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • धर्मद्विषाम् dharmadviṣām

    upapada tatpuruṣa (द्विष् = √द्विष् with the zero affix क्विप्)

    Members
    धर्म + द्विष्
    Vigraha
    धर्मं द्विषन्ति इति धर्मद्विषः, तेषाम् dharmaṃ dviṣanti iti dharmadviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of those who hate dharma”
    In the verse
    the enemies of the right order

  • महासुराणाम् mahāsurāṇām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तः असुराः, तेषाम् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the great asuras”
    In the verse
    Śumbha, Niśumbha and their generals

  • अनेकैः anekaiḥ

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ (नञ्) + एक
    Vigraha
    न एकानि अनेकानि, तैः na ekāni anekāni, taiḥ
    Literally
    “not one”
    In the verse
    many

  • आत्ममूर्तिम् ātmamūrtim

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    आत्मन् + मूर्ति
    Vigraha
    आत्मनः मूर्तिः, ताम् ātmanaḥ mūrtiḥ, tām
    Literally
    “the body of (your) self”
    In the verse
    your own form
Morphology & anvaya

  • कृतम् kṛtam

    Passive construction with the past participle: एतत् यत् कदनं त्वया कृतम् ‘this slaughter which was done by you’; the agent stands in the instrumental.

  • प्रकरोति prakaroti

    Present third singular of प्र + √कृ (tanādi: करो-ति, strong stem करो-). In a rhetorical question: ‘who else does that?’ — no one.

  • बहुधा bahudhā

    Adverb of manner from बहु with the taddhita -धा (‘in … ways’), as द्विधा ‘in two’, सहस्रधा ‘in a thousand ways’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे देवि अम्बिके, अनेकैः रूपैः आत्ममूर्तिं बहुधा कृत्वा त्वया अद्य धर्मद्विषां महासुराणां यत् एतत् कदनं कृतम्, तत् का अन्या प्रकरोति?

he devi ambike, anekaiḥ rūpaiḥ ātmamūrtiṃ bahudhā kṛtvā tvayā adya dharmadviṣāṃ mahāsurāṇāṃ yat etat kadanaṃ kṛtam, tat kā anyā prakaroti?

A relative clause in the passive (यत् … कदनं त्वया कृतम्), with the absolutive phrase (रूपैः … कृत्वा) giving the means; the main clause is a rhetorical question resuming it with तत्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods recall the battle of chapters 8–10: the Devī appeared as many — the Mātṛkās, Kālī, Śivadūtī — and destroyed the asuras. No other being could have done it.

Traditional interpretation

The stanza answers Śumbha’s taunt that she fought ‘relying on the strength of others’ (10.3 अन्यासां बलमाश्रित्य). The Devī had replied that the many forms were her own powers and drew them back into herself: एकैवाहं जगत्यत्र द्वितीया का ममापरा (10.5), ‘I alone am here in the world; who else is there besides me?’, and ‘I stood here in many forms by my power’ (10.8 अहं विभूत्या बहुभिरिह रूपैर्यदास्थिता). The gods’ question का अन्या echoes her own द्वितीया का.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: plurality here is not division. The many forms are one power acting in many ways.

Verse 11.31

Śloka · upajāti (triṣṭubh)
‹›

विद्यासु शास्त्रेषु विवेकदीपे-ष्वाद्येषु वाक्येषु च का त्वदन्या।ममत्वगर्तेऽतिमहान्धकारेविभ्रामयत्येतदतीव विश्वम्॥⁠३१⁠॥

vidyāsu śāstreṣu vivekadīpe-ṣvādyeṣu vākyeṣu ca kā tvadanyā |mamatvagarte'timahāndhakārevibhrāmayatyetadatīva viśvam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

विद्यासु · शास्त्रेषु · विवेकदीपेषुआद्येषु · वाक्येषु · च · का · त्वदन्याममत्वगर्ते · अतिमहान्धकारेविभ्रामयति · एतत् · अतीव · विश्वम्

vidyāsu · śāstreṣu · vivekadīpeṣuādyeṣu · vākyeṣu · ca · kā · tvadanyāmamatvagarte · atimahāndhakārevibhrāmayati · etat · atīva · viśvam

Word by word

  1. विद्यासुvidyāsuin the branches of knowledgef. loc. pl. of विद्या
  2. शास्त्रेषुśāstreṣuin the treatises, the śāstrasn. loc. pl. of शास्त्र
  3. विवेकदीपेषुvivekadīpeṣuin the lamps of discernmentm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु (see Samāsa, alt)
  4. आद्येषुādyeṣuprimal, firstn. loc. pl. of आद्य (‘being at the beginning’); qualifies वाक्येषु
  5. वाक्येषुvākyeṣuin the utterances, the sayingsn. loc. pl. of वाक्य
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); closes the series of locatives
  7. काkāwhointerrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.
  8. त्वदन्याtvadanyāother than youf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); with का
  9. ममत्वगर्तेmamatvagartein the pit of ‘mine-ness’, of possessivenessm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. अतिमहान्धकारेatimahāndhakāre(the pit) of exceedingly great darknessm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ममत्वगर्ते
  11. विभ्रामयतिvibhrāmayatimakes (it) whirl, sends wanderingवि + √भ्रम् (‘to wander, whirl’), causative (णिच्), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. acc. sg.; qualifies विश्वम्
  13. अतीवatīvaexceedingly, utterlyindeclinable (adverb)
  14. विश्वम्viśvamthe universe, the whole worldn. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of विभ्रामयति

Translation

In the branches of knowledge, in the śāstras, in the primal sayings that are lamps of discernment — who is there other than you? (Who other than you) makes this universe whirl exceedingly in the pit of possessiveness, of very great darkness?

In readable EnglishIn all knowledge and every scripture, in the ancient words that are lamps of discernment, who is there but you? And who but you sets this whole world whirling in the dark abyss of ‘mine’?

The question का त्वदन्या is carried into the second half, as the third-person verb requires.

Textual verification / uncertain: Interpretive: विवेकदीपेषु may be a separate locative of the list or stand in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु; अतिमहान्धकारे may be a bahuvrīhi qualifying ममत्वगर्ते or a parallel locative. The sense hardly changes.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विवेकदीपेष्वाद्येषु vivekadīpeṣvādyeṣu → विवेकदीपेषु + आद्येषु

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) across the printed line · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    u before the unlike vowel ā becomes v. The printed line breaks inside the word (विवेकदीपे- / ष्वाद्येषु); the word belongs to the first line.

  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā → त्वत् + अन्या

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    The t of the pronoun stem त्वत् becomes d before a vowel.

  • ममत्वगर्तेऽतिमहान्धकारे mamatvagarte'timahāndhakāre → ममत्वगर्ते + अतिमहान्धकारे

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa); savarṇa-dīrgha inside the compound · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    -e + a: the a is absorbed (avagraha). Inside the compound, महा + अन्धकार → महान्धकार.

  • विभ्रामयत्येतदतीव vibhrāmayatyetadatīva → विभ्रामयति + एतत् + अतीव

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) and consonant sandhi (voicing) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    i before e becomes y; the final t of एतत् becomes d before the vowel of अतीव.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • विवेकदीपेषु vivekadīpeṣu

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    विवेक + दीप
    Vigraha
    विवेकस्य दीपाः, तेषु vivekasya dīpāḥ, teṣu
    Literally
    “in the lamps of discernment”
    In the verse
    in what illuminates the power to distinguish the real from the unreal
    Alternative
    Also a karmadhāraya of metaphor (विवेकः एव दीपः, ‘discernment, which is a lamp’). It may stand as a third item of the list (‘in the lamps of discernment’) or in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु (‘in the primal sayings, which are lamps of discernment’). The second is taken here; the first is equally grammatical.

  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    त्वद् + अन्या
    Vigraha
    त्वत् अन्या tvat anyā
    Literally
    “other than you”
    In the verse
    anyone besides you (अन्य takes the ablative)

  • ममत्वगर्ते mamatvagarte

    karmadhāraya (rūpaka: the pit that is possessiveness)

    Members
    ममत्व + गर्त
    Vigraha
    ममत्वम् एव गर्तः, तस्मिन् mamatvam eva gartaḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the pit which is ‘mine-ness’”
    In the verse
    in the abyss of attachment to ‘me and mine’
    Alternative
    Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, ममत्वस्य गर्तः ‘the pit of possessiveness’; the sense is the same.

  • अतिमहान्धकारे atimahāndhakāre

    bahuvrīhi (qualifying ममत्वगर्ते); अतिमहत् is a prādi tatpuruṣa (अतिशयेन महत्)

    Members
    अतिमहत् + अन्धकार
    Vigraha
    अतिमहान् अन्धकारः यस्मिन् सः, तस्मिन् atimahān andhakāraḥ yasmin saḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “in which there is exceedingly great darkness”
    In the verse
    pitch-dark
    Alternative
    It may also be a karmadhāraya (अतिमहान् अन्धकारः) in a second locative, ‘in the very great darkness’, parallel to ममत्वगर्ते.
Morphology & anvaya

  • विभ्रामयति vibhrāmayati

    Causative of √भ्रम् ‘to wander, whirl’: the root vowel takes vṛddhi before -अय- (भ्रम् → भ्रामय-), ‘makes wander’; with वि ‘astray, about’. Its subject is का त्वदन्या, third person — so the question of the second line runs on into the second half: ‘who but you makes this world whirl?’.

  • ममत्वगर्ते mamatvagarte

    ममत्व is an abstract noun in -त्व formed on मम, the genitive ‘of me, mine’: ‘mine-ness’, possessiveness. Abstracts formed on a case-form are rare and expressive.

  • अतीव atīva

    अति + इव fused (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101) into one adverb, ‘exceedingly’.

Anvaya · prose order

विद्यासु शास्त्रेषु विवेकदीपेषु आद्येषु वाक्येषु च त्वदन्या का (वर्तते)? अतिमहान्धकारे ममत्वगर्ते एतत् विश्वम् अतीव (त्वदन्या का) विभ्रामयति?

vidyāsu śāstreṣu vivekadīpeṣu ādyeṣu vākyeṣu ca tvadanyā kā (vartate)? atimahāndhakāre mamatvagarte etat viśvam atīva (tvadanyā kā) vibhrāmayati?

One rhetorical question (का त्वदन्या) governs both halves: in the first it is the subject of an understood ‘is’ with five locatives; in the second it is the subject of विभ्रामयति.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Two sides of one power. The Goddess is present in every source of knowledge and discernment; and it is she who sends the world whirling in the darkness of possessiveness. The same question, ‘who but you?’, covers both.

Traditional interpretation

The teaching is Medhas’s in the first chapter: creatures are thrown ‘into the whirlpool of mine-ness, the pit of delusion’ (1.53 ममतावर्त्ते मोहगर्ते निपातिताः) by the power of Mahāmāyā, who drags even the minds of the wise into delusion (1.55–56) and is also the supreme knowledge that frees (1.57). The ‘primal sayings’ are commonly understood as the Vedas. The question का त्वदन्या was asked in the gods’ hymn of chapter 4 as well (4.17).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the pit is ‘mine’. What darkens the world in this stanza is not ignorance of facts but the habit of possession.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) holds both sides in two names: विश्वभ्रमणकारिणी (name 889), ‘she who makes the universe whirl’, and ममताहन्त्री (name 165), ‘destroyer of possessiveness’. The texts are separate; the paired idea is shared.

Verse 11.32

Śloka · indravajrā
‹›

रक्षांसि यत्रोग्रविषाश्च नागायत्रारयो दस्युबलानि यत्र।दावानलो यत्र तथाब्धिमध्येतत्र स्थिता त्वं परिपासि विश्वम्॥⁠३२⁠॥

rakṣāṃsi yatrograviṣāśca nāgāyatrārayo dasyubalāni yatra |dāvānalo yatra tathābdhimadhyetatra sthitā tvaṃ paripāsi viśvam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

रक्षांसि · यत्र · उग्रविषाः · च · नागाःयत्र · अरयः · दस्युबलानि · यत्रदावानलः · यत्र · तथा · अब्धिमध्येतत्र · स्थिता · त्वम् · परिपासि · विश्वम्

rakṣāṃsi · yatra · ugraviṣāḥ · ca · nāgāḥyatra · arayaḥ · dasyubalāni · yatradāvānalaḥ · yatra · tathā · abdhimadhyetatra · sthitā · tvam · paripāsi · viśvam

Word by word

  1. रक्षांसिrakṣāṃsirākṣasas, demonsn. nom. pl. of रक्षस् (s-stem); अस्ति/सन्ति understood
  2. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place); correlative of तत्र
  3. उग्रविषाःugraviṣāḥof fierce venomm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies नागाः
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  5. नागाःnāgāḥserpentsm. nom. pl. of नाग
  6. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
  7. अरयःarayaḥenemiesm. nom. pl. of अरि (i-stem)
  8. दस्युबलानिdasyubalānibands of robbersn. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  9. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
  10. दावानलःdāvānalaḥa forest firem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  11. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
  12. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, here connective)
  13. अब्धिमध्येabdhimadhyein the midst of the oceann. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  14. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (correlative adverb of place)
  15. स्थिताsthitāstanding, presentpast participle (kta) of √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with त्वम्
  16. त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of परिपासि
  17. परिपासिparipāsiyou protectपरि + √पा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  18. विश्वम्viśvamthe worldn. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of परिपासि

Translation

Where there are rākṣasas, where serpents of fierce venom, where enemies, where bands of robbers, where a forest fire, and likewise in the midst of the ocean — standing there, you protect the world.

In readable EnglishWhere demons are, and serpents of deadly venom, where enemies and robber bands, where the forest is ablaze, and out on the open sea — there you stand and guard the world.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • यत्रोग्रविषाश्च yatrograviṣāśca → यत्र + उग्रविषाः + च

    vowel sandhi (guṇa); visarga sandhi · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    a + u → o. The visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś: -श्च.

  • नागा यत्रारयो nāgā yatrārayo → नागाः + यत्र + अरयः

    visarga and vowel sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114)

    -āḥ before the voiced y loses its visarga (across the printed line); a + a → ā (यत्रारयः); -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o (अरयो दस्यु…).

  • दावानलो यत्र dāvānalo yatra → दावानलः + यत्र

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.

  • तथाब्धिमध्ये tathābdhimadhye → तथा + अब्धिमध्ये

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • उग्रविषाः ugraviṣāḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    उग्र + विष
    Vigraha
    उग्रं विषं येषां ते ugraṃ viṣaṃ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose venom is fierce”
    In the verse
    deadly serpents

  • दस्युबलानि dasyubalāni

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दस्यु + बल
    Vigraha
    दस्यूनां बलानि dasyūnāṃ balāni
    Literally

    In the verse

  • दावानलः dāvānalaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दाव + अनल
    Vigraha
    दावस्य अनलः dāvasya analaḥ
    Literally
    “the fire of the forest”
    In the verse

    Alternative
    दाव itself can mean ‘forest fire’; then a karmadhāraya, दावः एव अनलः. The ṣaṣṭhī reading, दाव = ‘forest’, is the usual one.

  • अब्धिमध्ये abdhimadhye

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अब्धि ‘holder of waters’ is itself an upapada compound of अप् and धि)

    Members
    अब्धि + मध्य
    Vigraha
    अब्धेः मध्यम्, तस्मिन् abdheḥ madhyam, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the middle of the ocean”
    In the verse
    at sea, far from any help
Morphology & anvaya

  • रक्षांसि rakṣāṃsi

    Neuter s-stem plural: रक्षस् → रक्षांसि, as तेजांसि in 11.27.

  • अरयः arayaḥ

    Nominative plural of the i-stem अरि with guṇa of the stem vowel: अरि → अरय् + अः.

  • परिपासि paripāsi

    Present second singular of परि + √पा ‘to protect’ (adādi: पा-सि); परि ‘all around’ strengthens the sense: ‘you guard on every side’.

Anvaya · prose order

यत्र रक्षांसि, यत्र उग्रविषाः नागाः च, यत्र अरयः, यत्र दस्युबलानि, यत्र दावानलः, तथा अब्धिमध्ये, तत्र स्थिता त्वं विश्वं परिपासि।

yatra rakṣāṃsi, yatra ugraviṣāḥ nāgāḥ ca, yatra arayaḥ, yatra dasyubalāni, yatra dāvānalaḥ, tathā abdhimadhye, tatra sthitā tvaṃ viśvaṃ paripāsi |

A series of relative clauses of place (यत्र …) with the verb ‘to be’ understood, closed by the correlative तत्र and the main clause.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A catalogue of the dangers of the wild and of travel: demons, snakes, enemies, robbers, fire, the sea. Wherever danger is, the Goddess is there (तत्र स्थिता) and protects. The repeated यत्र, four times in three lines, builds the list.

Traditional interpretation

The Devī makes the matching promise in chapter 12: one who remembers her story is freed from distress when surrounded by forest fire, beset by robbers or held by enemies, or tossed by the wind ‘in a ship on the great ocean’ (12.25–29 दावाग्निपरिवारितः … दस्युभिर्वा वृतः … स्थितः पोते महार्णवे).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the hymn does not say she removes every danger from the world, but that she stands within it. Protection is presence.

Verse 11.33

Śloka · indravajrā
‹›

विश्वेश्वरि त्वं परिपासि विश्वंविश्वात्मिका धारयसीति विश्वम्।विश्वेशवन्द्या भवती भवन्तिविश्वाश्रया ये त्वयि भक्तिनम्राः॥⁠३३⁠॥

viśveśvari tvaṃ paripāsi viśvaṃviśvātmikā dhārayasīti viśvam |viśveśavandyā bhavatī bhavantiviśvāśrayā ye tvayi bhaktinamrāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

विश्वेश्वरि · त्वम् · परिपासि · विश्वम्विश्वात्मिका · धारयसि · इति · विश्वम्विश्वेशवन्द्या · भवती · भवन्तिविश्वाश्रयाः · ये · त्वयि · भक्तिनम्राः

viśveśvari · tvam · paripāsi · viśvamviśvātmikā · dhārayasi · iti · viśvamviśveśavandyā · bhavatī · bhavantiviśvāśrayāḥ · ye · tvayi · bhaktinamrāḥ

Word by word

  1. विश्वेश्वरिviśveśvariO Queen of the universef. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of परिपासि
  3. परिपासिparipāsiprotectपरि + √पा (adādi, 2P), laṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  4. विश्वम्viśvamthe universen. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of परिपासि
  5. विश्वात्मिकाviśvātmikābeing the self of the universef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of the implied त्वम्
  6. धारयसिdhārayasiyou uphold, sustain√धृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to hold’), stem धारय- (causative form, in the sense of the simple root), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. इतिitithus, thereforeindeclinable; here marks the reason: ‘(being) the self of all, therefore …’
  8. विश्वम्viśvamthe universen. acc. sg.; object of धारयसि
  9. विश्वेशवन्द्याviśveśavandyāto be revered by the lords of the universef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of भवती
  10. भवतीbhavatīyou, Your Ladyshiphonorific pronoun भवत्, f. nom. sg. (construed with the third person; अस्ति understood)
  11. भवन्तिbhavantibecome√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  12. विश्वाश्रयाःviśvāśrayāḥrefuges of the universem. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of भवन्ति
  13. येyethose whorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of भवन्ति
  14. त्वयिtvayito you (lit. ‘in you’)युष्मद्, loc. sg.; locative of the object of devotion
  15. भक्तिनम्राःbhaktinamrāḥbowed in devotionm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ये

Translation

O Queen of the universe, you protect the universe; being the self of the universe, therefore you uphold the universe. Your Ladyship is to be revered by the lords of the universe; those who are bowed in devotion to you become the refuges of the universe.

In readable EnglishQueen of the universe, you guard the universe; as its very self you hold the universe up. The lords of the universe revere you, and those who bow to you in devotion become the shelter of the universe.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari → विश्व + ईश्वरि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e. The same in विश्वेश- (विश्व + ईश).

  • विश्वं विश्वात्मिका viśvaṃ viśvātmikā → विश्वम् + विश्वात्मिका

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before the consonant v becomes anusvāra, across the printed line.

  • धारयसीति dhārayasīti → धारयसि + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i + i → ī.

  • विश्वाश्रया ये viśvāśrayā ye → विश्वाश्रयाः + ये

    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before the voiced y loses its visarga.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    विश्व + ईश्वरी
    Vigraha
    विश्वस्य ईश्वरी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ viśvasya īśvarī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “ruler of the universe”
    In the verse
    the sovereign of all that is (as in 11.3 विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वम्)

  • विश्वात्मिका viśvātmikā

    bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -क, feminine -इका)

    Members
    विश्व + आत्मन्
    Vigraha
    विश्वम् आत्मा यस्याः सा viśvam ātmā yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose self is the universe”
    In the verse
    the universe is her very being
    Alternative
    Also ‘she who is the self of the universe’ (विश्वस्य आत्मा यस्याः स्वरूपम्); the -आत्मिका formation allows both. The reason clause (इति) fits either: because she is one with it, she holds it.

  • विश्वेशवन्द्या viśveśavandyā

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (with the gerundive वन्द्य) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (विश्वेश)

    Members
    विश्वेश + वन्द्य
    Vigraha
    विश्वस्य ईशाः विश्वेशाः; विश्वेशैः वन्द्या viśvasya īśāḥ viśveśāḥ; viśveśaiḥ vandyā
    Literally
    “to be revered by the lords of the universe”
    In the verse
    honoured even by the gods who rule the world

  • विश्वाश्रयाः viśvāśrayāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    विश्व + आश्रय
    Vigraha
    विश्वस्य आश्रयाः viśvasya āśrayāḥ
    Literally
    “refuges of the universe”
    In the verse
    a shelter for the whole world

  • भक्तिनम्राः bhaktinamrāḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भक्ति + नम्र
    Vigraha
    भक्त्या नम्राः bhaktyā namrāḥ
    Literally
    “bent low by devotion”
    In the verse
    bowing in devotion
Morphology & anvaya

  • भवती bhavatī

    The honorific pronoun भवत् ‘Your Honour’ (feminine भवती) takes a third-person verb. It sits beside भवन्ति (√भू, 3 pl.): the same sound in two different words, a deliberate echo.

  • धारयसि dhārayasi

    धारयति ‘holds, supports’ has the causative form but the sense of the simple root √धृ. With the preceding विश्वात्मिका … इति: ‘being the universe’s self, you uphold it’.

  • विश्वेशवन्द्या viśveśavandyā

    वन्द्य is the gerundive (ण्यत्) of √वन्द् (bhvādi 1Ā ‘to praise, revere’): ‘to be revered’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे विश्वेश्वरि, त्वं विश्वं परिपासि। (त्वं) विश्वात्मिका (असि) इति विश्वं धारयसि। भवती विश्वेशवन्द्या (अस्ति)। ये त्वयि भक्तिनम्राः, (ते) विश्वाश्रयाः भवन्ति।

he viśveśvari, tvaṃ viśvaṃ paripāsi | (tvaṃ) viśvātmikā (asi) iti viśvaṃ dhārayasi | bhavatī viśveśavandyā (asti) | ye tvayi bhaktinamrāḥ, (te) viśvāśrayāḥ bhavanti |

Four short statements, one to a line: she protects; being its self she sustains; she is revered by its lords; her devotees become its refuge.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The word विश्व ‘universe’ is struck six times in four lines. She protects the universe, sustains it because it is her own self, is honoured by its rulers, and makes her devotees its refuge. The last line takes up 11.29: those who take refuge in her become a refuge (आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति).

Traditional interpretation

The thought that she holds the world because she is its self is Brahmā’s in the first carita (1.75 त्वयैतद्धार्यते विश्वं), and the gods opened this hymn with the same title (11.3 प्रसीद विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वम्). ‘The lords of the universe’ are the ruling gods, who, as Brahmā says, took their bodies through her (1.84–85).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has विश्वधारिणी (name 759), ‘she who upholds the universe’. A shared idea; separate works.

Verse 11.34

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

देवि प्रसीद परिपालय नोऽरिभीते-र्नित्यं यथासुरवधादधुनैव सद्यः।पापानि सर्वजगतां प्रशमं नयाशुउत्पातपाकजनितांश्च महोपसर्गान्॥⁠३४⁠॥

devi prasīda paripālaya no'ribhīte-rnityaṃ yathāsuravadhādadhunaiva sadyaḥ |pāpāni sarvajagatāṃ praśamaṃ nayāśuutpātapākajanitāṃśca mahopasargān ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवि · प्रसीद · परिपालय · नः · अरिभीतेःनित्यम् · यथा · असुरवधात् · अधुना · एव · सद्यःपापानि · सर्वजगताम् · प्रशमम् · नय · आशुउत्पातपाकजनितान् · च · महोपसर्गान्

devi · prasīda · paripālaya · naḥ · aribhīteḥnityam · yathā · asuravadhāt · adhunā · eva · sadyaḥpāpāni · sarvajagatām · praśamam · naya · āśuutpātapākajanitān · ca · mahopasargān

Word by word

  1. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  2. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. परिपालयparipālayaprotectपरि + √पाल् (curādi, 10 ‘to protect’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  4. नःnaḥusअस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of परिपालय
  5. अरिभीतेःaribhīteḥfrom fear of enemiesf. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. नित्यम्nityamalwaysindeclinable (adverbial acc. of नित्य)
  7. यथाyathāasindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
  8. असुरवधात्asuravadhātthrough the slaying of the asurasm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of means/cause
  9. अधुनाadhunānowindeclinable (adverb of time)
  10. एवevajustindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  11. सद्यःsadyaḥat once, instantlyindeclinable (adverb)
  12. पापानिpāpānievils, sinsn. acc. pl. of पाप; object of नय
  13. सर्वजगताम्sarvajagatāmof all the worldsn. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  14. प्रशमम्praśamamto rest, to extinctionm. acc. sg. of प्रशम; the goal with नय
  15. नयnayalead, bring√नी (bhvādi, 1U ‘to lead’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  16. आशुāśuquicklyindeclinable (adverb)
  17. उत्पातपाकजनितान्utpātapākajanitānborn of the ripening of portentsm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महोपसर्गान्
  18. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  19. महोपसर्गान्mahopasargāngreat calamitiesm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); second object of नय

Translation

O Devī, be gracious; protect us always from the fear of enemies, as (you did) just now, at once, through the slaying of the asuras; lead quickly to extinction the sins of all the worlds and the great calamities born of the ripening of portents.

In readable EnglishBe gracious, Devī. Guard us always from fear of our foes, as you did just now, at once, by slaying the asuras. Swiftly bring to rest the sins of all the worlds and the great calamities that ripen from evil portents.

‘as you did’ supplies the verb implied by यथा; ‘our’ is supplied with ‘foes’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नोऽरिभीतेर्नित्यं no'ribhīternityaṃ → नः + अरिभीतेः + नित्यम्

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa; visarga as r · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    -aḥ before a becomes -o and absorbs the a (नोऽरि…). After e the visarga of अरिभीतेः stays r before the voiced n. The printed line breaks inside the word (अरिभीते- / र्नित्यं); the word belongs to the first line.

  • यथासुरवधादधुनैव yathāsuravadhādadhunaiva → यथा + असुरवधात् + अधुना + एव

    vowel and consonant sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + a → ā; the final t becomes d before a vowel; ā + e → ai (अधुनैव).

  • नयाशु nayāśu → नय + आशु

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • नयाशु उत्पात nayāśu utpāta → आशु + उत्पात…

    no sandhi at the end of a pāda · —

    आशु ends the third pāda and उत्पात… begins the fourth. The pause between metrical lines is not bridged, so u + u stay apart.

  • उत्पातपाकजनितांश्च utpātapākajanitāṃśca → उत्पातपाकजनितान् + च

    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Final n before c becomes anusvāra + s, and the s becomes ś beside c: -ांश्च.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अरिभीतेः aribhīteḥ

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अरि + भीति
    Vigraha
    अरिभ्यः भीतिः, तस्याः aribhyaḥ bhītiḥ, tasyāḥ
    Literally
    “fear from enemies”
    In the verse
    the dread of hostile powers
    Alternative
    Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, अरीणां भीतिः ‘enemies’ fear’, i.e. fear caused by enemies; the sense is the same.

  • असुरवधात् asuravadhāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + वध
    Vigraha
    असुराणां वधः, तस्मात् asurāṇāṃ vadhaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the slaying of the asuras”
    In the verse
    through your victory over Śumbha and his host

  • सर्वजगताम् sarvajagatām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + जगत्
    Vigraha
    सर्वाणि जगन्ति, तेषाम् sarvāṇi jaganti, teṣām
    Literally
    “of all worlds”
    In the verse
    of every world

  • उत्पातपाकजनितान् utpātapākajanitān

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (उत्पातपाक)

    Members
    उत्पात + पाक + जनित
    Vigraha
    उत्पातानां पाकः उत्पातपाकः; उत्पातपाकेन जनिताः, तान् utpātānāṃ pākaḥ utpātapākaḥ; utpātapākena janitāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “produced by the ripening of portents”
    In the verse
    the disasters that portents foretell, coming to fruition

  • महोपसर्गान् mahopasargān

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + उपसर्ग
    Vigraha
    महान्तः उपसर्गाः, तान् mahāntaḥ upasargāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “great calamities”
    In the verse
    plagues and disasters (महा + उपसर्ग → महोपसर्ग, आद्गुणः 6.1.87)
Morphology & anvaya

  • परिपालय paripālaya

    Imperative second singular of the curādi root √पाल् (stem पालय-), with परि ‘all around’: ‘protect on every side’. The stanza has three imperatives: प्रसीद, परिपालय, नय.

  • नय naya

    Imperative of √नी (thematic stem नय-). With the accusative of the goal प्रशमम्, ‘lead to quiescence’: an idiom for ‘put an end to’.

  • उत्पातपाकजनितान् utpātapākajanitān

    जनित, the last member, is the past participle of the causative of √जन् ‘to be born’: ‘brought to birth, produced’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे देवि, प्रसीद। यथा अधुना एव असुरवधात् सद्यः (अस्मान् अपालयः), (तथा) नित्यं नः अरिभीतेः परिपालय। सर्वजगतां पापानि उत्पातपाकजनितान् महोपसर्गान् च आशु प्रशमं नय।

he devi, prasīda | yathā adhunā eva asuravadhāt sadyaḥ (asmān apālayaḥ), (tathā) nityaṃ naḥ aribhīteḥ paripālaya | sarvajagatāṃ pāpāni utpātapākajanitān mahopasargān ca āśu praśamaṃ naya |

Three imperatives. The second is qualified by a comparison (यथा … सद्यः) whose verb is understood; the third has two objects joined by च and the goal प्रशमम्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last long stanza of the hymn, in vasantatilakā (fourteen syllables a line), the metre of the gods’ hymn in chapter 4, which also opened a stanza with देवि प्रसीद (4.14). The gods ask for continued protection, citing what she has just done, and for the calming of sin and calamity in every world.

Traditional interpretation

The Devī herself promises the same in chapter 12: hearing her story ‘would quell all calamities born of plague, and the threefold portent’ (12.8 उपसर्गानशेषांस्तु महामारीसमुद्भवान्। तथा त्रिविधमुत्पातं माहात्म्यं शमयेन्मम), and she prescribes its hearing in every rite of pacification (12.16–17). The prayer of chapter 11 and the promise of chapter 12 answer each other.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: ‘as you did just now’ — the prayer rests on remembered help. Gratitude for the past becomes trust for the future.

Verse 11.35

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

प्रणतानां प्रसीद त्वं देवि विश्वार्तिहारिणि।त्रैलोक्यवासिनामीड्ये लोकानां वरदा भव॥⁠३५⁠॥

praṇatānāṃ prasīda tvaṃ devi viśvārtihāriṇi |trailokyavāsināmīḍye lokānāṃ varadā bhava ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

प्रणतानाम् · प्रसीद · त्वम् · देवि · विश्वार्तिहारिणित्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् · ईड्ये · लोकानाम् · वरदा · भव

praṇatānām · prasīda · tvam · devi · viśvārtihāriṇitrailokyavāsinām · īḍye · lokānām · varadā · bhava

Word by word

  1. प्रणतानाम्praṇatānāmto those who bow downpast participle (kta) of प्र + √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. gen. pl.; genitive with प्रसीद
  2. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of प्रसीद and भव
  4. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  5. विश्वार्तिहारिणिviśvārtihāriṇiO remover of the world’s afflictionf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम्trailokyavāsināmby the dwellers of the three worldsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); genitive of the agent with ईड्ये
  7. ईड्येīḍyeO you who are to be praisedf. voc. sg. of ईड्या, gerundive (ण्यत्) of √ईड् (adādi, 2Ā ‘to praise’)
  8. लोकानाम्lokānāmfor the worlds, to all peoplem. gen. pl. of लोक; with वरदा
  9. वरदाvaradāgiver of boonsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of भव
  10. भवbhavabe√भू (bhvādi, 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Be gracious, you, to those who bow down, O Devī, remover of the world’s affliction; O you who are to be praised by the dwellers of the three worlds, be the giver of boons to the worlds.

In readable EnglishBe gracious to those who bow before you, Devī, who take away the sorrow of the world. Praised by all who dwell in the three worlds, be the giver of boons to all the worlds.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रणतानां प्रसीद त्वं देवि praṇatānāṃ prasīda tvaṃ devi → प्रणतानाम् + प्रसीद; त्वम् + देवि

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; the same in लोकानां वरदा.

  • विश्वार्तिहारिणि viśvārtihāriṇi → विश्व + आर्ति + हारिणि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनामीड्ये trailokyavāsināmīḍye → त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् + ईड्ये

    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant

    The m stays and is written together with the following vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • विश्वार्तिहारिणि viśvārtihāriṇi

    upapada tatpuruṣa (हारिन् = √हृ with the agent affix -इन्, feminine -ई) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (विश्वार्ति)

    Members
    विश्व + आर्ति + हारिन्
    Vigraha
    विश्वस्य आर्तिः विश्वार्तिः; विश्वार्तिं हरति इति विश्वार्तिहारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ viśvasya ārtiḥ viśvārtiḥ; viśvārtiṃ harati iti viśvārtihāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “she who takes away the affliction of the world”
    In the verse
    remover of all suffering

  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् trailokyavāsinām

    upapada tatpuruṣa (वासिन् = √वस् with the agent affix -इन्)

    Members
    त्रैलोक्य + वासिन्
    Vigraha
    त्रैलोक्ये वसन्ति इति त्रैलोक्यवासिनः, तेषाम् trailokye vasanti iti trailokyavāsinaḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of those who dwell in the three worlds”
    In the verse
    all beings, gods and men

  • वरदा varadā

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√दा with the affix -अ)

    Members
    वर + दा
    Vigraha
    वरं ददाति इति वरदा varaṃ dadāti iti varadā
    Literally
    “boon-giver”
    In the verse
    she who grants what is asked
Morphology & anvaya

  • ईड्ये īḍye

    Gerundive of √ईड् ‘to praise’: ईड्य ‘to be praised’, feminine ईड्या, vocative ईड्ये. With a gerundive the agent may stand in the genitive (त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् ‘by the dwellers of the three worlds’).

  • प्रणतानाम् praṇatānām

    Genitive plural of the past participle प्रणत ‘bowed down’ (the n of √नम् becomes ṇ after the r of the prefix प्र). प्रसीद takes the genitive of the person favoured: ‘be gracious to those who bow’.

  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् trailokyavāsinām

    त्रैलोक्य ‘the three worlds’ is a taddhita (with vṛddhi of the first vowel) from त्रिलोक; वासिन् ‘dweller’ is an agent noun of √वस् ‘to dwell’.

Anvaya · prose order

हे विश्वार्तिहारिणि त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् ईड्ये देवि, त्वं प्रणतानां प्रसीद, लोकानां वरदा भव।

he viśvārtihāriṇi trailokyavāsinām īḍye devi, tvaṃ praṇatānāṃ prasīda, lokānāṃ varadā bhava |

Two imperatives addressed to one subject (त्वम्), each with a genitive of the persons concerned (प्रणतानाम्, लोकानाम्); three vocatives name her.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last verse of the gods’ hymn (the Nārāyaṇī Stuti, 11.3–35) returns to the anuṣṭubh and closes with a request: ‘be the giver of boons’ (वरदा भव). The Devī answers at once in the next verses with the same word: वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ (11.37), ‘I am the giver of boons, hosts of gods; choose the boon you wish’.

Traditional interpretation

The hymn is framed by one prayer. It opened with देवि प्रपन्नार्तिहरे प्रसीद (11.3), ‘O Devī who remove the affliction of those who take refuge, be gracious’, and it ends with प्रसीद … विश्वार्तिहारिणि: the remover of the suppliants’ pain is now the remover of the world’s.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name वरदा (name 331). A shared epithet; separate works.

Verse 11.36

Speaker
‹›

देव्युवाच॥⁠३६⁠॥

devyuvāca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवी · उवाच

devī · uvāca

Word by word

  1. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); subject of उवाच
  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

The Goddess said:

In readable EnglishThe Goddess said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देव्युवाच devyuvāca → देवी + उवाच

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final ī before the unlike vowel u becomes y: devy uvāca.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • उवाच uvāca

    Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplication (उ-वाच), as in 1.1: the narrative past, ‘(she) said’.

Anvaya · prose order

देवी उवाच।

devī uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn of the gods (11.2–35) is over, and the Goddess answers it. This is her first speech since the death of Śumbha; within the frame it is still Medhas who reports it to Suratha and Samādhi (the ṛṣi of 11.1).

Verse 11.37

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ।तं वृणुध्वं प्रयच्छामि जगतामुपकारकम्॥⁠३७⁠॥

varadāhaṃ suragaṇā varaṃ yanmanasecchatha |taṃ vṛṇudhvaṃ prayacchāmi jagatāmupakārakam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

वरदा · अहम् · सुरगणाः · वरम् · यत् · मनसा · इच्छथतम् · वृणुध्वम् · प्रयच्छामि · जगताम् · उपकारकम्

varadā · aham · suragaṇāḥ · varam · yat · manasā · icchathatam · vṛṇudhvam · prayacchāmi · jagatām · upakārakam

Word by word

  1. वरदाvaradāgiver of boonsf. nom. sg. of वरदा (वरद + ṭāp); compound (see Samāsa); predicate of अहम्, with (अस्मि) understood
  2. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of the nominal sentence
  3. सुरगणाःsuragaṇāḥO hosts of godsm. voc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); address
  4. वरम्varamboonm. acc. sg. of वर; object of इच्छथ inside the relative clause, and taken up by तम्
  5. यत्yatwhich, whateverrelative pronoun यद्, n. acc. sg., used generically (‘whatever’) with the masculine वरम्; correlative तम्
  6. मनसाmanasāin (your) mind, at heartn. instr. sg. of मनस् (s-stem); instrument with इच्छथ
  7. इच्छथicchathayou desire√इष् (tudādi, 6P ‘to wish’, present stem इच्छ-), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. तम्tamthat (boon)pronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; correlative of यत्, agreeing with वरम्; object of वृणुध्वम्
  9. वृणुध्वम्vṛṇudhvamchoose√वृ (वृञ्, svādi, 5U ‘to choose’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
  10. प्रयच्छामिprayacchāmiI grant, I shall giveप्र + √दा (bhvādi, 1P, present stem यच्छ-), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. जगताम्jagatāmto the worlds, for the worldsn. gen. pl. of जगत्; with उपकारकम्
  12. उपकारकम्upakārakambeneficial, helpfulm. acc. sg. of उपकारक (उप + √कृ + ṇvul, agent noun in -aka); qualifies तम् (वरम्)

Translation

I am the giver of boons, O hosts of gods. The boon which you desire in your mind — choose that, beneficial to the worlds; I grant it.

In readable EnglishI am the giver of boons, you hosts of gods. Choose the boon your minds desire, one that will benefit the worlds, and I shall grant it.

The object of प्रयच्छामि is supplied (‘it’), and the present is rendered as a promise (‘I shall grant’).

Textual verification / uncertain: यत् … तम्: the relative is neuter and the correlative masculine. Read here as a generic ‘whatever (boon)’; it may also be a loose agreement for यम्. The sense is the same either way.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वरदाहं varadāhaṃ → वरदा + अहम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā. The final m of अहम् becomes anusvāra before the s of सुरगणाः (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • सुरगणा वरं suragaṇā varaṃ → सुरगणाः + वरम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before the voiced v: the visarga (as ru) becomes y, and before a consonant that y is always lost, leaving -ā.

  • यन्मनसेच्छथ yanmanasecchatha → यत् + मनसा + इच्छथ

    consonant sandhi (nasalisation); vowel sandhi (guṇa) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Final t (voiced to d at word end, 8.2.39) becomes the nasal n before the m of मनसा; then ā + i → e (मनसा + इच्छथ → मनसेच्छथ).

  • जगतामुपकारकम् jagatāmupakārakam → जगताम् + उपकारकम्

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m stands before the vowel u and is written with it.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • वरदा varadā

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    वर + द
    Vigraha
    वरं ददाति इति वरदा varaṃ dadāti iti varadā
    Literally
    “she who gives boons”
    In the verse
    the bestower of boons — the title with which she offers the gods their wish

  • सुरगणाः suragaṇāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    सुर + गण
    Vigraha
    सुराणां गणाः surāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally
    “the hosts of the gods”
    In the verse
    the assembled gods, addressed together
Morphology & anvaya

  • वृणुध्वम् vṛṇudhvam

    Imperative, 2nd plural, ātmanepada, of √वृ (svādi, stem वृणु-): ‘choose for yourselves’. The middle voice suits a choice made for one’s own benefit.

  • प्रयच्छामि prayacchāmi

    √दा forms its present on the substitute stem यच्छ-; with प्र, ‘to give, to grant’. The present stands for the immediate future: ‘I (will) grant it’.

  • इच्छथ icchatha

    Present of √इष् (tudādi), stem इच्छ-, 2nd plural: ‘you wish’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सुरगणाः, अहं वरदा (अस्मि)। यत् वरं मनसा इच्छथ, जगताम् उपकारकं तं वृणुध्वम्; (तं) प्रयच्छामि।

(he) suragaṇāḥ, ahaṃ varadā (asmi) | yat varaṃ manasā icchatha, jagatām upakārakaṃ taṃ vṛṇudhvam; (taṃ) prayacchāmi |

A nominal sentence (अहं वरदा) and the address; then a relative clause (यत् वरं … इच्छथ) picked up by तम्, the object of the imperative वृणुध्वम्; प्रयच्छामि closes with an implied object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Pleased by the hymn, the Goddess offers a boon, as she did after the hymn of chapter 4 (4.32). She sets one condition herself: the boon is to be जगताम् उपकारकम्, of benefit to the worlds, not to the gods alone.

Traditional interpretation

Each of the text’s great hymns is followed by a boon (1.95–98 to Viṣṇu from the asuras, 4.32–37 and here). In the Śākta reading the pattern teaches that praise is answered: the Goddess is वरदा, the one who grants what is asked.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) gives the same epithet as a name: 331, वरदा. Here it is the Goddess’s own word for herself in the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa; the shared title is a shared Śākta idea, not a borrowing between the texts.

देवा ऊचुः॥⁠३८⁠॥

Word by word

  1. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
  2. ऊचुःūcuḥsaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देवा ऊचुः devā ūcuḥ → देवाः + ऊचुः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y and the y is dropped; the two vowels then stand apart and are not joined again.

Verse 11.39

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनं त्रैलोक्यस्याखिलेश्वरि।एवमेव त्वया कार्यमस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्॥⁠३९⁠॥

sarvābādhāpraśamanaṃ trailokyasyākhileśvari |evameva tvayā kāryamasmadvairivināśanam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् · त्रैलोक्यस्य · अखिलेश्वरिएवम् · एव · त्वया · कार्यम् · अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्

sarvābādhāpraśamanam · trailokyasya · akhileśvarievam · eva · tvayā · kāryam · asmadvairivināśanam

Word by word

  1. सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम्sarvābādhāpraśamanamthe quelling of all afflictionsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of (भवतु) कार्यम्, together with अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्
  2. त्रैलोक्यस्यtrailokyasyaof the three worldsn. gen. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोकी/त्रिलोक: ‘the triad of worlds’); with the afflictions
  3. अखिलेश्वरिakhileśvariO Queen of allf. voc. sg. of अखिलेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)
  4. एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  5. एवevajust, exactlyindeclinable (emphatic particle); with एवम्, ‘in just this way’
  6. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent with the gerundive कार्यम्
  7. कार्यम्kāryamis to be done, must be donegerundive (kṛtya, ṇyat) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), n. nom. sg.; predicate
  8. अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्asmadvairivināśanamthe destruction of our enemiesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second subject of कार्यम्

Translation

O Queen of all, the quelling of all afflictions of the three worlds — and in just this way the destruction of our enemies — is to be done by you.

In readable EnglishO Sovereign of all, quell every affliction of the three worlds, and destroy our enemies just as you have done now.

The gerundive (‘is to be done by you’) is rendered as a request; ‘as you have done now’ interprets एवम् एव, ‘in just this way’.

Textual verification / uncertain: सर्वाबाधा: read as सर्व + आबाधा ‘all afflictions’ (the usual reading); सर्व + अबाधा would make no sense here.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनं sarvābādhāpraśamanaṃ → सर्व + आबाधा + प्रशमनम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā (सर्व + आबाधा → सर्वाबाधा). The final m becomes anusvāra before the t of त्रैलोक्यस्य (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • त्रैलोक्यस्याखिलेश्वरि trailokyasyākhileśvari → त्रैलोक्यस्य + अखिलेश्वरि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + a → ā. Inside the vocative compound, अखिल + ईश्वरि gives -e- by guṇa (आद्गुणः, 6.1.87).

  • एवमेव evameva → एवम् + एव

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m is written with the following e. The same in कार्यमस्मद्.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् sarvābādhāpraśamanam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member सर्वाबाधा is a karmadhāraya: सर्वाः च ताः आबाधाः च)

    Members
    सर्वाबाधा + प्रशमन
    Vigraha
    सर्वासाम् आबाधानां प्रशमनम् sarvāsām ābādhānāṃ praśamanam
    Literally
    “the calming of all afflictions”
    In the verse
    the removal of every trouble that besets the three worlds

  • अखिलेश्वरि akhileśvari

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अखिल + ईश्वरी
    Vigraha
    अखिलस्य ईश्वरी akhilasya īśvarī
    Literally
    “ruler of the whole”
    In the verse
    sovereign of everything

  • अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् asmadvairivināśanam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अस्मद्वैरि)

    Members
    अस्मद् + वैरि + विनाशन
    Vigraha
    अस्माकं वैरिणः अस्मद्वैरिणः; तेषां विनाशनम् asmākaṃ vairiṇaḥ asmadvairiṇaḥ; teṣāṃ vināśanam
    Literally
    “the destruction of the enemies of us”
    In the verse
    the slaying of our enemies — as she has just slain Śumbha and Niśumbha
Morphology & anvaya

  • कार्यम् kāryam

    Gerundive of √कृ with ṇyat (कृ + य, vṛddhi → कार्य): ‘to be done’. The agent goes in the instrumental (त्वया), the thing to be done is the grammatical subject; neuter singular agrees with प्रशमनम् and विनाशनम् taken together as one task.

  • त्रैलोक्यस्य trailokyasya

    Taddhita with vṛddhi of the first syllable: त्रि-लोक → त्रैलोक्य ‘the three worlds as a whole’ (neuter collective).

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) अखिलेश्वरि, त्रैलोक्यस्य सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् (तथा) अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् एवम् एव त्वया कार्यम्।

(he) akhileśvari, trailokyasya sarvābādhāpraśamanam (tathā) asmadvairivināśanam evam eva tvayā kāryam |

A passive construction with the gerundive: the two nouns of action are the subjects, कार्यम् the predicate, त्वया the agent; एवम् एव points back to what she has just done.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods ask for two things: that every affliction of the three worlds be quelled, and that their enemies be destroyed एवम् एव, ‘in just this way’ — as Śumbha and Niśumbha have just been. They ask not for one deliverance but for a standing promise.

Traditional interpretation

The request matches the Goddess’s own condition in 11.37: the boon is for the three worlds, not for the gods alone. Her answer (11.41–55) turns it into the prophecy of her future descents.
