Direct textual meaning

The Goddess grants the gods’ request. What follows (11.41–55) is her prophecy: the future forms in which she will come down to destroy the enemies of the worlds. The speech runs to the end of the chapter; chapter 12 continues it with the fruits of hearing the text.

Verse 11.41

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे प्राप्ते अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे।शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चैवान्यावुत्पत्स्येते महासुरौ॥⁠४१⁠॥

vaivasvate'ntare prāpte aṣṭāviṃśatime yuge |śumbho niśumbhaścaivānyāvutpatsyete mahāsurau ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

वैवस्वते · अन्तरे · प्राप्ते · अष्टाविंशतिमे · युगेशुम्भः · निशुम्भः · च · एव · अन्यौ · उत्पत्स्येते · महासुरौ

vaivasvate · antare · prāpte · aṣṭāviṃśatime · yugeśumbhaḥ · niśumbhaḥ · ca · eva · anyau · utpatsyete · mahāsurau

Word by word

  1. वैवस्वतेvaivasvatein the Vaivasvata (age)n. loc. sg. of वैवस्वत (taddhita from विवस्वत् ‘the Sun’: belonging to Vaivasvata, the seventh Manu); qualifies अन्तरे
  2. अन्तरेantaremanvantara, intervaln. loc. sg. of अन्तर; short for मन्वन्तरे, as in 1.4; locative absolute with प्राप्ते
  3. प्राप्तेprāptehaving come, when (it) has arrivedpast passive participle (kta) of प्र + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to reach’), n. loc. sg.; predicate of the locative absolute
  4. अष्टाविंशतिमेaṣṭāviṃśatimein the twenty-eighthordinal of अष्टाविंशति ‘twenty-eight’, n. loc. sg.; qualifies युगे
  5. युगेyugein the yuga, the agen. loc. sg. of युग; here one great cycle (mahāyuga) of the manvantara
  6. शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject (with निशुम्भः) of उत्पत्स्येते
  7. निशुम्भःniśumbhaḥNiśumbham. nom. sg.; proper name
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  9. एवevaindeed, likewiseindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  10. अन्यौanyautwo others, another (pair)pronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. du.; qualifies the pair शुम्भः निशुम्भः
  11. उत्पत्स्येतेutpatsyetewill arise, will be bornउद् + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to go’; with उद् ‘to arise’), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, ātmanepada
  12. महासुरौmahāsurautwo great asurasm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to the pair

Translation

When the Vaivasvata manvantara has come, in the twenty-eighth yuga, two others, Śumbha and Niśumbha, great asuras, will arise.

In readable EnglishIn the age of Vaivasvata Manu, in its twenty-eighth cycle, two other great asuras named Śumbha and Niśumbha will be born.

‘Cycle’ renders युग, which here means a whole round of four ages; ‘named’ interprets अन्यौ: a second pair bearing the same names.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे vaivasvate'ntare → वैवस्वते + अन्तरे

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is absorbed (avagraha). The same junction is left open at प्राप्ते अष्टा- (see sandhi_exceptions).

  • शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चैवान्यावुत्पत्स्येते śumbho niśumbhaścaivānyāvutpatsyete → शुम्भः + निशुम्भः + च + एव + अन्यौ + उत्पत्स्येते

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)

    शुम्भः before the voiced n becomes शुम्भो; निशुम्भः before c becomes निशुम्भश्; च + एव → चैव (a + e → ai); चैव + अन्यौ → चैवान्यौ (a + a → ā); अन्यौ before u becomes अन्याव् (au → āv); that v could optionally be dropped (लोपः शाकल्यस्य, 8.3.19, giving अन्या उत्-), but the text keeps it.

Left unjoined

  • प्राप्ते | अष्टाविंशतिमे — Hiatus at the pāda boundary: वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे प्राप्ते is a complete eight-syllable pāda and अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे the next. Eliding the a (प्राप्तेऽष्टाविंशतिमे) would take a syllable from the second pāda; the verse, like much Purāṇic verse, leaves the vowels apart across the pāda break (a metrical licence), and the Gita Press prints them so.
Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरौ mahāsurau

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तौ च तौ असुरौ च mahāntau ca tau asurau ca
    Literally
    “two great asuras”
    In the verse
    a mighty demon pair (महत् becomes महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)
Morphology & anvaya

  • उत्पत्स्येते utpatsyete

    Future of उद् + √पद् (aniṭ): stem पत्स्य- (d → t before s), dual ātmanepada -इते → -एते after the stem vowel: ‘(the two) will arise’. The first future verb of the prophecy.

  • अष्टाविंशतिमे aṣṭāviṃśatime

    An ordinal in -म from अष्टाविंशति. The regular ordinals of the twenties are अष्टाविंश and अष्टाविंशतितम; the shortened अष्टाविंशतिम is a Purāṇic form, kept by the metre.

  • प्राप्ते prāpte

    Past participle of प्र + √आप् in the locative absolute with अन्तरे: ‘the Vaivasvata age having come’, i.e. in the Vaivasvata age.

Anvaya · prose order

वैवस्वते अन्तरे प्राप्ते अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे अन्यौ शुम्भः निशुम्भः च एव महासुरौ उत्पत्स्येते।

vaivasvate antare prāpte aṣṭāviṃśatime yuge anyau śumbhaḥ niśumbhaḥ ca eva mahāsurau utpatsyete |

Time is given by a locative absolute (वैवस्वते अन्तरे प्राप्ते) and a locative (अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे); the subject is the pair शुम्भः निशुम्भः च with अन्यौ and महासुरौ; the verb is the dual future उत्पत्स्येते.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The prophecy begins with a date. The present story is set long before; the Goddess now looks ahead to the seventh manvantara, that of Vaivasvata Manu, and to its twenty-eighth great cycle, when a second Śumbha and Niśumbha will be born. Her answer to the gods’ request (11.39) is a promise to return.

Traditional interpretation

In Purāṇic reckoning the present world-age is the twenty-eighth great cycle of the Vaivasvata manvantara, the cycle in which Kṛṣṇa was born. The next verse confirms the setting: the Goddess will be born in Nanda’s house.

Verse 11.42

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नन्दगोपगृहे जाता यशोदागर्भसम्भवा।ततस्तौ नाशयिष्यामि विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी॥⁠४२⁠॥

nandagopagṛhe jātā yaśodāgarbhasambhavā |tatastau nāśayiṣyāmi vindhyācalanivāsinī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

नन्दगोपगृहे · जाता · यशोदागर्भसम्भवाततः · तौ · नाशयिष्यामि · विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी

nandagopagṛhe · jātā · yaśodāgarbhasambhavātataḥ · tau · nāśayiṣyāmi · vindhyācalanivāsinī

Word by word

  1. नन्दगोपगृहेnandagopagṛhein the house of Nanda the cowherdn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); place of जाता
  2. जाताjātābornpast participle (kta) of √जन् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to be born’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the subject ‘I’ (अहम्, understood from the verb)
  3. यशोदागर्भसम्भवाyaśodāgarbhasambhavāborn from the womb of Yaśodāf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject ‘I’
  4. ततःtataḥthen, thereafterindeclinable (adverb of time, तद् + tas)
  5. तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. acc. du.; object of नाशयिष्यामि (Śumbha and Niśumbha)
  6. नाशयिष्यामिnāśayiṣyāmiI shall destroycausative of √नश् (divādi, 4P ‘to perish’; causative ‘to destroy’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनीvindhyācalanivāsinīdwelling on the Vindhya mountainf. nom. sg. of विन्ध्याचलनिवासिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject

Translation

Born in the house of Nanda the cowherd, born from the womb of Yaśodā, then, dwelling on the Vindhya mountain, I shall destroy those two.

In readable EnglishBorn in the house of Nanda the cowherd, from the womb of Yaśodā, I shall then dwell on the Vindhya mountain and destroy them both.

The participial nominative विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी is turned into a finite clause (‘I shall dwell’).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततस्तौ tatastau → ततः + तौ

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी vindhyācalanivāsinī → विन्ध्य + अचल + निवासिनी

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā (विन्ध्य + अचल → विन्ध्याचल).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नन्दगोपगृहे nandagopagṛhe

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member नन्दगोप is a karmadhāraya: नन्दः च असौ गोपः च)

    Members
    नन्दगोप + गृह
    Vigraha
    नन्दगोपस्य गृहम्, तस्मिन् nandagopasya gṛham, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the house of Nanda the cowherd”
    In the verse
    in the cowherd settlement where Kṛṣṇa grew up

  • यशोदागर्भसम्भवा yaśodāgarbhasambhavā

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (यशोदागर्भ)

    Members
    यशोदागर्भ + सम्भव
    Vigraha
    यशोदायाः गर्भः यशोदागर्भः; यशोदागर्भात् सम्भवः यस्याः सा yaśodāyāḥ garbhaḥ yaśodāgarbhaḥ; yaśodāgarbhāt sambhavaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose birth is from Yaśodā’s womb”
    In the verse
    born as Yaśodā’s daughter
    Alternative
    Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa of the upapada kind, यशोदागर्भात् सम्भवति इति, ‘arising from Yaśodā’s womb’; the sense is the same. The bahuvrīhi matches चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः in 1.4.

  • विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी vindhyācalanivāsinī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (saptamī; the agent suffix -in of habit), its first member a karmadhāraya: विन्ध्यः च असौ अचलः च

    Members
    विन्ध्याचल + निवासिन्
    Vigraha
    विन्ध्याचले निवसति इति (शीला) vindhyācale nivasati iti (śīlā)
    Literally
    “she who dwells on the Vindhya mountain”
    In the verse
    Vindhyavāsinī, the Goddess of the Vindhya hills
Morphology & anvaya

  • नाशयिष्यामि nāśayiṣyāmi

    Causative stem नाशय- (√नश् with vṛddhi + णिच्) with the future suffix -इष्य- and the 1st singular -आमि: ‘I shall cause to perish’.

  • जाता jātā

    Past participle of √जन्: the root’s n is replaced by ā (ज + आ + त → जात), feminine जाता. Used as a finite verb would be: ‘(I,) born …’.

Anvaya · prose order

(अहं) नन्दगोपगृहे यशोदागर्भसम्भवा जाता विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी (सती) ततः तौ नाशयिष्यामि।

(ahaṃ) nandagopagṛhe yaśodāgarbhasambhavā jātā vindhyācalanivāsinī (satī) tataḥ tau nāśayiṣyāmi |

The subject is the ‘I’ of the verb; the three feminine nominatives (जाता, यशोदागर्भसम्भवा, विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी) describe her; ततः तौ नाशयिष्यामि is the main clause.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first future form: the Goddess will be born to Yaśodā in the house of Nanda the cowherd, and, dwelling on the Vindhya hills, will slay the second Śumbha and Niśumbha. The text names no new name here; the epithet विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी serves as one.

Traditional interpretation

This is the daughter of Yaśodā whom the Purāṇic Kṛṣṇa story (Harivaṃśa, Bhāgavata) tells of: exchanged for the infant Kṛṣṇa, she escapes the hands of Kaṃsa and goes to dwell on the Vindhya. The tradition identifies her with Yogamāyā and with Vindhyavāsinī, still worshipped on the Vindhya hills.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the identical compound as a name: 336, विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी. The two texts are distinct; they share the cult of the Vindhya Goddess.

Verse 11.43

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

पुनरप्यतिरौद्रेण रूपेण पृथिवीतले।अवतीर्य हनिष्यामि वैप्रचित्तांस्तु दानवान्॥⁠४३⁠॥

punarapyatiraudreṇa rūpeṇa pṛthivītale |avatīrya haniṣyāmi vaipracittāṃstu dānavān ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

पुनः · अपि · अतिरौद्रेण · रूपेण · पृथिवीतलेअवतीर्य · हनिष्यामि · वैप्रचित्तान् · तु · दानवान्

punaḥ · api · atiraudreṇa · rūpeṇa · pṛthivītaleavatīrya · haniṣyāmi · vaipracittān · tu · dānavān

Word by word

  1. पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb); its visarga stands for r
  2. अपिapialso, once moreindeclinable (particle); with पुनः, ‘yet again’
  3. अतिरौद्रेणatiraudreṇaexceedingly terriblen. instr. sg. of अतिरौद्र; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपेण
  4. रूपेणrūpeṇain a form, with a formn. instr. sg. of रूप; instrumental of manner (‘in a form’)
  5. पृथिवीतलेpṛthivītaleon the surface of the earthn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); place of अवतीर्य
  6. अवतीर्यavatīryahaving descendedabsolutive (lyap) of अव + √तॄ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to cross’; with अव ‘to descend’)
  7. हनिष्यामिhaniṣyāmiI shall slay√हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. वैप्रचित्तान्vaipracittānthe Vaipracittas, descendants of Vipracittim. acc. pl. of वैप्रचित्त (taddhita aṇ of descent from विप्रचित्ति); qualifies दानवान्
  9. तुtuand, in turnindeclinable (particle, connective)
  10. दानवान्dānavānDānavas (demons, sons of Danu)m. acc. pl. of दानव (taddhita from दनु); object of हनिष्यामि

Translation

Yet again, having descended in an exceedingly terrible form onto the surface of the earth, I shall slay the Dānavas, the descendants of Vipracitti.

In readable EnglishAnd again, descending to earth in a form of utter ferocity, I shall slay the Dānavas born of Vipracitti.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पुनरप्यतिरौद्रेण punarapyatiraudreṇa → पुनः + अपि + अतिरौद्रेण

    visarga sandhi (r restored); vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    The visarga of पुनः stands for an original r, which reappears before a vowel (पुनर् + अपि). Then i before the unlike vowel a becomes y (अपि + अति → अप्यति).

  • वैप्रचित्तांस्तु vaipracittāṃstu → वैप्रचित्तान् + तु

    consonant sandhi (n before t) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)

    Final n before t becomes ṃs: the vowel is nasalised (written as anusvāra) and an s is inserted.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिरौद्रेण atiraudreṇa

    prādi tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अति + रौद्र
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन रौद्रम्, तेन atiśayena raudram, tena
    Literally
    “exceedingly fierce”
    In the verse
    terrible beyond measure — the form that will devour the Vaipracittas (11.44)

  • पृथिवीतले pṛthivītale

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पृथिवी + तल
    Vigraha
    पृथिव्याः तलम्, तस्मिन् pṛthivyāḥ talam, tasmin
    Literally
    “on the surface of the earth”
    In the verse
    on earth, among men
Morphology & anvaya

  • अवतीर्य avatīrya

    Absolutive with a prefix (-य, lyap) of अव + √तॄ; the root vowel ṝ becomes īr (तीर्): ‘having descended’. The verb from which avatāra, ‘descent’, is formed.

  • हनिष्यामि haniṣyāmi

    Future of √हन् with the connecting vowel i (हन् + इष्य + आमि): ‘I shall kill’.

  • वैप्रचित्तान् vaipracittān

    Taddhita of descent with vṛddhi of the first syllable: विप्रचित्ति → वैप्रचित्त ‘son or descendant of Vipracitti’, a Dānava chief.

Anvaya · prose order

पुनः अपि अतिरौद्रेण रूपेण पृथिवीतले अवतीर्य (अहं) वैप्रचित्तान् दानवान् तु हनिष्यामि।

punaḥ api atiraudreṇa rūpeṇa pṛthivītale avatīrya (ahaṃ) vaipracittān dānavān tu haniṣyāmi |

The absolutive अवतीर्य, with its manner (अतिरौद्रेण रूपेण) and place (पृथिवीतले), precedes the main verb हनिष्यामि and its object वैप्रचित्तान् दानवान्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second descent. The Goddess will come down to earth in a most terrible form to kill the Vaipracittas, the Dānava descendants of Vipracitti. The verb अवतीर्य, ‘having descended’, names what the whole passage describes: her avatāras.

Traditional interpretation

The form is named only in the next verse, from its result: her teeth will be red as pomegranate flowers, and she will be called Raktadantikā (11.44–45).

Verse 11.44

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भक्षयन्त्याश्च तानुग्रान् वैप्रचित्तान्महासुरान्।रक्ता दन्ता भविष्यन्ति दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः॥⁠४४⁠॥

bhakṣayantyāśca tānugrān vaipracittānmahāsurān |raktā dantā bhaviṣyanti dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भक्षयन्त्याः · च · तान् · उग्रान् · वैप्रचित्तान् · महासुरान्रक्ताः · दन्ताः · भविष्यन्ति · दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः

bhakṣayantyāḥ · ca · tān · ugrān · vaipracittān · mahāsurānraktāḥ · dantāḥ · bhaviṣyanti · dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ

Word by word

  1. भक्षयन्त्याःbhakṣayantyāḥof (me) as I devourpresent participle (śatṛ) of √भक्ष् (curādi, 10, stem भक्षय- ‘to eat’), f. gen. sg. (भक्षयन्ती); possessive with दन्ताः, ‘my’ understood
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. तान्tānthosepronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies the asuras
  4. उग्रान्ugrānfiercem. acc. pl. of उग्र; qualifies महासुरान्
  5. वैप्रचित्तान्vaipracittānthe Vaipracittasm. acc. pl. of वैप्रचित्त (as in 11.43); qualifies महासुरान्
  6. महासुरान्mahāsurāngreat asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of भक्षयन्त्याः
  7. रक्ताःraktāḥredpast participle (kta) of √रञ्ज् (‘to be coloured’), m. nom. pl.; predicate with भविष्यन्ति
  8. दन्ताःdantāḥteethm. nom. pl. of दन्त; subject
  9. भविष्यन्तिbhaviṣyantiwill become√भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाःdāḍimīkusumopamāḥlike pomegranate flowersm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दन्ताः

Translation

And of (me) as I devour those fierce Vaipracitta great asuras, the teeth will become red, like pomegranate flowers.

In readable EnglishAnd as I devour those fierce great asuras of Vipracitti’s line, my teeth will turn red, like the flowers of the pomegranate.

‘My’ renders the genitive feminine participle, which refers to the speaker.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • भक्षयन्त्याश्च bhakṣayantyāśca → भक्षयन्त्याः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.

  • तानुग्रान् tānugrān → तान् + उग्रान्

    no change (final n before a vowel) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply

    The n is not doubled, because the vowel before it (ā) is long; it is simply written with उग्रान्.

  • रक्ता दन्ता भविष्यन्ति raktā dantā bhaviṣyanti → रक्ताः + दन्ताः + भविष्यन्ति

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant (d, bh): the visarga (as ru) becomes y, and before a consonant that y is always lost, leaving -ā.

  • दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ → दाडिमीकुसुम + उपमाः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरान् mahāsurān

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तः च ते असुराः च, तान् mahāntaḥ ca te asurāḥ ca, tān
    Literally
    “great asuras”
    In the verse
    the mighty demons

  • दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दाडिमीकुसुम)

    Members
    दाडिमीकुसुम + उपमा
    Vigraha
    दाडिम्याः कुसुमं दाडिमीकुसुमम्; दाडिमीकुसुमम् उपमा येषां ते dāḍimyāḥ kusumaṃ dāḍimīkusumam; dāḍimīkusumam upamā yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “whose likeness is the pomegranate flower”
    In the verse
    red as pomegranate blossoms
    Alternative
    उपम may also be taken as the adjective ‘like’, giving a tatpuruṣa दाडिमीकुसुमैः उपमाः ‘comparable to pomegranate flowers’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • भक्षयन्त्याः bhakṣayantyāḥ

    Present participle of the curādi stem भक्षय-: भक्षयत्, feminine भक्षयन्ती (with the nasal), genitive singular -याः. It refers to the speaker: ‘of (me) devouring’, so ‘my teeth’.

  • रक्ताः raktāḥ

    Past participle of √रञ्ज्, the nasal lost and j → k before t (रक्त), used as the adjective ‘red’.

Anvaya · prose order

तान् उग्रान् वैप्रचित्तान् महासुरान् भक्षयन्त्याः च (मम) दन्ताः दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः रक्ताः भविष्यन्ति।

tān ugrān vaipracittān mahāsurān bhakṣayantyāḥ ca (mama) dantāḥ dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ raktāḥ bhaviṣyanti |

The genitive participial phrase (भक्षयन्त्याः … महासुरान्) gives the owner of the teeth; the main clause is दन्ताः रक्ताः भविष्यन्ति with the comparison दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The terrible form of 11.43 devours the Vaipracittas, and her teeth are stained red like pomegranate flowers. The simile is visual and exact: the red of the blossom, not of blood, is the image the text chooses.

Traditional interpretation

The feature gives the form its name in the next verse, Raktadantikā, ‘the Red-toothed’. The devouring recalls Cāmuṇḍā (Kālī) in the Raktabīja battle, told to range the field ‘devouring’ the asuras born of his blood — the same participle, भक्षयन्ती (8.55; the battle 8.53–61).

Verse 11.45

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततो मां देवताः स्वर्गे मर्त्यलोके च मानवाः।स्तुवन्तो व्याहरिष्यन्ति सततं रक्तदन्तिकाम्॥⁠४५⁠॥

tato māṃ devatāḥ svarge martyaloke ca mānavāḥ |stuvanto vyāhariṣyanti satataṃ raktadantikām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · माम् · देवताः · स्वर्गे · मर्त्यलोके · च · मानवाःस्तुवन्तः · व्याहरिष्यन्ति · सततम् · रक्तदन्तिकाम्

tataḥ · mām · devatāḥ · svarge · martyaloke · ca · mānavāḥstuvantaḥ · vyāhariṣyanti · satatam · raktadantikām

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥtherefore, from then onindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tas)
  2. माम्māmmefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. sg.; object of व्याहरिष्यन्ति
  3. देवताःdevatāḥthe deitiesf. nom. pl. of देवता; subject (with मानवाः)
  4. स्वर्गेsvargein heavenm. loc. sg. of स्वर्ग
  5. मर्त्यलोकेmartyalokein the world of mortalsm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  7. मानवाःmānavāḥhuman beings (lit. ‘descendants of Manu’)m. nom. pl. of मानव (taddhita from मनु); subject
  8. स्तुवन्तःstuvantaḥpraisingpresent participle (śatṛ) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies the subjects
  9. व्याहरिष्यन्तिvyāhariṣyantiwill call, will nameवि + आ + √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take’; with वि-आ ‘to utter, to call’), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. सततम्satatamconstantly, everindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत)
  11. रक्तदन्तिकाम्raktadantikāmRaktadantikā, ‘the Red-toothed’f. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate accusative with माम्

Translation

Therefore the deities in heaven and the humans in the world of mortals, praising me, will constantly call me Raktadantikā.

In readable EnglishSo the gods in heaven and men on earth will praise me and call me ever by the name Raktadantikā, the Red-toothed.

‘The Red-toothed’ glosses the name; ‘by the name’ makes the double accusative explicit.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततो मां tato māṃ → ततः + माम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o. The final m of माम् becomes anusvāra before d (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • स्तुवन्तो व्याहरिष्यन्ति stuvanto vyāhariṣyanti → स्तुवन्तः + व्याहरिष्यन्ति

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.

  • देवताः स्वर्गे devatāḥ svarge → देवताः + स्वर्गे

    no change (visarga before s) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before a sibilant the visarga may stand; the Gita Press keeps it (देवताः स्वर्गे, not देवतास्स्वर्गे).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • मर्त्यलोके martyaloke

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मर्त्य + लोक
    Vigraha
    मर्त्यानां लोकः, तस्मिन् martyānāṃ lokaḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the world of mortals”
    In the verse
    on earth, among men

  • रक्तदन्तिकाम् raktadantikām

    bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -ka and the feminine -ā, the a before k becoming i)

    Members
    रक्त + दन्त (+ ka)
    Vigraha
    रक्ताः दन्ताः यस्याः सा रक्तदन्तिका, ताम् raktāḥ dantāḥ yasyāḥ sā raktadantikā, tām
    Literally
    “she whose teeth are red”
    In the verse
    the Red-toothed Goddess, named from 11.44
Morphology & anvaya

  • व्याहरिष्यन्ति vyāhariṣyanti

    Future of वि + आ + √हृ (हर् + इष्य + अन्ति); वि before आ becomes व्या (yaṇ). With two accusatives, ‘to call someone something’: मां … रक्तदन्तिकाम्.

  • स्तुवन्तः stuvantaḥ

    Present participle of √स्तु (adādi): the root u becomes uv before the vowel (स्तुवत्), nom. pl. स्तुवन्तः.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः स्वर्गे देवताः मर्त्यलोके च मानवाः स्तुवन्तः मां सततं रक्तदन्तिकां व्याहरिष्यन्ति।

tataḥ svarge devatāḥ martyaloke ca mānavāḥ stuvantaḥ māṃ satataṃ raktadantikāṃ vyāhariṣyanti |

Two subjects with their places (स्वर्गे देवताः, मर्त्यलोके मानवाः), the participle स्तुवन्तः, the verb व्याहरिष्यन्ति with object (माम्) and predicate accusative (रक्तदन्तिकाम्).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second form receives its name: Raktadantikā, ‘she whose teeth are red’. Gods in heaven and humans on earth will both use it — the prophecy names the worshippers as well as the form.

Traditional interpretation

The Mūrtirahasya, one of the three rahasyas recited after the Saptaśatī in the Gita Press pāṭha, describes the future forms of this passage for meditation, Raktadantikā among them. In the chapter itself she is known only by what these two verses say.

Verse 11.46

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भूयश्च शतवार्षिक्यामनावृष्ट्यामनम्भसि।मुनिभिः संस्तुता भूमौ सम्भविष्याम्ययोनिजा॥⁠४६⁠॥

bhūyaśca śatavārṣikyāmanāvṛṣṭyāmanambhasi |munibhiḥ saṃstutā bhūmau sambhaviṣyāmyayonijā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भूयः · च · शतवार्षिक्याम् · अनावृष्ट्याम् · अनम्भसिमुनिभिः · संस्तुता · भूमौ · सम्भविष्यामि · अयोनिजा

bhūyaḥ · ca · śatavārṣikyām · anāvṛṣṭyām · anambhasimunibhiḥ · saṃstutā · bhūmau · sambhaviṣyāmi · ayonijā

Word by word

  1. भूयःbhūyaḥagain, once moreindeclinable (comparative भूयस् used as an adverb)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. शतवार्षिक्याम्śatavārṣikyāmlasting a hundred yearsf. loc. sg. of शतवार्षिकी (taddhita from the dvigu शतवर्ष; see Samāsa); qualifies अनावृष्ट्याम्
  4. अनावृष्ट्याम्anāvṛṣṭyāmin a drought (lit. ‘absence of rain’)f. loc. sg. of अनावृष्टि; compound (see Samāsa); locative of time
  5. अनम्भसिanambhasiwithout waterloc. sg. of the bahuvrīhi अनम्भस् ‘waterless’ (see Samāsa); an s-stem bahuvrīhi has one form for all genders, so it agrees with अनावृष्ट्याम्
  6. मुनिभिःmunibhiḥby the sagesm. instr. pl. of मुनि (i-stem); agent with संस्तुता
  7. संस्तुताsaṃstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the subject ‘I’
  8. भूमौbhūmauon the earthf. loc. sg. of भूमि (i-stem)
  9. सम्भविष्यामिsambhaviṣyāmiI shall come into being, I shall appearसम् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  10. अयोनिजाayonijānot born from a wombf. nom. sg. of अयोनिज; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of the subject ‘I’

Translation

And again, in a drought lasting a hundred years, without water, praised by the sages, I shall come into being on the earth, not born from a womb.

In readable EnglishAnd once more, when the earth lies a hundred years without rain or water, I shall appear on earth at the sages’ praise, born of no womb.

‘At the sages’ praise’ renders मुनिभिः संस्तुता (‘praised by the sages’) as the occasion of her coming.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • भूयश्च bhūyaśca → भूयः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.

  • शतवार्षिक्यामनावृष्ट्यामनम्भसि śatavārṣikyāmanāvṛṣṭyāmanambhasi → शतवार्षिक्याम् + अनावृष्ट्याम् + अनम्भसि

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Both final m’s stand before a vowel and are written with it: three locatives in one written word.

  • सम्भविष्याम्ययोनिजा sambhaviṣyāmyayonijā → सम्भविष्यामि + अयोनिजा

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before the unlike vowel a becomes y.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शतवार्षिक्याम् śatavārṣikyām

    dvigu (समाहार) as the base of a taddhita

    Members
    शतवर्ष + (taddhita -इक, feminine -ई)
    Vigraha
    शतं वर्षाणि शतवर्षम्; शतवर्षं भवा शतवार्षिकी, तस्याम् śataṃ varṣāṇi śatavarṣam; śatavarṣaṃ bhavā śatavārṣikī, tasyām
    Literally
    “of a hundred years’ duration”
    In the verse
    a drought that lasts a century

  • अनावृष्ट्याम् anāvṛṣṭyām

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अन् (नञ्) + आवृष्टि
    Vigraha
    न आवृष्टिः अनावृष्टिः, तस्याम् na āvṛṣṭiḥ anāvṛṣṭiḥ, tasyām
    Literally
    “in the non-raining”
    In the verse
    in a drought

  • अनम्भसि anambhasi

    bahuvrīhi (with the negative particle)

    Members
    अन् (नञ्) + अम्भस्
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते अम्भः यस्यां सा अनम्भाः, तस्याम् na vidyate ambhaḥ yasyāṃ sā anambhāḥ, tasyām
    Literally
    “in (one) that has no water”
    In the verse
    when no water is left
    Alternative
    It may also stand on its own as ‘when (the earth) is waterless’, with भुवि or जगति understood; the sense does not change.

  • अयोनिजा ayonijā

    nañ tatpuruṣa, its second member an upapada tatpuruṣa (योनिज)

    Members
    अ (नञ्) + योनिज
    Vigraha
    न योनिजा अयोनिजा; योनौ जायते इति योनिजा na yonijā ayonijā; yonau jāyate iti yonijā
    Literally
    “not born in a womb”
    In the verse
    self-manifest, without human birth
Morphology & anvaya

  • सम्भविष्यामि sambhaviṣyāmi

    Future of सम् + √भू (भो + इष्य → भविष्य): ‘I shall come into being’. The prefix सम् gives the sense of appearing or being born.

  • संस्तुता saṃstutā

    Past participle of सम् + √स्तु: ‘praised’. The sages’ praise comes before her appearance: they call, and she appears.

Anvaya · prose order

भूयः च शतवार्षिक्याम् अनम्भसि अनावृष्ट्यां (सत्यां) मुनिभिः संस्तुता (अहम्) अयोनिजा भूमौ सम्भविष्यामि।

bhūyaḥ ca śatavārṣikyām anambhasi anāvṛṣṭyāṃ (satyāṃ) munibhiḥ saṃstutā (aham) ayonijā bhūmau sambhaviṣyāmi |

The locatives give the occasion (a waterless hundred-year drought); मुनिभिः संस्तुता gives the cause; the main clause is (अहम्) अयोनिजा भूमौ सम्भविष्यामि.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The third descent begins: a drought of a hundred years, the sages praise her, and she appears on earth अयोनिजा, born of no mother. Unlike the daughter of Yaśodā (11.42), this form has no human birth.

Traditional interpretation

Verses 11.46–50 are one episode with three names: Śatākṣī (11.47), Śākambharī (11.49) and Durgā (11.50), all of the same appearance (तत्रैव, ‘right there’, 11.49). The tradition treats them as one Goddess named by three of her deeds.

Verse 11.47

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततः शतेन नेत्राणां निरीक्षिष्यामि यन्मुनीन्।कीर्तयिष्यन्ति मनुजाः शताक्षीमिति मां ततः॥⁠४७⁠॥

tataḥ śatena netrāṇāṃ nirīkṣiṣyāmi yanmunīn |kīrtayiṣyanti manujāḥ śatākṣīmiti māṃ tataḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · शतेन · नेत्राणाम् · निरीक्षिष्यामि · यत् · मुनीन्कीर्तयिष्यन्ति · मनुजाः · शताक्षीम् · इति · माम् · ततः

tataḥ · śatena · netrāṇām · nirīkṣiṣyāmi · yat · munīnkīrtayiṣyanti · manujāḥ · śatākṣīm · iti · mām · tataḥ

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time, तद् + tas)
  2. शतेनśatenawith a hundredn. instr. sg. of शत; instrument of निरीक्षिष्यामि
  3. नेत्राणाम्netrāṇāmof eyesn. gen. pl. of नेत्र; with शतेन (‘a hundred of eyes’)
  4. निरीक्षिष्यामिnirīkṣiṣyāmiI shall look uponनिर् + √ईक्ष् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to see’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, here in parasmaipada
  5. यत्yatsince, becauseindeclinable (conjunction, the neuter यद् used causally); correlative of the second ततः
  6. मुनीन्munīnthe sagesm. acc. pl. of मुनि; object of निरीक्षिष्यामि
  7. कीर्तयिष्यन्तिkīrtayiṣyantiwill celebrate, will proclaim√कीर्त् (curādi, 10 ‘to mention, praise’, stem कीर्तय-), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. मनुजाःmanujāḥhuman beings (lit. ‘Manu-born’)m. nom. pl. of मनुज; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  9. शताक्षीम्śatākṣīmŚatākṣī, ‘the Hundred-eyed’f. acc. sg. of शताक्षी; compound (see Samāsa); the name, in the accusative with माम्
  10. इतिitias (quotation marker)indeclinable (particle marking a name or quotation)
  11. माम्māmmefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. sg.; object of कीर्तयिष्यन्ति
  12. ततःtataḥthereforeindeclinable (adverb, correlative of यत्)

Translation

Then, since I shall look upon the sages with a hundred eyes, men will therefore proclaim me as ‘Śatākṣī’.

In readable EnglishBecause I shall look upon the sages with a hundred eyes, men will call me Śatākṣī, the Hundred-eyed.

The first ततः (‘then’) is left untranslated in tr; ‘the Hundred-eyed’ glosses the name.

Textual verification / uncertain: यत्: read as the conjunction ‘since’, answered by ततः. It could also be taken as ‘when’ (‘when I look upon the sages …’); the sense is nearly the same.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • निरीक्षिष्यामि यन्मुनीन् nirīkṣiṣyāmi yanmunīn → निरीक्षिष्यामि + यत् + मुनीन्

    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t (voiced to d at word end, 8.2.39) becomes the nasal n before the m of मुनीन्.

  • शताक्षीमिति śatākṣīmiti → शताक्षीम् + इति

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m is written with the i of इति. Inside the compound, शत + अक्षी → शताक्षी (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).

  • मनुजाः शताक्षीम् manujāḥ śatākṣīm → मनुजाः + शताक्षीम्

    no change (visarga before ś) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before a sibilant the visarga may stand; the text keeps it here and in ततः शतेन.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शताक्षीम् śatākṣīm

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    शत + अक्षि
    Vigraha
    शतम् अक्षीणि यस्याः सा शताक्षी, ताम् śatam akṣīṇi yasyāḥ sā śatākṣī, tām
    Literally
    “she who has a hundred eyes”
    In the verse
    Śatākṣī, the Hundred-eyed. At the end of a bahuvrīhi अक्षि takes the stem अक्ष (a samāsānta suffix), and the feminine is formed in -ī.

  • मनुजाः manujāḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मनु + ज
    Vigraha
    मनोः जाताः मनुजाः manoḥ jātāḥ manujāḥ
    Literally
    “born of Manu”
    In the verse
    human beings
Morphology & anvaya

  • निरीक्षिष्यामि nirīkṣiṣyāmi

    Future of निर् + √ईक्ष् with iṭ (ईक्ष् + इष्य). √ईक्ष् is ātmanepadin, so the classical form would be निरीक्षिष्ये; the parasmaipada ending is an epic and Purāṇic licence (ārṣa).

  • यत् yat

    The neuter relative used as a conjunction, ‘since, because’, answered by ततः ‘therefore’ at the end of the verse: यत् … ततः.

  • कीर्तयिष्यन्ति kīrtayiṣyanti

    Future of the curādi stem कीर्तय- (कीर्तय + इष्य + अन्ति); with माम् … शताक्षीम् इति, ‘they will proclaim me as Śatākṣī’.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः यत् (अहं) नेत्राणां शतेन मुनीन् निरीक्षिष्यामि, ततः मनुजाः मां शताक्षीम् इति कीर्तयिष्यन्ति।

tataḥ yat (ahaṃ) netrāṇāṃ śatena munīn nirīkṣiṣyāmi, tataḥ manujāḥ māṃ śatākṣīm iti kīrtayiṣyanti |

A causal clause (यत् … निरीक्षिष्यामि) and its correlative main clause (ततः … कीर्तयिष्यन्ति); इति marks the name.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first name of the third descent: she looks upon the sages with a hundred eyes, and so is called Śatākṣī. The text gives only the gaze; it does not say what the eyes do.

Traditional interpretation

Later Śākta retellings fill the gap. In the Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa the Hundred-eyed Goddess, seeing the suffering of the drought, weeps from all her eyes, and her tears become the rain that refills the rivers. The Devī Māhātmya itself says only that she looked.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the first thing the Goddess does in the famine is to see. Attention comes before relief.

Verse 11.48

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततोऽहमखिलं लोकमात्मदेहसमुद्भवैः।भरिष्यामि सुराः शाकैरावृष्टेः प्राणधारकैः॥⁠४८⁠॥

tato'hamakhilaṃ lokamātmadehasamudbhavaiḥ |bhariṣyāmi surāḥ śākairāvṛṣṭeḥ prāṇadhārakaiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · अहम् · अखिलम् · लोकम् · आत्मदेहसमुद्भवैःभरिष्यामि · सुराः · शाकैः · आ · वृष्टेः · प्राणधारकैः

tataḥ · aham · akhilam · lokam · ātmadehasamudbhavaiḥbhariṣyāmi · surāḥ · śākaiḥ · ā · vṛṣṭeḥ · prāṇadhārakaiḥ

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  2. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  3. अखिलम्akhilamentire, wholem. acc. sg. of अखिल; qualifies लोकम्
  4. लोकम्lokamworldm. acc. sg. of लोक; object of भरिष्यामि
  5. आत्मदेहसमुद्भवैःātmadehasamudbhavaiḥarisen from my own bodym. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शाकैः
  6. भरिष्यामिbhariṣyāmiI shall sustain, I shall nourish√भृ (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to bear, support’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. सुराःsurāḥO godsm. voc. pl. of सुर; address
  8. शाकैःśākaiḥwith vegetables, with edible plantsm. instr. pl. of शाक; instrument of भरिष्यामि
  9. आāuntilindeclinable (preposition, karmapravacanīya of limit, governing the ablative)
  10. वृष्टेःvṛṣṭeḥthe rainf. abl. sg. of वृष्टि (i-stem; √वृष् + ktin); governed by आ
  11. प्राणधारकैःprāṇadhārakaiḥlife-sustainingm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शाकैः

Translation

Then I, O gods, shall sustain the whole world, until the rain, with life-sustaining vegetables arisen from my own body.

In readable EnglishThen, O gods, until the rains return, I shall feed the whole world with life-giving plants grown from my own body.

‘Until the rains return’ renders आ वृष्टेः, ‘until the rain’; ‘feed’ renders भरिष्यामि, ‘sustain, nourish’.

Textual verification / uncertain: The printed आवृष्टेः is divided here as आ + वृष्टेः (‘until the rain’), the reading of the usual translations. Taking आवृष्टि as one noun (‘rainfall’, ablative ‘from the rain’) makes poorer sense of the verse.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततोऽहमखिलं tato'hamakhilaṃ → ततः + अहम् + अखिलम्

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is absorbed (avagraha). The m of अहम् is written with अखिलम्.

  • लोकमात्मदेहसमुद्भवैः lokamātmadehasamudbhavaiḥ → लोकम् + आत्मदेहसमुद्भवैः

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m is written with the ā of आत्म-.

  • शाकैरावृष्टेः śākairāvṛṣṭeḥ → शाकैः + आ + वृष्टेः

    visarga sandhi (r before a vowel) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (as ru) appears as r before a vowel: शाकै + र् + आ. आ and वृष्टेः are simply written together.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • आत्मदेहसमुद्भवैः ātmadehasamudbhavaiḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (आत्मदेह)

    Members
    आत्मदेह + समुद्भव
    Vigraha
    आत्मनः देहः आत्मदेहः; आत्मदेहात् समुद्भवः येषां ते, तैः ātmanaḥ dehaḥ ātmadehaḥ; ātmadehāt samudbhavaḥ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally
    “whose origin is from one’s own body”
    In the verse
    grown from my own body
    Alternative
    Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa, आत्मदेहात् समुद्भवाः ‘arisen from my body’; the sense is the same.

  • प्राणधारकैः prāṇadhārakaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (object genitive with an agent noun in -aka)

    Members
    प्राण + धारक
    Vigraha
    प्राणानां धारकाः, तैः prāṇānāṃ dhārakāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “holders of the life-breaths”
    In the verse
    life-sustaining
Morphology & anvaya

  • भरिष्यामि bhariṣyāmi

    Future of √भृ: roots in ṛ take the connecting vowel i in the future (भर् + इष्य + आमि). ‘I shall bear, sustain’ — the verb from which the name Śākambharī (11.49) is made.

  • आ ā

    आ (आङ्) with the ablative marks a limit: आ वृष्टेः ‘until the rain’. The text writes it joined (आवृष्टेः); it is not a compound, since वृष्टेः keeps its own case ending.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सुराः, ततः अहम् आ वृष्टेः आत्मदेहसमुद्भवैः प्राणधारकैः शाकैः अखिलं लोकं भरिष्यामि।

(he) surāḥ, tataḥ aham ā vṛṣṭeḥ ātmadehasamudbhavaiḥ prāṇadhārakaiḥ śākaiḥ akhilaṃ lokaṃ bhariṣyāmi |

Subject अहम्, verb भरिष्यामि, object अखिलं लोकम्; the instrument शाकैः with two epithets; आ वृष्टेः gives the limit of time.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

In the drought the Goddess feeds the world from her own body: edible plants that keep life going until the rain comes. The verse prepares her name in 11.49, Śākambharī, ‘she who bears (the world with) vegetables’.

Traditional interpretation

The image of the Goddess as the one who feeds is old in Śākta devotion: Śākambharī is worshipped as the giver of food in famine. The verse itself is precise about the limit — आ वृष्टेः, only until the rain — the Goddess sustains, and the ordinary order resumes.

Verse 11.49

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शाकम्भरीति विख्यातिं तदा यास्याम्यहं भुवि।तत्रैव च वधिष्यामि दुर्गमाख्यं महासुरम्॥⁠४९⁠॥

śākambharīti vikhyātiṃ tadā yāsyāmyahaṃ bhuvi |tatraiva ca vadhiṣyāmi durgamākhyaṃ mahāsuram ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शाकम्भरी · इति · विख्यातिम् · तदा · यास्यामि · अहम् · भुवितत्र · एव · च · वधिष्यामि · दुर्गमाख्यम् · महासुरम्

śākambharī · iti · vikhyātim · tadā · yāsyāmi · aham · bhuvitatra · eva · ca · vadhiṣyāmi · durgamākhyam · mahāsuram

Word by word

  1. शाकम्भरीśākambharīŚākambharī, ‘she who sustains with vegetables’f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the name, cited in the nominative before इति
  2. इतिitias (quotation marker)indeclinable (particle marking a name)
  3. विख्यातिम्vikhyātimfame, renownf. acc. sg. of विख्याति (वि + √ख्या + ktin); goal of यास्यामि
  4. तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  5. यास्यामिyāsyāmiI shall go (to), I shall attain√या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  7. भुविbhuvion earthf. loc. sg. of भू
  8. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place, तद् + tral)
  9. एवevaright, justindeclinable (emphatic particle); with तत्र, ‘in that very place, at that same time’
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. वधिष्यामिvadhiṣyāmiI shall slay√वध् (the stem वध, used for √हन्), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. दुर्गमाख्यम्durgamākhyamnamed Durgamam. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महासुरम्
  13. महासुरम्mahāsuramgreat asuram. acc. sg.; compound (karmadhāraya, as in 11.44); object of वधिष्यामि

Translation

Then I shall go to renown on earth as ‘Śākambharī’; and right there I shall slay the great asura named Durgama.

In readable EnglishThen I shall be famed on earth as Śākambharī, and in that same descent I shall slay the great asura called Durgama.

‘In that same descent’ interprets तत्र एव, ‘right there’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शाकम्भरीति śākambharīti → शाकम्भरी + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ī + i → ī.

  • यास्याम्यहं yāsyāmyahaṃ → यास्यामि + अहम्

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the unlike vowel a becomes y. The m of अहम् becomes anusvāra before bh (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • तत्रैव tatraiva → तत्र + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai.

  • दुर्गमाख्यं durgamākhyaṃ → दुर्गम + आख्यम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā; the final m is anusvāra before the m of महासुरम् (8.3.23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शाकम्भरी śākambharī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√भृ with the suffix khac, which inserts m after the first member; feminine in -ī)

    Members
    शाक + (म्) + भरी
    Vigraha
    शाकान् बिभर्ति इति शाकम्भरी śākān bibharti iti śākambharī
    Literally
    “she who bears vegetables”
    In the verse
    she who sustains the world with plants (11.48)

  • दुर्गमाख्यम् durgamākhyam

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    दुर्गम + आख्या
    Vigraha
    दुर्गमः आख्या यस्य सः, तम् durgamaḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ, tam
    Literally
    “whose name is Durgama”
    In the verse
    the asura called Durgama, ‘hard to approach’
Morphology & anvaya

  • वधिष्यामि vadhiṣyāmi

    Pāṇini’s grammar uses वध as a replacement of √हन् only in the aorist and the benedictive; a future वधिष्यामि is not classical but is common in epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit (ārṣa). It recurs in 11.53.

  • यास्यामि yāsyāmi

    Future of √या (aniṭ: या + स्य + आमि). With विख्यातिम्, ‘I shall go to fame’, i.e. become famous.

Anvaya · prose order

तदा अहं भुवि शाकम्भरी इति विख्यातिं यास्यामि। तत्र एव च दुर्गमाख्यं महासुरं वधिष्यामि।

tadā ahaṃ bhuvi śākambharī iti vikhyātiṃ yāsyāmi | tatra eva ca durgamākhyaṃ mahāsuraṃ vadhiṣyāmi |

Two clauses: (1) अहं … विख्यातिं यास्यामि with the name in an इति phrase; (2) तत्र एव च … वधिष्यामि with its object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second name of the third descent: Śākambharī, ‘she who sustains with plants’, from the deed of 11.48. In the same appearance (तत्र एव) she slays a great asura named Durgama, whose name gives her third name in 11.50.

Traditional interpretation

The text says nothing more of Durgama. Later Śākta retellings, such as the Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, make him the cause of the drought: having taken the Vedas from the gods by a boon, he stopped the sacrifices, and the rains failed. The Devī Māhātmya gives only the name and the slaying.

Verse 11.50

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

दुर्गा देवीति विख्यातं तन्मे नाम भविष्यति।पुनश्चाहं यदा भीमं रूपं कृत्वा हिमाचले॥⁠५०⁠॥

durgā devīti vikhyātaṃ tanme nāma bhaviṣyati |punaścāhaṃ yadā bhīmaṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvā himācale ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

दुर्गा · देवी · इति · विख्यातम् · तत् · मे · नाम · भविष्यतिपुनः · च · अहम् · यदा · भीमम् · रूपम् · कृत्वा · हिमाचले

durgā · devī · iti · vikhyātam · tat · me · nāma · bhaviṣyatipunaḥ · ca · aham · yadā · bhīmam · rūpam · kṛtvā · himācale

Word by word

  1. दुर्गाdurgāDurgāf. nom. sg. of दुर्गा; the name, cited in the nominative before इति
  2. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; part of the cited name ‘Durgā Devī’
  3. इतिitithus, as (quotation marker)indeclinable (particle marking a name)
  4. विख्यातम्vikhyātamrenowned, famouspast participle (kta) of वि + √ख्या (adādi, 2P ‘to tell’; with वि ‘to make known’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of नाम with भविष्यति
  5. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies नाम
  6. मेmemyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  7. नामnāmanamen. nom. sg. of नामन्; subject of भविष्यति
  8. भविष्यतिbhaviṣyatiwill be, will become√भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb); its visarga stands for r
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of the clause continued in 11.51
  12. यदाyadāwhenindeclinable (relative adverb of time); answered by तदा in 11.51
  13. भीमम्bhīmamterrible, fearsomen. acc. sg. of भीम; qualifies रूपम्
  14. रूपम्rūpamformn. acc. sg. of रूप; object of कृत्वा
  15. कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving taken, having assumed (lit. ‘having made’)absolutive (ktvā) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U)
  16. हिमाचलेhimācaleon the Himālaya, the snow mountainm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

‘Durgā Devī’ — thus that name of mine will become renowned. And again, when I, having assumed a terrible form on the Himālaya, —

In readable EnglishThat name of mine, Durgā Devī, will be renowned. And again, when I take a terrible form on the Himālaya,

The second line is the beginning of a sentence completed in 11.51–52.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • दुर्गा देवीति durgā devīti → दुर्गा + देवी + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ī + i → ī (देवी + इति → देवीति).

  • विख्यातं तन्मे vikhyātaṃ tanme → विख्यातम् + तत् + मे

    anusvāra; consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final m becomes anusvāra before t; final t (voiced to d at word end, 8.2.39) becomes n before the nasal m of मे.

  • पुनश्चाहं punaścāhaṃ → पुनः + च + अहम्

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c becomes s, then ś; च + अहम् → चाहम् (a + a → ā). The m of अहम् is anusvāra before y (8.3.23).

  • हिमाचले himācale → हिम + अचले

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हिमाचले himācale

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    हिम + अचल
    Vigraha
    हिमस्य अचलः हिमाचलः, तस्मिन् himasya acalaḥ himācalaḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “on the mountain of snow”
    In the verse
    on the Himālaya
    Alternative
    Also analysed as a karmadhāraya with the middle member dropped, हिमयुक्तः अचलः ‘the snowy mountain’; the referent is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • दुर्गा durgā

    The name is given from the deed of 11.49: she who slew Durgama is called Durgā. As a word, दुर्ग (दुस् + √गम्, ‘hard to reach’) with the feminine -ā; the text connects it with the asura’s name, not with this etymology.

  • विख्यातम् vikhyātam

    Past participle of वि + √ख्या, neuter to agree with नाम: ‘(that name) will become famous’.

  • कृत्वा kṛtvā

    Absolutive of √कृ with -त्वा: ‘having made (a form)’, i.e. assuming it. The clause it begins is completed in 11.51.

Anvaya · prose order

‘दुर्गा देवी’ इति तत् मे नाम विख्यातं भविष्यति। पुनः च यदा अहं हिमाचले भीमं रूपं कृत्वा (11.51: रक्षांसि भक्षयिष्यामि …)

‘durgā devī’ iti tat me nāma vikhyātaṃ bhaviṣyati | punaḥ ca yadā ahaṃ himācale bhīmaṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvā (11.51: rakṣāṃsi bhakṣayiṣyāmi …)

The first line closes the third descent: तत् मे नाम (subject) विख्यातं भविष्यति, with the name in an इति phrase. The second line opens a new यदा-clause (the fourth descent) whose verb is in 11.51.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The third name of the same descent: having slain Durgama (11.49), the Goddess will be known as Durgā Devī. The second line opens the fourth descent: a terrible form on the Himālaya, completed in the next verse.

Traditional interpretation

Here the text explains the name Durgā by a deed — the slaying of Durgama — not by the meaning ‘hard to approach’. The name has been used of her throughout the Devī Māhātmya; the prophecy gives it its story.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has दुर्गमा and दुर्गा as consecutive names, 189 and 190. The pairing is shared Śākta vocabulary; the Sahasranāma does not tell the story of Durgama.

Verse 11.51

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

रक्षांसि भक्षयिष्यामि मुनीनां त्राणकारणात्।तदा मां मुनयः सर्वे स्तोष्यन्त्यानम्रमूर्तयः॥⁠५१⁠॥

rakṣāṃsi bhakṣayiṣyāmi munīnāṃ trāṇakāraṇāt |tadā māṃ munayaḥ sarve stoṣyantyānamramūrtayaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

रक्षांसि · भक्षयिष्यामि · मुनीनाम् · त्राणकारणात्तदा · माम् · मुनयः · सर्वे · स्तोष्यन्ति · आनम्रमूर्तयः

rakṣāṃsi · bhakṣayiṣyāmi · munīnām · trāṇakāraṇāttadā · mām · munayaḥ · sarve · stoṣyanti · ānamramūrtayaḥ

Word by word

  1. रक्षांसिrakṣāṃsithe rākṣasas, demonsn. acc. pl. of रक्षस् (s-stem); object of भक्षयिष्यामि
  2. भक्षयिष्यामिbhakṣayiṣyāmiI shall devour√भक्ष् (curādi, 10, stem भक्षय- ‘to eat’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; verb of the यदा-clause begun in 11.50
  3. मुनीनाम्munīnāmof the sagesm. gen. pl. of मुनि; with त्राणकारणात्
  4. त्राणकारणात्trāṇakāraṇātfor the sake of protectionn. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of cause
  5. तदाtadāthenindeclinable (correlative of यदा in 11.50)
  6. माम्māmmefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोष्यन्ति
  7. मुनयःmunayaḥthe sagesm. nom. pl. of मुनि; subject
  8. सर्वेsarveallpronominal adjective सर्व, m. nom. pl.; qualifies मुनयः
  9. स्तोष्यन्तिstoṣyantiwill praise√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. आनम्रमूर्तयःānamramūrtayaḥwith bodies bowed lowm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मुनयः

Translation

— shall devour the rākṣasas for the protection of the sages, then all the sages, with bodies bowed low, will praise me;

In readable Englishand devour the rākṣasas to protect the sages, then all the sages will bow low and praise me,

The participial compound आनम्रमूर्तयः is rendered as a verb (‘bow low’). The sentence runs from 11.50 to 11.52.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मुनीनां त्राणकारणात् munīnāṃ trāṇakāraṇāt → मुनीनाम् + त्राणकारणात्

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m becomes anusvāra before the consonant t. The same in मां मुनयः.

  • स्तोष्यन्त्यानम्रमूर्तयः stoṣyantyānamramūrtayaḥ → स्तोष्यन्ति + आनम्रमूर्तयः

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the unlike vowel ā becomes y.

  • मुनयः सर्वे munayaḥ sarve → मुनयः + सर्वे

    no change (visarga before s) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before a sibilant the visarga may stand; the text keeps it.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • त्राणकारणात् trāṇakāraṇāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    त्राण + कारण
    Vigraha
    त्राणस्य कारणम्, तस्मात् trāṇasya kāraṇam, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the cause of protection”
    In the verse
    for the sake of protecting (the sages)
    Alternative
    Also read as a karmadhāraya, त्राणम् एव कारणम् ‘protection being the reason’; the sense is the same.

  • आनम्रमूर्तयः ānamramūrtayaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    आनम्र + मूर्ति
    Vigraha
    आनम्राः मूर्तयः येषां ते ānamrāḥ mūrtayaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “whose bodies are bowed down”
    In the verse
    bowing low in reverence
Morphology & anvaya

  • स्तोष्यन्ति stoṣyanti

    Future of √स्तु (aniṭ): guṇa स्तो + स्य, s → ṣ after o (स्तोष्य-), with -अन्ति. It recurs in 11.54.

  • रक्षांसि rakṣāṃsi

    Neuter plural of रक्षस्: the s-stem lengthens its vowel and inserts a nasal (anusvāra) before -सि in the nom./acc. plural.

Anvaya · prose order

(यदा अहं हिमाचले भीमं रूपं कृत्वा) मुनीनां त्राणकारणात् रक्षांसि भक्षयिष्यामि, तदा सर्वे मुनयः आनम्रमूर्तयः (सन्तः) मां स्तोष्यन्ति,

(yadā ahaṃ himācale bhīmaṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvā) munīnāṃ trāṇakāraṇāt rakṣāṃsi bhakṣayiṣyāmi, tadā sarve munayaḥ ānamramūrtayaḥ (santaḥ) māṃ stoṣyanti,

The first line completes the यदा-clause of 11.50 (verb भक्षयिष्यामि); the second is its correlative तदा-clause, whose sentence ends with the name in 11.52.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fourth descent: on the Himālaya, in a terrible form, the Goddess devours rākṣasas to protect the sages, and the sages bow and praise her. The name follows in 11.52.

Traditional interpretation

As in the third descent, the occasion is the sages’ need; each form in this prophecy is called down by a particular distress and named by those it saves.

Verse 11.52

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भीमा देवीति विख्यातं तन्मे नाम भविष्यति।यदारुणाख्यस्त्रैलोक्ये महाबाधां करिष्यति॥⁠५२⁠॥

bhīmā devīti vikhyātaṃ tanme nāma bhaviṣyati |yadāruṇākhyastrailokye mahābādhāṃ kariṣyati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भीमा · देवी · इति · विख्यातम् · तत् · मे · नाम · भविष्यतियदा · अरुणाख्यः · त्रैलोक्ये · महाबाधाम् · करिष्यति

bhīmā · devī · iti · vikhyātam · tat · me · nāma · bhaviṣyatiyadā · aruṇākhyaḥ · trailokye · mahābādhām · kariṣyati

Word by word

  1. भीमाbhīmāBhīmā, ‘the Terrible’f. nom. sg. of भीमा; the name, cited in the nominative before इति
  2. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg.; part of the cited name ‘Bhīmā Devī’
  3. इतिitithus, as (quotation marker)indeclinable (particle marking a name)
  4. विख्यातम्vikhyātamrenownedpast participle (kta) of वि + √ख्या, n. nom. sg.; predicate of नाम (as in 11.50)
  5. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies नाम
  6. मेmemyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  7. नामnāmanamen. nom. sg. of नामन्; subject of भविष्यति
  8. भविष्यतिbhaviṣyatiwill be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. यदाyadāwhenindeclinable (relative adverb of time); answered by तदा in 11.53
  10. अरुणाख्यःaruṇākhyaḥthe one named Aruṇam. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of करिष्यति
  11. त्रैलोक्येtrailokyein the three worldsn. loc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita, as in 11.39)
  12. महाबाधाम्mahābādhāmgreat affliction, great oppressionf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of करिष्यति
  13. करिष्यतिkariṣyatiwill cause, will do√कृ (tanādi, 8U), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

‘Bhīmā Devī’ — thus that name of mine will become renowned. When the one named Aruṇa causes great affliction in the three worlds, —

In readable Englishand my name will be renowned as Bhīmā Devī. When the asura called Aruṇa brings great oppression on the three worlds,

‘Asura’ is supplied; the text gives only the name Aruṇa, but 11.53 calls him महासुरम्.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • भीमा देवीति bhīmā devīti → भीमा + देवी + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ī + i → ī, as in 11.50.

  • विख्यातं तन्मे vikhyātaṃ tanme → विख्यातम् + तत् + मे

    anusvāra; consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    As in 11.50.

  • यदारुणाख्यस्त्रैलोक्ये yadāruṇākhyastrailokye → यदा + अरुणाख्यः + त्रैलोक्ये

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    ā + a → ā (यदा + अरुण- → यदारुण-); visarga before t becomes s.

  • महाबाधां करिष्यति mahābādhāṃ kariṣyati → महाबाधाम् + करिष्यति

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m becomes anusvāra before k.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अरुणाख्यः aruṇākhyaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    अरुण + आख्या
    Vigraha
    अरुणः आख्या यस्य सः aruṇaḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “whose name is Aruṇa”
    In the verse
    an asura called Aruṇa

  • महाबाधाम् mahābādhām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + बाधा
    Vigraha
    महती चासौ बाधा च, ताम् mahatī cāsau bādhā ca, tām
    Literally
    “a great affliction”
    In the verse
    grievous oppression of the worlds
Morphology & anvaya

  • करिष्यति kariṣyati

    Future of √कृ (roots in ṛ take iṭ: कर् + इष्य + ति): ‘(he) will do, will cause’. With बाधाम्, ‘will cause affliction’.

Anvaya · prose order

(तदा) ‘भीमा देवी’ इति तत् मे नाम विख्यातं भविष्यति। यदा अरुणाख्यः त्रैलोक्ये महाबाधां करिष्यति, (11.53: तदा अहं …)

(tadā) ‘bhīmā devī’ iti tat me nāma vikhyātaṃ bhaviṣyati | yadā aruṇākhyaḥ trailokye mahābādhāṃ kariṣyati, (11.53: tadā ahaṃ …)

The first line closes the sentence of 11.50–51 with the name; the second opens the fifth descent with a यदा-clause whose correlative is in 11.53.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fourth form is named: Bhīmā Devī, ‘the Terrible Goddess’, from the terrible form of 11.50. The second line opens the fifth and last named descent: an asura called Aruṇa will afflict the three worlds.

Traditional interpretation

The line ‘… इति विख्यातं तन्मे नाम भविष्यति’ is repeated word for word from 11.50, a formula that seals each naming. The repeated formula is how the prophecy keeps its shape as a list of names.

Verse 11.53

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तदाहं भ्रामरं रूपं कृत्वाऽसंख्येयषट्पदम्।त्रैलोक्यस्य हितार्थाय वधिष्यामि महासुरम्॥⁠५३⁠॥

tadāhaṃ bhrāmaraṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvā'saṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadam |trailokyasya hitārthāya vadhiṣyāmi mahāsuram ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तदा · अहम् · भ्रामरम् · रूपम् · कृत्वा · असंख्येयषट्पदम्त्रैलोक्यस्य · हितार्थाय · वधिष्यामि · महासुरम्

tadā · aham · bhrāmaram · rūpam · kṛtvā · asaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadamtrailokyasya · hitārthāya · vadhiṣyāmi · mahāsuram

Word by word

  1. तदाtadāthenindeclinable (correlative of यदा in 11.52)
  2. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  3. भ्रामरम्bhrāmaramof bees, bee-liken. acc. sg. of भ्रामर (taddhita aṇ from भ्रमर ‘bee’); qualifies रूपम्
  4. रूपम्rūpamformn. acc. sg. of रूप; object of कृत्वा
  5. कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving assumed (lit. ‘having made’)absolutive (ktvā) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U)
  6. असंख्येयषट्पदम्asaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadammade of countless beesn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपम्
  7. त्रैलोक्यस्यtrailokyasyaof the three worldsn. gen. sg. of त्रैलोक्य; with हितार्थाय
  8. हितार्थायhitārthāyafor the welfarem. dat. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); dative of purpose
  9. वधिष्यामिvadhiṣyāmiI shall slay√वध् (the stem वध, used for √हन्), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada (as in 11.49)
  10. महासुरम्mahāsuramthe great asuram. acc. sg.; compound (karmadhāraya, as in 11.44); object of वधिष्यामि (Aruṇa)

Translation

— then I, having assumed a bee-form of countless six-footed ones, shall slay the great asura for the welfare of the three worlds.

In readable Englishthen I shall take a form of countless bees and slay that great asura for the good of the three worlds.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तदाहं tadāhaṃ → तदा + अहम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā; the m of अहम् becomes anusvāra before bh (8.3.23).

  • कृत्वाऽसंख्येयषट्पदम् kṛtvā'saṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadam → कृत्वा + असंख्येयषट्पदम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā. The Gita Press writes an avagraha after the ā to show where the initial a of असंख्येय- was absorbed; it is a reading aid, not a separate sound.

  • हितार्थाय hitārthāya → हित + अर्थाय

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असंख्येयषट्पदम् asaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadam

    bahuvrīhi (its members: असंख्येय, a nañ tatpuruṣa न संख्येयः; षट्पद, a bahuvrīhi षट् पादाः यस्य सः, ‘six-footed’ = bee)

    Members
    असंख्येय + षट्पद
    Vigraha
    असंख्येयाः षट्पदाः यस्मिन् तत् asaṃkhyeyāḥ ṣaṭpadāḥ yasmin tat
    Literally
    “(a form) in which the six-footed ones are countless”
    In the verse
    a form made of innumerable bees

  • हितार्थाय hitārthāya

    caturthī tatpuruṣa (with अर्थ, always compounded)

    Members
    हित + अर्थ
    Vigraha
    हिताय अयम् हितार्थः, तस्मै hitāya ayam hitārthaḥ, tasmai
    Literally
    “for the purpose of welfare”
    In the verse
    for the good (of the three worlds)
Morphology & anvaya

  • भ्रामरम् bhrāmaram

    Taddhita with vṛddhi of the first syllable: भ्रमर → भ्रामर ‘belonging to bees, consisting of bees’. The feminine भ्रामरी becomes her name in 11.54.

  • वधिष्यामि vadhiṣyāmi

    Purāṇic future of the stem वध, as in 11.49.

Anvaya · prose order

तदा अहं असंख्येयषट्पदं भ्रामरं रूपं कृत्वा त्रैलोक्यस्य हितार्थाय (तं) महासुरं वधिष्यामि।

tadā ahaṃ asaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadaṃ bhrāmaraṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvā trailokyasya hitārthāya (taṃ) mahāsuraṃ vadhiṣyāmi |

The correlative तदा-clause of 11.52: the absolutive phrase (… रूपं कृत्वा), the purpose (त्रैलोक्यस्य हितार्थाय), the verb वधिष्यामि and its object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fifth descent: against Aruṇa the Goddess takes a form made of innumerable bees and kills him for the welfare of the three worlds. The form is a swarm, not a single body.

Traditional interpretation

Later Śākta retellings, such as the Devī Bhāgavata Purāṇa, explain the form: Aruṇa had a boon that no two- or four-footed creature could kill him, so the Goddess came as six-footed bees. The Devī Māhātmya itself gives no reason for the form.

Verse 11.54

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भ्रामरीति च मां लोकास्तदा स्तोष्यन्ति सर्वतः।इत्थं यदा यदा बाधा दानवोत्था भविष्यति॥⁠५४⁠॥

bhrāmarīti ca māṃ lokāstadā stoṣyanti sarvataḥ |itthaṃ yadā yadā bādhā dānavotthā bhaviṣyati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भ्रामरी · इति · च · माम् · लोकाः · तदा · स्तोष्यन्ति · सर्वतःइत्थम् · यदा · यदा · बाधा · दानवोत्था · भविष्यति

bhrāmarī · iti · ca · mām · lokāḥ · tadā · stoṣyanti · sarvataḥittham · yadā · yadā · bādhā · dānavotthā · bhaviṣyati

Word by word

  1. भ्रामरीbhrāmarīBhrāmarī, ‘the Goddess of the bees’f. nom. sg. of भ्रामरी (feminine of the taddhita भ्रामर, 11.53); the name, cited in the nominative before इति
  2. इतिitias (quotation marker)indeclinable (particle marking a name)
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  4. माम्māmmefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोष्यन्ति
  5. लोकाःlokāḥpeople, the worldsm. nom. pl. of लोक; subject
  6. तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  7. स्तोष्यन्तिstoṣyantiwill praise√स्तु (adādi, 2U), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada (as in 11.51)
  8. सर्वतःsarvataḥeverywhere, on all sidesindeclinable (सर्व + tas)
  9. इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  10. यदाyadāwhenindeclinable (relative adverb of time)
  11. यदाyadā(when)everindeclinable; repeated for distribution: यदा यदा ‘whenever’, answered by तदा तदा in 11.55
  12. बाधाbādhāaffliction, oppressionf. nom. sg. of बाधा; subject of भविष्यति
  13. दानवोत्थाdānavotthāarising from the Dānavasf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies बाधा
  14. भविष्यतिbhaviṣyatiwill arise, will be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

And then the people everywhere will praise me as ‘Bhrāmarī’. Thus, whenever affliction arising from the Dānavas shall occur, —

In readable Englishand people everywhere will then praise me as Bhrāmarī. Thus, whenever oppression born of the demons arises,

‘Demons’ renders दानव (the sons of Danu); the sentence is completed in 11.55.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • भ्रामरीति bhrāmarīti → भ्रामरी + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ī + i → ī.

  • लोकास्तदा lokāstadā → लोकाः + तदा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • इत्थं यदा itthaṃ yadā → इत्थम् + यदा

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m becomes anusvāra before the consonant y.

  • दानवोत्था dānavotthā → दानव + उत्था

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दानवोत्था dānavotthā

    upapada tatpuruṣa (pañcamī: the source in the ablative; उत्थ from उद् + √स्था with the suffix -a, feminine -ā)

    Members
    दानव + उत्था
    Vigraha
    दानवेभ्यः उत्तिष्ठति इति दानवोत्था dānavebhyaḥ uttiṣṭhati iti dānavotthā
    Literally
    “rising from the Dānavas”
    In the verse
    trouble caused by the demons
Morphology & anvaya

  • भ्रामरी bhrāmarī

    Feminine in -ī of भ्रामर (11.53): ‘she of the bees’. The name is taken from the form, as Raktadantikā and Śatākṣī were.

  • दानवोत्था dānavotthā

    उत्था: उद् + √स्था, the s of स्था lost after उद् (उत्था-), with the agent suffix -a: ‘that which rises up’.

Anvaya · prose order

तदा च लोकाः सर्वतः मां ‘भ्रामरी’ इति स्तोष्यन्ति। इत्थं यदा यदा दानवोत्था बाधा भविष्यति, (11.55: तदा तदा …)

tadā ca lokāḥ sarvataḥ māṃ ‘bhrāmarī’ iti stoṣyanti | itthaṃ yadā yadā dānavotthā bādhā bhaviṣyati, (11.55: tadā tadā …)

The first line closes the fifth descent with the name; the second begins the closing generalisation, a distributive यदा यदा-clause whose correlative तदा तदा is in 11.55.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fifth form is named: Bhrāmarī, ‘she of the bees’, praised everywhere. With इत्थम्, ‘thus’, the Goddess turns from the list of named descents to a rule: whenever demons cause affliction.

Traditional interpretation

After the daughter of Yaśodā (11.42) the prophecy has named six forms in four descents: Raktadantikā; Śatākṣī, Śākambharī and Durgā (one descent, 11.46–50); Bhīmā; and Bhrāmarī. The Mūrtirahasya recited after the Saptaśatī gives meditations on these forms.

Verse 11.55

Half-śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तदा तदावतीर्याहं करिष्याम्यरिसंक्षयम्॥⁠५५⁠॥ॐ

tadā tadāvatīryāhaṃ kariṣyāmyarisaṃkṣayam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तदा · तदा · अवतीर्य · अहम् · करिष्यामि · अरिसंक्षयम्

tadā · tadā · avatīrya · aham · kariṣyāmi · arisaṃkṣayam

Word by word

  1. तदाtadāthenindeclinable (correlative of यदा in 11.54)
  2. तदाtadā(then) each timeindeclinable; repeated for distribution, answering यदा यदा: ‘each time then’
  3. अवतीर्यavatīryahaving descendedabsolutive (lyap) of अव + √तॄ (bhvādi, 1P), as in 11.43
  4. अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
  5. करिष्यामिkariṣyāmiI shall bring about√कृ (tanādi, 8U), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. अरिसंक्षयम्arisaṃkṣayamthe destruction of the enemiesm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of करिष्यामि

Translation

— then, each time, having descended, I shall bring about the destruction of the enemies.

In readable Englisheach time I shall descend and destroy the enemies.

The half-śloka completes the sentence begun in 11.54; ‘each time’ renders the doubled तदा.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तदावतीर्याहं tadāvatīryāhaṃ → तदा + अवतीर्य + अहम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā twice: तदा + अवतीर्य → तदावतीर्य, अवतीर्य + अहम् → अवतीर्याहम्. The m becomes anusvāra before k (8.3.23).

  • करिष्याम्यरिसंक्षयम् kariṣyāmyarisaṃkṣayam → करिष्यामि + अरिसंक्षयम्

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the unlike vowel a becomes y.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अरिसंक्षयम् arisaṃkṣayam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अरि + संक्षय
    Vigraha
    अरीणां संक्षयः, तम् arīṇāṃ saṃkṣayaḥ, tam
    Literally
    “the complete destruction of enemies”
    In the verse
    the ending of the foes (of the worlds and the gods)
Morphology & anvaya

  • तदा tadā

    Repetition for distribution (vīpsā): यदा यदा … तदा तदा ‘whenever … each time then’. The doubled word makes a single promise into a standing rule.

  • करिष्यामि kariṣyāmi

    Future of √कृ with iṭ (कर् + इष्य + आमि). With अरिसंक्षयम्, ‘I shall make an end of the enemies’ — the last verb of the chapter.

Anvaya · prose order

(यदा यदा दानवोत्था बाधा भविष्यति,) तदा तदा अहम् अवतीर्य अरिसंक्षयं करिष्यामि॥

(yadā yadā dānavotthā bādhā bhaviṣyati,) tadā tadā aham avatīrya arisaṃkṣayaṃ kariṣyāmi ||

The correlative clause of 11.54b: the doubled तदा तदा, the absolutive अवतीर्य, the verb करिष्यामि and its object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The prophecy closes in a rule: whenever demons afflict the worlds, the Goddess will descend (अवतीर्य) and destroy the enemies. This answers the gods’ request of 11.39 exactly: एवम् एव, ‘in just this way’, every time. A half-śloka, counted by the Gita Press as its own mantra; the closing ॐ belongs to the recitation.

Traditional interpretation

The construction recalls the Bhagavadgītā’s promise of descent (4.7–8, यदा यदा हि धर्मस्य …): the same यदा यदा and the same idea of a recurring descent. Here the one who descends is the Goddess, and the occasion is the oppression of the worlds by demons.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the chapter ends not with a final victory but with a promise of return. The text treats protection as a pattern that repeats, not a single event.

❁

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये देव्याः स्तुतिर्नामैकादशोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye devyāḥ stutirnāmaikādaśo'dhyāyaḥ ||

Reflecting on the chapter

With Śumbha dead the battles are over, and the chapter is given to the gods’ thanksgiving. Their hymn, the Nārāyaṇī Stuti, names the Goddess as the ground and nourishment of the world, as Viṣṇu’s power and Māyā, and, verse after verse, as Nārāyaṇī in every form she took in the war; it asks her protection, and ends by asking her to be the giver of boons (11.2–35). She offers a boon; the gods ask that the worlds be freed of affliction and their enemies destroyed as they have just been (11.37–39). She answers with the promise of five future descents — as the daughter of Yaśodā on the Vindhya, as Raktadantikā, as Śatākṣī, Śākambharī and Durgā, as Bhīmā and as Bhrāmarī — and with a rule: whenever the Dānavas oppress the world, she will come down and destroy the enemies (11.41–55).

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 11.5You are Viṣṇu’s power, the seed of the universe, the supreme Māyā: by you the world is bewildered, and, gracious, you are the cause of liberation on earth.
  • 11.6All forms of knowledge and all women in the worlds are your forms; you alone, the Mother, fill this world — what praise can there be of you?
  • 11.10सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये — Śivā, Tryambakā, Gaurī saluted as Nārāyaṇī: the most widely recited verse of the hymn.
  • 11.24Form of all, Lady of all, endowed with every power: protect us from fears, Durgā Devī — the refrain turns to a prayer for protection.
  • 11.29Pleased, you remove every illness; angered, every cherished hope; those who take refuge in you meet no misfortune and become a refuge themselves.
  • 11.31Who but you dwells in all knowledge and the lamps of discernment — and who but you whirls this world in the dark pit of ‘mine’?
  • 11.55With 11.54: whenever oppression born of the Dānavas arises, each time I shall descend and destroy the enemies.
Vocabulary, constructions and insights

Principal vocabulary

  • नारायणीnārāyaṇīNārāyaṇī, the Goddess as the power of Nārāyaṇa; the name of the refrain
  • प्रसीदprasīda‘be gracious’, the hymn’s first and last prayer
  • शरणागतःśaraṇāgataḥone who has come for refuge
  • आर्तिःārtiḥaffliction, distress
  • मायाmāyāthe power that bewilders the world
  • मुक्तिहेतुःmuktihetuḥthe cause of liberation
  • ममत्वम्mamatvam‘mine-ness’, possessiveness
  • विवेकःvivekaḥdiscernment
  • आश्रयताāśrayatāthe state of being a refuge
  • वरदाvaradāgiver of boons
  • अवतीर्यavatīryahaving descended; the verb behind avatāra
  • अयोनिजाayonijāborn of no womb
  • शाकम्भरीśākambharīshe who sustains the world with plants
  • भ्रामरीbhrāmarīshe of the bees

Important grammatical constructions

  • The refrain: a chain of vocatives closed by नमः अस्तु ते, the imperative of √अस् with the dative (11.8–23); it changes to Durgā (11.24), Kātyāyanī (11.25) and Bhadrakālī (11.26), and to the statement त्वां नता वयम् (11.28).
  • Vocatives by stem: ī-stems shorten (नारायणि, गौरि, लक्ष्मि), ā-stems end in -e (शिवे, लज्जे), i-stems take guṇa (शक्ते, नियते); पुष्टि and भूति are stem-form vocatives kept as printed (11.22–23).
  • An instrumental of form among the vocatives (माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण, 11.14; नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण, 11.18; शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण, 11.20).
  • Prayer in the imperative, with the danger in the ablative and the purpose in the dative (त्राहि … भयेभ्यः, 11.24; पातु नो भीतेः, 11.26; शुभाय … भवतु, 11.28).
  • Rhetorical questions with का in place of a denial (का ते स्तुतिः, 11.6; तत्प्रकरोति कान्या, 11.30; का त्वदन्या, 11.31).
  • The gerundive of obligation with an instrumental agent (त्वया कार्यम्, 11.39), and with a genitive agent (त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् ईड्ये, 11.35).
  • The future of prophecy, including Purāṇic forms (वधिष्यामि, 11.49, 11.53; निरीक्षिष्यामि, 11.47), and names given with इति or a double accusative (11.45, 11.47, 11.49, 11.54).
  • Correlative clauses carried across verses: यदा … तदा (11.50–51, 11.52–53) and the distributive यदा यदा … तदा तदा (11.54–55).

Śākta insights

  • The many are one: each of the Mothers who fought in chapters 8–9 is saluted as Nārāyaṇī (11.13–21), and the gods’ ‘who else could do this?’ (11.30) answers the Goddess’s own claim to be alone (10.5).
  • The power that bewilders is the power that frees: she is the supreme Māyā and, when gracious, the cause of liberation (11.5); she is in every lamp of discernment and whirls the world in the pit of ‘mine’ (11.31); and, in the Gita Press reading, she is both Mahāvidyā and Mahā-avidyā (11.22).
  • All knowledge and every woman are her forms, and she alone fills the world (11.6): the verse from which the Śākta tradition draws its reverence for women as forms of the Goddess.
  • Her gentle and her terrible forms are one protection: the gentle three-eyed face is asked first, then the flaming trident, the bell and the bloodied sword (11.25–28); her pleasure takes away illness, her anger every cherished hope (11.29).
  • Refuge becomes refuge-giving: those who take refuge in her become a refuge themselves (11.29), and those who bow to her become the shelter of the universe (11.33).
  • Her grace is a standing promise, not a single act: the gods ask for ‘just this way’ (11.39), and she promises to descend whenever oppression arises (11.54–55).

Śrīvidyā connections

The one specific connection is liturgical, not in the Purāṇa text: the Gita Press dhyāna before this chapter meditates on Bhuvaneśī — Bhuvaneśvarī, a name the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also gives the Goddess (294, भुवनेश्वरी) — three-eyed (compare 453, त्रिनयना), smiling, and holding the noose and the goad, two of the four weapons the Lalitā Sahasranāma gives Lalitā (8, रागस्वरूपपाशाढ्या; 9, क्रोधाकाराङ्कुशोज्ज्वला). In the hymn the parallels are names and ideas: नारायणी (298), the name of the refrain; स्वर्गापवर्गदा (764, compare 11.8); सर्वमङ्गला (200, compare 11.10); विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी (402), which states in one name what 11.22 states by pairing महाविद्ये and, in the Gita Press reading, महाऽविद्ये; विश्वभ्रमणकारिणी (889) and ममताहन्त्री (165), the two sides of 11.31; and कराङ्गुलिनखोत्पन्ननारायणदशाकृतिः (80), Viṣṇu’s avatāras coming from the Goddess, beside the Boar and the Man-Lion of 11.17–18. In the prophecy विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी (11.42) is also a name there in the identical form (336), and दुर्गमा and दुर्गा stand side by side (189, 190), though the Lalitā Sahasranāma does not tell the story of Durgama (11.49–50). The Mothers of 11.13–21 overlap with the eight Mothers honoured in the outer square of the Śrīcakra (see chapter 8), a separate liturgical tradition. These are shared Śākta names and images, not evidence that one text depends on the other; the chapter itself states no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine.

Numbering
Gita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 91) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.
